\hat{G}\rightarrow\hat{G}/\hat{B}
and
\hat{G}\rightarrow\hat{G}/B^{-}
are Zariski locally trivial.

Proof.

Let
\Omega=U^{-}\hat{B}/\hat{B}
and
\Omega_{W}=\bigcup_{w\in W}w\Omega
.
We must prove that

(2.3.4)

\Omega_{W}=\hat{G}/\hat{B}.

The left hand side is open, and both sides are ind-ft ind-schemes over
\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular they are Jacobson,
and their formation commutes with arbitrary base change by Proposition
2.3.1
.
If
x
is a closed point in the complement in
\hat{G}/\hat{B}
,
then it is sent by the Jacobson property to a closed point in
s\in\Spec(\mathbb{Z})
.
Since
\Omega_{W}
surjects to
\Omega_{W,s}
(because
U^{-}(K)
is generated by
U_{-\alpha_{i}}(K)
for any field
K
), if we know the equality (
2.3.4
) over any finite field,
we would get a contradiction.
Now over a field, the decomposition is a general fact for refined Tits systems
[
56
, Proof of Theorem 5.2.3, (16)]
.
Further, the tuple
(\hat{G}_{k},N_{k},\hat{U}_{k},U_{k}^{-},T_{k},\Delta)
is a refined Tits system over any field
k
by
[
80
, §3.16]
.
∎

By (
2.3.4
) we have an open cover

(2.3.5)

\hat{G}=\bigcup_{w\in W}w\hat{U}_{J}P_{J}^{-}.

Thus the quotient
\mathbb{X}_{J}
has an open cover by schemes isomorphic to
\hat{U}_{J}
and the map

\hat{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{X}_{J}

is Zariski locally trivial.
We now focus on the Borel case. We want to describe
\hat{G}/B^{-}
as a Proj algebra.
In view of the description of
U^{-}
in the next paragraph, we switch from
\hat{G}/B^{-}
to
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B
.

By
[
65
, p. 58 Cor. 2]
, for each
\lambda,\mu\in\Lambda_{+}
we have a surjection of
U(\mathfrak{g})
-modules

(2.3.6)

L(\lambda)^{\vee}\otimes L(\mu)^{\vee}\rightarrow L(\lambda+\mu)^{\vee}.

It gives a commutative ring structure on the Abelian group

R=\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}.

We set
\mathbb{X}=\Proj^{\Lambda_{+}}(R).

Here the
\Proj
is relative to the
\Lambda_{+}
-grading.
By
[
54
, Def. 1.12]
, we have

\mathbb{X}=\big(\Spec(R)\setminus\{x\in\Spec(R)\,;\,x\neq 0\,\text{on}\penalty\ L(\omega_{i})^{\vee},\forall i\in I\}\big)/T

By (
2.3.6
) the sum
\bigoplus_{i\in I}L(\omega_{i})^{\vee}

generates
\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}
.
Hence there is a surjection of
\Lambda_{+}
-graded rings

\bigotimes\limits_{i\in I}S(L(\omega_{i})^{\vee})\twoheadrightarrow\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}

where
S(V)
is the symmetric algebra of an Abelian group
V
.
By
[
67
, Lem. 3.14, 3.16]
, we get a closed immersion

(2.3.7)

\mathbb{X}\hookrightarrow\Proj^{\Lambda_{+}}\Big(\bigotimes\limits_{i\in I}S(L(\omega_{i})^{\vee})\Big)=\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})^{\wedge}).

To identify an open cell in
\mathbb{X}
, we follow
[
54
, Prop. 1.18]
.
Let
V(\omega)=U(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{b})}\mathbb{Z}_{\omega}
be the Verma module of highest weight
\omega
.
From
[
80
, §3.1]
, we have

(2.3.8)

\mathbb{Z}[\hat{U}^{-}]=U(\mathfrak{n}^{-})^{\vee}=V(0)^{\vee}.

The surjection
V(\omega)\twoheadrightarrow L(\omega)
yields an injection

(2.3.9)

\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}\subset\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}V(\omega)^{\vee}\cong\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}\mathbb{Z}[\hat{U}^{-}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{-\omega}\subset\mathbb{Z}[\hat{B}^{-}],

The left hand side a subring of the right hand side.
Hence the scheme
\mathbb{X}
is integral.
By inverting
v_{\omega}^{\vee}
, we obtain an isomorphism of algebras

(2.3.10)

\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}[(v_{\omega}^{\vee})^{-1}]\cong\mathbb{Z}[\hat{U}^{-}]

We deduce that

(2.3.11)

\hat{U}\cong\mathbb{X}\setminus\{v_{i}^{\vee}=0\}_{i\in I}.

Since
N_{G}(T)
acts on
\mathbb{X}
, we deduce that
\bigcup_{w\in W}w\hat{U}
is open in
\mathbb{X}
.
Hence

(2.3.12)

\mathbb{X}=\bigcup_{w\in W}w\hat{U},

because one has equality over
k
-points for any field
k
by
[
54
, Thm. 1.23]
.
On the other hand, we have a map

(2.3.13)

\phi:\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})^{\wedge})

that sends
g
to the tuple
(g\cdot v_{i})_{i\in I}
.

Lemma 2.3.5
.

The group
B
is the stabilizer of the lines
(\mathbb{Z}v_{i})_{i\in I}
.
The map
\phi
yields a monomorphism

\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B\rightarrow\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})^{\wedge}).

Proof.

Let
H
be the stabilizer of the lines
(\mathbb{Z}v_{i})_{i\in I}
.
Since
B
is closed in
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
, it is also closed in
H
.
Recall that
\hat{U}^{-}B
is open in
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
.
Further
B=H\cap(\hat{U}^{-}B)
, because
H\cap\hat{U}^{-}=\{1\}
.
Indeed, this is obvious on
k
-points and the Lie algebras match.
Thus
B
is also open in
H
.
Finally, by the Bruhat decomposition of
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
the
k
-points are the same and
we deduce that
B=H
.
∎

Theorem 2.3.6
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The map
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B\rightarrow\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})^{\wedge})
factors through
\mathbb{X}
.
It yields an isomorphism
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B\cong\mathbb{X}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

Let
J
be a ft subset of
\Delta
.
The scheme
\mathbb{X}_{J}=\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/P_{J}
is formally smooth and separated over
\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

For (a), recall that by (
2.3.11
), (
2.3.5
) and (
2.3.11
), the group
\hat{U}^{-}
sits as a dense open in

\mathbb{X}
and
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B
and there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})^{\wedge})}
\textstyle{\hat{U}^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Now
\hat{U}^{-}
is schematically dense in
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B
and is reduced.
By (
2.3.7
), the schematic image of
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B
factors through
\mathbb{X}
.
Further, the diagram is
W
-equivariant.
Combining (
2.3.5
) and (
2.3.12
), we deduce that it is an isomorphism.
Now, we prove (b).
Since
\hat{U}_{J}
is formally smooth, because formal smoothness is Zariski local by
[
86
, Tag. 0D0F]
and
(
2.3.12
), we deduce that
\mathbb{X}_{J}
is formally smooth.
For separatedness, the case
P=B
follows from (a) and (
2.3.7
).
The general case follows by
[
86
, Tag. 09MQ]
because
the map
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B\rightarrow\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/P_{J}
is projective and surjective.
∎

We now move to
\mathbb{C}
. We will need it for Proposition
4.2.3
.
By
[
54
, Thm. 1.23]
, the quotient
\mathbb{X}_{\mathbb{C}}
coincides with the one in
[
55
, §4]
.
For each
w\in W
, we consider the
\hat{B}_{\mathbb{C}}^{-}
-orbit
\mathbb{O}^{w}=\hat{B}_{\mathbb{C}}^{-}\cdot w
in
\mathbb{X}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
By
[
55
, Lem. 4.5.7, Cor. 4.5.8]
this orbit is affine locally closed of codimension
\ell(w)
.
Here, the codimension is defined in terms of tangent spaces as both schemes are formally smooth.
We obtain a stratification
\mathbb{X}_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W}\mathbb{O}^{w}.

By
[
55
, Prop. 4.5.11]
, the closure of
\mathbb{O}^{w}
in
\mathbb{X}_{\mathbb{C}}
is

\overline{\mathbb{O}^{w}}=\bigsqcup_{w\leq v}\mathbb{O}^{v}.

2.4.
The structure of the ind-group
U

2.4.0.
The comparison of
U
and
\hat{U}

We first recall some facts on
\hat{G}/\hat{B}
.
Recall that
\hat{G}
is simply connected and that
\hat{G}/\hat{B}
is a colimit of Schubert varieties
S_{w}
, see (
2.3.3
).
On each
S_{w}
there is a
\hat{G}
-equivariant line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{S_{w}}(\omega)

for
\omega\in\Lambda_{+}
. By
[
66
, p. 253 Prop. 24]
, we have

H^{0}(S_{w},\mathcal{O}_{S_{w}}(\omega))=L_{w}(\omega)^{\vee}=(U(\mathfrak{n})\cdot w\cdot v_{\omega})^{\vee},\quad H^{>0}(S_{w},\mathcal{O}_{S_{w}}(\omega))=\{0\}.

Note that
\mathcal{O}_{S_{w}}(\omega)
corresponds to
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{w}(-\omega)
in loc. cit.
Set

(2.4.1)

\displaystyle R_{w}=\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L_{w}(\omega)^{\vee}.

The product is given
by the surjections deduced from (
2.3.6
) as in
[
54
, Cor. 1.14]

L_{w}(\omega)^{\vee}\otimes L_{w}(\gamma)^{\vee}\rightarrow L_{w}(\omega+\gamma)^{\vee},\quad\omega,\gamma\in\Lambda_{+}

If
\omega\in\Lambda_{++}
there is a closed immersion
S_{w}\subset\mathbb{P}(L_{w}(\omega))
by
[
65
, p. 58 Cor. 1]
.
Thus, as in
[
54
, Cor. 1.16]
, we have

S_{w}=\Proj^{\Lambda_{+}}(R_{w}).

We consider the topological ring

\widehat{R}=\lim_{w\in W}R_{w}.

We have
\hat{G}/\hat{B}=\TProj(\widehat{R})
, where we define the topological Proj to be

(2.4.2)

\TProj(\widehat{R})=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{w\in W}(\Proj(R_{w})).

Since
\lim_{w}L_{w}(\omega)^{\vee}=L(\omega)^{*}
, it is convenient to write

\widehat{R}=\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}}L(\omega)^{*}.

Recall the groups
U
and
U^{-}
introduced in §
2.3.2
.
We want to identify
U^{-}
inside
\hat{G}/\hat{B}
and compare it with
\hat{U}^{-}
.
There is an injection of rings

\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda^{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}\rightarrow\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}}L(\omega)^{*}

such that for every
w\in W
, the composite with the projection

\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda^{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}\rightarrow\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}}L(\omega)^{*}\rightarrow\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L_{w}(\omega)^{\vee}

is a surjection of graded rings.
Using Theorem
2.3.6
and
[
67
, Lem. 3.14]
, we get an ind-closed morphism

(2.4.3)

\hat{G}/\hat{B}\rightarrow\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B.

and by considering the algebras, a Cartesian diagram whose horizontal maps are closed immersions

(2.4.4)

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.88368pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.05557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 44.82813pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.82813pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i\in 73.84334pt\raise-23.99109pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 41.88368pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.88368pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})^{\wedge})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

In (
2.5.1
) we will prove that
G/B\cong\hat{G}/\hat{B}
, for
G
the minimal group over
\mathbb{Z}
.
Then the morphism (
2.4.3
) will be obtained through an embedding of
G
in
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
.

Proposition 2.4.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

We have an isomorphism

U^{-}\cong\hat{U}^{-}\times_{\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}/B}\hat{G}/\hat{B}.

\mathrm{(b)}

The morphism
U^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-}
is ind-closed and is a morphism of group ind-schemes.

\mathrm{(c)}

We have
\Lie(U^{-}_{k})\cong\Lie(U^{-})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}k\cong\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{-}
as
k
-Lie algebras for any ring
k
.
In particular, the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
e^{*}\Omega^{1}_{U^{-}/\mathbb{Z}}
is dual to a projective
\mathbb{Z}
-module.

Proof.

Let first prove (a) and (b).
By (
2.3.10
), we have

\mathbb{Z}[\hat{U}^{-}]=\bigoplus_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}L(\omega)^{\vee}[(v_{\omega}^{\vee})^{-1}].

Thus, by Theorem
2.3.6
and (
2.4.2
), it is enough to check that

\mathbb{Z}[U^{-}]=\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}}L(\omega)^{*}[(v_{\omega}^{\vee})^{-1}].

This is equivalent to proving that

(2.4.5)

U^{-}=(\hat{G}/\hat{B})\setminus\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{+}}\{v_{\omega}^{\vee}=0\}.

One inclusion is clear. Equality of opens can be checked over
k
-points for any field
k
and this follows from the beginning of the proof of
[
44
, Cor. 4.3]
.
Finally, the morphism is a morphism of group ind-schemes, because the map

U^{-}\rightarrow\prod\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{+}}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega)^{\wedge})

given by
g\mapsto(g\cdot v_{\omega})
is equivariant by left action by
U^{-}
and it factors through
\hat{U}^{-}
.
Now, we prove (c).
From (a), (b) and (
2.4.4
) and since the formation of this objects commute with base change,
we deduce that
U_{k}^{-}
identifies schematically
with

\{g\in\hat{U}_{k}^{-}\,;\,g\cdot L(\omega)_{k}\subset L(\omega)_{k}\,,\,\forall\omega\in\Lambda_{+}\}.

Since
\Lie(U_{k}^{-})
is a colimit of finite dimensional
T
-stable spaces, it is contained in
the set of
T
-finite vectors of
\Lie(\hat{U}_{k}^{-})=\hat{\mathfrak{n}}_{k}^{-}
by (
2.2.6
), and the later is

\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{-}
.
For the equality, note that
(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{k}
stabilizes
L(\omega)_{k}
for any
\omega\in\Lambda
and
any
\alpha\in R^{-}
.
The last claim follows from Proposition
2.1.16
.
∎

For every
w\in W
we define the group schemes

\hat{U}_{w}^{-}=\hat{U}^{-}\cap w(\hat{U}^{-}),\quad\hat{U}^{-,w}=\hat{U}^{-}\cap w(\hat{U})

and the group ind-schemes

U_{w}^{-}=U^{-}\cap\hat{U}_{w}^{-},\quad U^{-,w}=U^{-}\cap\hat{U}^{-,w}.

Proposition 2.4.2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

We have
U^{-,w}=\hat{U}^{-,w}
.
It is a smooth affine group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The multiplication yields an isomorphism

U_{w}^{-}\times U^{-,w}\cong U^{-}

of ind-schemes over
\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Using the decomposition (
2.2.6
) we deduce that the multiplication gives an isomorphism of schemes

(2.4.6)

\hat{U}_{w}^{-}\times\hat{U}^{-,w}\cong\hat{U}^{-}.

Similarly, we get an isomorphism

\prod\limits_{\alpha\in R^{-}\cap w(R^{+})}U_{\alpha}\cong\hat{U}^{-,w},

where
R^{-}\cap wR^{+}
is a finite set of real roots.
Consequently, we have
U^{-,w}\cong\hat{U}^{-,w}
and
U^{-,w}
is a smooth affine group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.
Intersecting (
2.4.6
) with
U^{-}
yields the isomorphism

U_{w}^{-}\times U^{-,w}\cong U^{-}
.
∎

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
the set
\Psi_{n}=\{\alpha\in R^{-}\,;\,\hgt(\alpha)\leq-n\}\cup\{R^{-}_{\textrm{im}}\}

is a closed set of negative roots, because
R^{-}_{\textrm{im}}
is closed in
R^{-}
by

[
52
, Prop. 5.2, Ex. 5.16]
, see also
[
64
, Lem. 3.6]
for details.
Consequently, by
[
80
, §3.1]
, one can define the pro-unipotent group

\hat{U}^{-}_{(n)}=\hat{U}^{-}_{\Psi_{n}}
.
The group of
R
-points of
\hat{U}^{-}_{(n)}
over a ring
R
is the subgroup of
\hat{U}^{-}(R)
formed by products

\prod_{x}[\exp](\lambda_{x}x)
where
\lambda_{x}\in R
and

x
runs over a basis of
\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Psi_{n}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
.
We set
U_{(n)}^{-}=U^{-}\cap\hat{U}^{-}_{(n)}
.

Proposition 2.4.3
.

\mathrm{(a)}

There is an ind-closed embedding
U^{-}\hookrightarrow\hat{U}^{-}
over
\mathbb{Z}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The composed map
U^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-,(n)}
is surjective. It yields an isomorphism of schemes over
\mathbb{Z}

U^{-}/U^{-}_{(n)}\cong\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{U}^{-}_{(n)}.

The map
U^{-}\rightarrow U^{-}/U^{-}_{(n)}
splits as a morphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-ind-schemes.

Proof.

Part (a) is proved in Proposition
2.4.1
. For (b), it is sufficient to note that the quotient
\hat{U}^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-,(n)}
is isomorphic to the
product of
U_{\alpha}
’s for all negative real roots
\alpha
such that
\hgt(\alpha)>n
.
Since
U^{-}
contains all negative real root groups, the composition
U^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{U}^{-}_{(n)}
splits.
∎

We define

\hat{U}^{-,(n)}=\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{U}^{-}_{(n)}
.
We will also use a normal subgroup without splitting.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\hat{V}_{(n)}^{-}\subset\hat{U}^{-}
be the normal subgroup associated as above
with the set of negative roots
\{\alpha\in R^{-}\,;\,\hgt(\alpha)\leq-n\}
.
The quotient
\hat{V}^{-,(n)}=\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{V}_{(n)}^{-}
is a smooth unipotent group.
We set
V_{(n)}^{-}=U^{-}\cap\hat{V}_{(n)}^{-}
.

Lemma 2.4.4
.

The composed map
U^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{V}_{(n)}^{-}
yields an isomorphism

[U^{-}/V_{(n)}^{-}]_{\mathrm{fppf}}\cong\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{V}_{(n)}^{-}.

We sheafify the left hand side for the fppf topology.

Proof.

We first check the surjectivity on
k
-points for any field
k
.
The proof of
[
56
, Cor. 7.3.8]
implies that the group

\hat{V}_{k}^{-,(n)}
is generated by the image of the groups
(U_{-\alpha_{i}})_{k}
for
i\in I
.
To prove the isomorphism above,
note that the schemes
\hat{V}^{-,(n)}
and

X=\prod\limits_{i\in I}U_{-\alpha_{i}}
are smooth over
\mathbb{Z}
, and that
the multiplication gives a map

m:X\rightarrow\hat{V}^{-,(n)}
.
Set

X^{1}=X\bigsqcup X
, and, for each
d>1
, define inductively

X^{d}=X^{1}\times X^{d-1}.

Let
m^{1}:X^{1}\rightarrow\hat{V}^{-,(n)}
be the map given by
m^{1}=(m,\iota\circ m)
, where

\iota
is the inversion, and set

m^{d}=\mu\circ(m^{1}\times m^{d-1}):X^{d}\rightarrow\hat{V}^{-,(n)},

where
\mu
is the multiplication in
\hat{V}^{-,(n)}
.
By the above, the map

\bigsqcup\limits_{d\geq 0}X^{d}\rightarrow\hat{V}^{-,(n)}

is surjective, beacuse it is surjective on
k
-points for any field
k
.
Thus, by
[
24
, Exp. 6B, Prop. 7.6]
, the sheaf
\hat{V}^{-,(n)}
is the fppf-sheafification of the presheaf

R\rightarrow\left\langle U_{-\alpha_{i}}(R)\,;\,i\in I\right\rangle.

Consequently, the morphism
U^{-}\rightarrow\hat{V}^{-,(n)}
is a surjection of fppf sheaves and it induces thus an isomorphism

U^{-}/V_{(n)}^{-}\cong\hat{U}^{-}/\hat{V}_{(n)}^{-}.

∎

2.4.4.
Lie algebras of Kac-Moody groups and formal smoothness

We want to compare
\Lie(\hat{G})
and
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
.
For all
i\in I
, the minimal parabolic
P_{i}\subset\hat{G}
yields a morphism
\SL_{2}\rightarrow\hat{G}
over
\mathbb{Z}
,
hence a morphism

\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\rightarrow\Lie(\hat{G})

of Lie algebras over
\mathbb{Z}
,
and the inclusion
\hat{B}\subset\hat{G}
yields a morphism
\hat{\mathfrak{b}}\rightarrow\Lie(\hat{G})

of Lie algebras over
\mathbb{Z}
.
Further, we have
[e_{i},f_{j}]=0
for
i\neq j
.
Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
be the quotient of the free Lie algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
by the relations (
2.2.1
), completed in the
\mathfrak{n}
direction.
By the above we have a morphism
\kappa:\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\Lie(\hat{G}_{\mathbb{Q}})

of Lie algebras over
\mathbb{Q}
.
We claim that this morphism factors through a morphism

(2.4.7)

\displaystyle\kappa:\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\Lie(\hat{G}_{\mathbb{Q}}).

We must prove the relations (
2.2.2
).
The first relation holds in
\hat{\mathfrak{b}}=\Lie(\hat{B})
.
Since
\Lie(\hat{G}_{\mathbb{Q}})
is an integrable
\mathfrak{sl}_{2,j}
-module
e_{j}
,
f_{j}

act locally nilpotently, hence
[
56
, Lem. 1.3.9]
implies that the second relation in (
2.2.2
) also holds.

Theorem 2.4.5
.

The map
\kappa
restricts to an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-Lie algebras
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}\to\Lie(\hat{G}).

Proof.

The map
\kappa
maps
\hat{\mathfrak{b}}
isomorphically onto
\Lie(\hat{B})
as
\mathbb{Z}
-Lie algebras.
In particular, it is a Lie algebra homomorphism which identifies the Cartan algebras.
For weight reasons, it maps

\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{-}
into
\Lie(U^{-}_{\mathbb{Q}})
.
By Proposition
2.4.1
the embedding

\theta:\Lie(U^{-})\rightarrow\Lie(\hat{U}^{-})=\hat{\mathfrak{n}}^{-}

factors through a
\mathbb{Z}
-Lie
algebra isomorphism
\Lie(U^{-})\cong\mathfrak{n}^{-}
.
Hence, the composed map
\theta\circ\kappa
restricts to a
\mathbb{Q}
-Lie algebra homomorphism

\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{-}\to\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{-}
such that
f_{i}\mapsto f_{i}
.
Thus it restricts to the identity of
\mathfrak{n}^{-}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and a fortiori of
\mathfrak{n}^{-}
and
\kappa
restricts
to a
\mathbb{Z}
-Lie algebra isomorphism
\mathfrak{n}\to\Lie(U^{-})
, from which
we deduce that
\kappa
is a Lie algebra isomorphism
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\Lie(\hat{G})
.
∎

Theorem 2.4.6
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero. Then
\hat{G}_{k}
and
\hat{G}_{k}/\hat{B}_{k}
are formally smooth over
k
.
If
G
is affine over any algebraically closed field
k
, then
G
is formally smooth.

Proof.

The map
\hat{G}_{k}\rightarrow\hat{G}_{k}/\hat{B}_{k}
is Zariski locally trivial and formally smooth as
\hat{B}_{k}
is.
Thus it is enough to prove that
\hat{G}_{k}/\hat{B}_{k}
is formally smooth.
By Lemma
2.3.3
, it is covered by translates of
U_{k}^{-}
, which is formally smooth by Theorem
2.1.14
.
For the affine case, in the formal case, from the description of
[
88
, App. 2]
we have that
\hat{G}=\mathbb{G}_{\hat{K}}
for a quasi-split
reductive group scheme
\mathbb{G}
over
k(\!(t)\!)
. From that, we get that
\hat{G}/\hat{B}=G/B
is formally smooth and thus
U^{-}\subset\hat{G}/\hat{B}

also. By considering
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
, we have the corresponding assertion for
U
and we get that
G
is also formally smooth, as it is
covered by translates of the open cell.
∎

Remark 2.4.7
.

We expect that the same statement should hold over
\mathbb{Z}
, or already over any field
k
.
This would imply immediately all the geometric results of §6.3.

2.5.
The Minimal Kac-Moody group

In this section we give a construction of minimal KM groups, as group ind-schemes of ind-ft over
\mathbb{Z}
.
Over
\mathbb{Z}
, the group functor
E_{\mathcal{D}}
introduced by Tits
[
87
, §3.6]
is known to be the wrong functor because,
already in the ft case, it does not recover the Chevalley group schemes, see
[
88
, §1.2]
.
Only the points of
E_{\mathcal{D}}
over a field
k
are well-behaved.
Over
\mathbb{C}
, there is a construction due to Kumar
[
56
, §7]
.
Our construction is independent and gives the same answer over
\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}
be a semisimple simply connected KM root datum.

2.5.0.
The definition of the minimal Kac-Moody group

Let

(2.5.1)

\displaystyle\Omega=U^{-}\times T\times U\subset\hat{\Omega}=U^{-}\times T\times\hat{U}.

By Proposition
2.4.1
applied to
U
, the subfunctor
\Omega
is ind-closed in
\hat{\Omega}
and
is representable by an ind-scheme of ind-ft over
\mathbb{Z}
. Let
B=U\rtimes T
.
Let
G
the subfunctor of
\hat{G}
defined as the Zariski sheafification of the presheaf

(2.5.2)

R\mapsto\bigcup_{w\in W}w\Omega(R).

More generally, for an arbitrary commutative ring
k
, we can form the same way the subfunctor
G_{k}\subset\hat{G}_{k}
, by considering translates of
U_{k}\times T_{k}\times U^{-}_{k}
and sheafifying for Zariski topology.

Proposition 2.5.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

We have a canonical isomorphism
G\times_{\Spec(\mathbb{Z})}\Spec(k)\cong G_{k}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

\hat{\Omega}\cap w\Omega\subset\Omega
as ind-schemes for each
w\in W
.

\mathrm{(c)}

The map
G\rightarrow\hat{G}
is ind-closed. The functor
G
is representable by an ind-affine ind-scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.

\mathrm{(d)}

\hat{B}\cap G=B
and
\hat{U}\cap G=U
.

\mathrm{(e)}

The composed map

G\rightarrow\hat{G}\rightarrow\hat{G}/\hat{B}

factors through an isomorphism
G/B\cong\hat{G}/\hat{B}.

\mathrm{(f)}

The morphism
G\rightarrow G/B
is Zariski locally trivial.
The ind-scheme
G
is ind-normal of ind-ft.

Proof.

(a) We have a canonical map
G\times_{\Spec(\mathbb{Z})}\Spec(k)\rightarrow G_{k}
, that already exists at the level of presheaves. It is sufficient to
prove the isomorphism Zariski locally, thus we are reduced to the claim for the open cell where it follows from the fact that attractors
commute with base change and Proposition
2.3.1
(b).

For (b), let
x\in(\hat{\Omega}\cap w\Omega)(R)
for a ring
R
. We have

(2.5.3)

vt\hat{u}=wv_{1}t_{1}u_{1},\quad\hat{u}\in\hat{U}(R),\quad u_{1}\in U(R),\quad t,t_{1}\in T(R),\quad v,v_{1}\in U^{-}(R).

Thus
\hat{u}=wv^{\prime}t^{\prime}u^{\prime}
.
Now we make this element act on the highest vector
v_{\omega}
in
L(\omega)
for various
\omega
.
Since
\hat{U}\cdot v_{\omega}=v_{\omega}
by Lemma
2.3.5
, we get
w=t^{\prime}=v^{\prime}=1
and
\hat{u}=u^{\prime}
.
For (c), to prove that
G\rightarrow\hat{G}
is ind-closed, using Proposition
2.3.4
,
it is enough to prove that the base change to
w\hat{\Omega}
is ind-closed and the claim follows from (b) and Proposition
2.4.1
.
For (d), note that
\hat{B}\cap G=B
follows from (a).
Part (e) is clear as
\hat{G}/\hat{B}
is covered by
w
-translates of
U^{-}
.
Finally we prove (f). By (e), the functor
G/B
is ind-projective over
\mathbb{Z}
and by (a), the map
G\rightarrow G/B
is Zariski locally trivial.
Thus
G
is of ind-ft as
B
is. By (e), Proposition
2.3.1
and Lemma
2.3.3
,
the functors
B
and
G/B
are ind-normal. Thus
G
is also ind-normal.
∎

Proposition 2.5.2
.

The functor
G
is an ind-fp-affine group ind-scheme.
The obvious morphism
G\rightarrow\hat{G}
is a morphism of group ind-schemes over
\mathbb{Z}
and
\Lie(G)=\mathfrak{g}
.

Proof.

Consider the composed map

G\times G\rightarrow\hat{G}\times\hat{G}\to\hat{G}

given by the multiplication in
\hat{G}
.
We must prove that this map factors schematically through
G
.
Since
G
is ind-closed in
\hat{G}
and the source is geometrically reduced by Proposition
2.5.1
and
[
86
, Tag. 06DG]
,
it is enough to prove that it factors on
k
-points for any field
k
by
[
86
, Tag. 0356]
.
Let
G_{1}(k)\subset\hat{G}(k)
be the subgroup generated by
T(k)
by
U_{\alpha}(k)
for all
\alpha\in R_{{\mathrm{re}}}
.
Let
U_{1}(k)
and
U_{1}^{-}(k)
be
the subgroups generated by
U_{\alpha}(k)
for positive real roots and negative real roots respectively.
By §
2.3.2
and (
2.5.2
), we have

U(k)=U_{1}(k),\quad U^{-}(k)=U_{1}^{-}(k),\quad G(k)\subset G_{1}(k).

By
[
80
, Prop. 3.13]
, the group
G_{1}(k)
is the Tits minimal group.
By
[
79
, §1.5.4, §8.4.1]
the tuple

(G_{1}(k)\,,\,N(k)\,,\,U_{1}(k)\,,\,U_{1}^{-}(k)\,,\,T(k)\,,\,S)

where
S\subset W
is the set of simple reflexions is a refined Tits system.
Thus by
[
56
, Thm. 5.2.3]
we have

(2.5.4)

G_{1}(k)\subset\bigcup_{n\in N(k)}nU^{-}(k)U(k)=G(k).

The case of the inversion is analog and easier since we already know that
G(k)
is a group.
The description of the Lie algebra follows from the description of the open cell in Proposition
2.4.1
and Theorem
2.4.5
.
∎

2.5.2.
The set of
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
-points of the minimal KM group

Instead of an integral model of a minimal KM group, the references
[
21
]
or
[
40
]
use an abstract
group
G_{x}
which is defined by some generators.
We must compare the group
G_{x}
with
G(\widehat{\mathcal{O}})
, for
G
the minimal group ind-scheme constructed above.
Here it is important to
work with
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
rather than
\mathcal{O}
. The corresponding assertion for
\mathcal{O}
is probably wrong.
By
[
40
, §3.4]
, we have

G_{x}=(\hat{U}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\cap G(\hat{K}))U^{-}_{0}N(\hat{\mathcal{O}}),

with
N
the normalizer of
T
and
U^{-}_{0}
the group generated by
U_{\alpha}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
for
\alpha\in R_{{\mathrm{re}}}^{-}
.
The following proposition is an illustration that our minimal group
G
behaves well.

Proposition 2.5.3
.

We have
G_{x}=\langle U_{\pm\alpha_{i}}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}),T(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\,;\,i\in I\rangle=G(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
.

Proof.

By
[
48
, Prop. 3.1]
, we have
G_{x}=\langle U_{\pm\alpha_{i}}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}),T(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\,;\,i\in I\rangle
and
G_{x}\subset G(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
.
Conversely, let
g\in G(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
. Replacing
g
by
hg
with
h\in G(k)
a product by of elements of the middle group by (
2.5.4
),
we can assume that
g=1
modulo
s
. As
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
is a local ring, we have
g\in(UTU^{-})(\hat{\mathcal{O}})
.
Since
U(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\subset\hat{U}(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\cap G(\hat{K})
, we deduce that
G(\hat{\mathcal{O}})\subset G_{x}
.
∎

2.6.
Basic affine spaces of Kac-Moody groups

2.6.0.
The positive basic affine space

We assume that
G
is simply connected.
Let the Schubert variety
S_{w}
be as in §
2.3.0
.
We define as in (
2.3.7
) the closed embeddings of ind-ft-schemes
\mathbb{Z}
:

(2.6.1)

S_{w}\hookrightarrow G/B\hookrightarrow\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})).

There is a
T
-torsor over the right hand side given by

(2.6.2)

\prod\limits_{i\in I}(L(\omega_{i})\setminus\{0\})\rightarrow\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i})).

We consider the map

G\rightarrow\prod\limits_{i\in I}L(\omega_{i}),\quad g\mapsto(g\cdot v_{i})_{i\in I},

It is
U
-equivariant and factors through the left hand side of (
2.6.2
).
Since both functors are
T
-torsors over
G/B
, we get the following isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-ind-schemes of ind-ft

(2.6.3)

G/U\cong G/B\times_{\prod\limits_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}(L(\omega_{i}))}\prod\limits_{i\in I}(L(\omega_{i})\setminus\{0\}).

Proposition 2.6.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The obvious map
G/U\rightarrow\hat{G}/\hat{U}
is an isomorphism.

\mathrm{(b)}

The quotients
G/U
,
G/U^{-}
and
\hat{G}/\hat{U}
are representable by quasi-compact locally closed in ind-affine ind-ft ind-schemes
over
\mathbb{Z}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

If
G
is untwisted affine, then
G/U
is a presheaf quotient: for any ring
R
we have

G(R)/U(R)=(G/U)(R).

Proof.

To prove (a), note that the map
G/U\rightarrow\hat{G}/\hat{U}
is an isomorphism,
because both sides are
T
-torsors over
G/B\cong\hat{G}/\hat{B}
by Proposition
2.5.1
.
Now, by (a), it is enough to prove (b) for
G/U
, the other case is similar.
Then, the assertion follows from (
2.6.3
).
Finally, we prove (c). Let
R
be a ring, and
x\in(G/U)(R)
.
Taking again the notations of Corollary
2.1.19
, let
\bar{x}
be the image of
x\in(G/P)(R)
,
using Theorem
2.1.18
, we find
y\in G(R)
such that

y^{-1}x\in(P/U)(R)=(\mathbb{G}_{f}/U_{f})(R),

and the latter lifts as
U_{f}
is split unipotent.
∎

Remark 2.6.2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

It would be useful to know whether (c) holds beyond the untwisted affine case.

\mathrm{(b)}

Let
I_{w}
be the ideal of the ring
R_{w}
in (
2.4.1
) given by
I_{w}=\bigoplus\limits_{\omega\in\Lambda_{++}}L_{w}(\omega)^{\vee}
.
Following
[
67
, (4.6)]
, we expect that

G/U\times_{G/B}S_{w}\cong\Spec(R_{w})\setminus V(I_{w}).

This should be useful if one to consider the basic affine space
\overline{G/U}
in the Kac-Moody case.

We need the variant for the Kashiwara flag scheme.

Proposition 2.6.3
.

Assume that
G
is untwisted affine. The quotient
\hat{G}/U^{-}
is a presheaf quotient, i.e., for any algebra
R
we have

\hat{G}(R)/U^{-}(R)=(\hat{G}/U^{-})(R).

Proof.

The proof is the same proof as for Proposition
2.6.1
(c), replacing
P
by
P^{-}
and using Proposition
2.1.20
.
∎

3.
Affine Grassmannians of Kac-Moody groups

3.1.
Relative representability

3.1.0.
Definition of the affine Grassmannian

Let
G
be an ind-affine group ind-scheme over the commutative ring
k
.
Set
S=\Spec(k)
.
The affine Grasmmannian of
G
is the
S
-étale sheaf

(3.1.1)

\displaystyle\Gr_{G}=[G_{K}/G_{\mathcal{O}}].

For each
k
-algebra
R
the groupoid
\Gr_{G}(R)
consists of étale
G
-torsors over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
,
with a trivialization over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
, that are étale locally trivial over
\Spec(R)
.
The sheaf
\Gr_{G}
is not representable in general.
We also need the Laurent series version
\Gr_{G}^{\mathrm{f}}

where we replace
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
by
\Spec(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
, and
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
by
\Spec(R(\!(t)\!))
, i.e., we set

\Gr_{G}^{\mathrm{f}}=[G_{\hat{K}}/G_{\widehat{O}}].

Proposition 3.1.1
.

Let
G
be an ind-affine group ind-scheme over
k
.
Let
H\subset G
be a closed subgroup.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
G/H
is either ind-quasi-affine (resp. ind-strongly quasi-affine) or
open
G
-equivariantly in an ind-affine ind-scheme
Z
,
then the map
\Gr_{H}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is locally closed (resp. fp locally closed).

\mathrm{(b)}

If
G/H
is ind-affine or ind-fp-affine, then this map is closed or fp closed respectively.

Moreover, we have the same conclusion with
\Gr_{G}
replaced with
\Gr_{G}^{\mathrm{f}}
.

Remark 3.1.2
.

Recall that a
k
-scheme is quasi-affine (resp. strongly quasi-affine) if it is quasi-compact open of an affine scheme (resp. finitely presented affine scheme).
An open
V
in an affine scheme is not necessarily quasi-compact, but any quasi-compact open of
V
is quasi-affine.

Proof.

This lemma is well-known for algebraic groups
[
90
, Prop. 1.2.6]
. We start with the case of Laurent polynomials.
Let
(\mathcal{E},\beta)
be a
k
-point of
\Gr_{G}
represented by a morphism
S\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
.
We must prove that the morphism
\mathcal{F}\to S
with

\mathcal{F}=S\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{H}

is locally closed.
Let
\pi:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
be the structural morphism.
The trivialization
\beta
is given by a section of
\pi
over
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
.
Consider the étale quotient

\bar{\pi}:[\mathcal{E}/H]\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}.

Note that
[\mathcal{E}/H]
is étale locally isomorphic to
G/H
.
If
G/H
is ind-quasi-affine (resp. ind-strongly quasi-affine, ind-affine, ind-fp-affine), by
[
45
, Lem. 3.12]
, effectivity of descent for quasi-affine schemes (resp. affine schemes) and descent of finite presentation(
[
86
, Tag. 0245, 0247, 041V]
),
\mathcal{E}_{Z}
is represented by an ind-quasi-affine scheme (resp. ind-strongly quasi-affine, ind-affine, ind-fp-affine).

If
G/H
is open
G
-equivariantly in an ind-affine ind-scheme
Z
, we form the twisted quotient
\mathcal{E}_{Z}=\mathcal{E}\times^{G}Z
, which is ind-affine. Consequently
[\mathcal{E}/H]
is open in
\mathcal{E}_{Z}
by descent (
[
86
, Tag. 041V]
).

The section
\beta
yields a section

\bar{\beta}

of
\bar{\pi}
over
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
.
An
H
-reduction of
\mathcal{E}
is the same as a section of
\bar{\pi}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
.
Consider the presheaf
\mathcal{F}
over
S
that assigns to each
k\rightarrow R
the set of sections

\beta^{\prime}

of
\bar{\pi}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
such that
\beta^{\prime}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}=\bar{\beta}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}
.

Fix a filtered presentation
[\mathcal{E}/H]\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}V_{a}
with
V_{a}
open in an affine (resp. quasi-affine, resp. strongly quasi-affine, resp. affine, resp. fp affine) over
S
.
The section
\bar{\beta}
factors through some
V_{a}
and further through some qc subset
V_{aa^{\prime}}
of
V_{a}

which is quasi-affine by the remark above. In (b) we have
V_{a}=V_{aa^{\prime}}
.
We consider the subpresheaf
\mathcal{F}_{a}\subset\mathcal{F}

that consists of sections
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(R)
such that
\beta^{\prime}
factors through
V_{aa^{\prime}}
for a
k
-algebra
R
.
We claim that
\mathcal{F}_{a}=\mathcal{F}.

To prove this, let
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(R)
for a
k
-algebra
R
.
It is a section of
\bar{\pi}
.
We must prove that it factors through
V_{a}
.
Over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
, we have
\beta^{\prime}_{R}=\bar{\beta}_{R}
.
Since
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
is schematically dense in
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
and
[\mathcal{E}/H]
is ind-separated,
we have
\beta^{\prime}=\bar{\beta}
. The claim follows.
To conclude, we must prove that
\mathcal{F}_{a}
is locally closed in
S
.
This follows from the
following lemma.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Let
p:V\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
be a quasi-affine (resp. strongly quasi-affine, resp. affine, fp-affine) scheme,
with
\sigma
a section of
p
over
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
.
Then the presheaf over
S
that assigns to each morphism
k\rightarrow R
the set of sections
\sigma^{\prime}
of
p_{R}
such that

\sigma^{\prime}|_{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}=\sigma|_{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}
is represented by a locally closed
(resp. fp locally closed, closed, fp closed) subscheme of
S
.

Proof.

Assume first that
V
is affine.
We can suppose that
V\subset\mathbb{A}_{\mathcal{O}}^{(I)}
is closed, with the set
I
finite if
V
is fp.
We have

\sigma=(\sigma_{i}(s))_{i\in I}
with

\sigma_{i}(s)=\sum\sigma_{ij}s^{j}
in
K.

The presheaf considered is then defined by the closed subscheme of
S
defined by

\sigma_{ij}=0
for all
i\in I
and
j<0.

For the quasi-affine (resp. strongly quasi-affine) case, let
W
be affine (resp. fp-affine) such that
V
is qc open in
W
. The affine case gives a closed subscheme

\Spec(R)\subset S
and a tautological map
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}\rightarrow W\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\mathbb{A}_{R}^{1}
,
whose restriction to
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
is
\sigma|_{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}
.
A base change along the 0 section

\Spec(R)\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{R}^{1}

gives a section

\tau:\Spec(R)\rightarrow W\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\Spec(R).

The presheaf is represented by the quasi-compact open subscheme
\tau^{-1}(V\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\Spec(R))
of
\Spec(R)
.
∎

Finally for the Laurent series case, the proof goes through mutatis mutandis, replacing
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
by
\Spec(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
and
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
by
\Spec(R(\!(t)\!))
.
∎

Lemma 3.1.4
.

If an immersion of prestacks
\mathcal{Y}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
factors through a monomorphism of prestacks
\mathcal{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
,
then the map
\mathcal{Y}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is an immersion. If the map
\mathcal{Y}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is fp then the map
\mathcal{Y}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is also fp.

Proof.

Let
S\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
with
S=\Spec(k)
.
We must prove that the map
\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}S\rightarrow S
is locally closed (resp. locally closed and fp).
By assumption, the map
\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}S\rightarrow S
obtained through
S\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is an immersion
(resp. fp immersion).
But as
\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is a monomorphism, we get have
\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}S\cong\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}S
.
∎

Proposition 3.1.5
.

Let
G
be an ind-affine group ind-scheme over
k
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The functor
\Gr_{G}
has a closed diagonal.

\mathrm{(b)}

If further
G
is ind-fp-affine then
\Gr_{G}
has an fp closed diagonal.

Proof.

The map

{\Delta}:G_{K}\times G_{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow G_{K}\times G_{K}

such that

(g,h)\rightarrow(g,gh)
is closed (resp. fp closed),
being the composition of the multiplication map and the base change of the inclusion
G_{\mathcal{O}}\hookrightarrow G_{K}
which is closed (resp. fp closed).
The map
[{\Delta}/(G_{\mathcal{O}})^{2}]
is the diagonal of
\Gr_{G}
.
It is closed (resp. fp closed) by Lemma
2.1.4
.
∎

3.1.5.
Modular description of the affine Grassmannian

Let
G
be an ind-affine group ind-scheme over a ring
k
.
Let
\Gr^{\prime}_{G}
be the functor from
k
-algebras to sets taking
R
to
the set of isomorphism classes of
G
-torsors on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
with a trivialization over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
.
By
[
22
, Thm. 3.4, 3.6]
,
if
G
is a quasi-split reductive group scheme over
k
,
then the functor
\Gr^{\prime}_{G}

is isomorphic to the presheaf quotient
G_{K}/G_{\mathcal{O}}
, i.e.,
there are bijections

(3.1.2)

\Gr^{\prime}_{G}(R)\cong\Gr_{G}(R)\cong G(K_{R})/G(\mathcal{O}_{R}).

In particular, a
G
-torsor over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
is trivial.
For a KM group
G
the functors
\Gr^{\prime}_{G}
and
\Gr_{G}
may differ.
Nevertheless, we have the following result in the untwisted affine case.

Theorem 3.1.6
.

Let
G
be a untwisted affine minimal KM group of affine type over a ring
k
.
Let
R
be a
k
-algebra.
Any étale
G
-torsor over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
is trivial.
In particular, we have
\Gr_{G}^{\prime}\cong\Gr_{G}
and
\Gr_{G}
is a presheaf quotient.

Proof.

As in Definition
2.1.17
, let
G=\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,K}}\rtimes H
where
\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,K}}
is a central extension of
\mathbb{G}_{f,K}

by a split
k
-torus
Z
, the group
\mathbb{G}_{f}
split reductive over
k
, and
H
is a split
k
-torus.
Let
E
be a
G
-torsor over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}=\Spec(R[s])
trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}=\Spec(R[s,s^{-1}])
.
We want to prove that
E
is trivial.
Considering the map
p:G\rightarrow H
, the torus case, and the fact that the map
p
splits,
we deduce that the torsor
E
comes from a

\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,K}}
-torsor over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
which is trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
.
Pushing
E
along the central extension

\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,K}}\to\mathbb{G}_{f,K}
, we get a
\mathbb{G}_{f,K}
-torsor over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
which is trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
.
This defines an element in
\Gr_{\mathbb{G}_{f}}(R[t,t^{-1}])
.
Thus, using (
3.1.2
) for the group
\mathbb{G}_{f}
and the ring
R[t,t^{-1}]
, we deduce that
this
\mathbb{G}_{f,K}
-torsor is trivial.
Hence
E
comes from a
Z
-torsor over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
which over
R[s,s^{-1}]
comes from a section of
\mathbb{G}_{f,K}(R[s,s^{-1}])
.
The composed map

Z\rightarrow\widetilde{G_{f,K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{f,K}

factors through the isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,\mathcal{O}}}\cong\mathbb{G}_{f,\mathcal{O}}\times Z.

Hence, the class of
E
in
H^{1}(R[s,s^{-1}],Z)
has
trivial image in
H^{1}(R[s,s^{-1}],\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,K}})
.
Thus it comes from an element

x\in(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,K}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,\mathcal{O}}})(K_{R})=(\Gr_{\mathbb{G}_{f}})(R[s,s^{-1}]).

Using again (
3.1.2
) we get that
x
lifts as a point in
\mathbb{G}_{f,K}(R[s,s^{-1}])
.
Hence
x
has a trivial image in
H^{1}(K_{R},\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_{f,\mathcal{O}}})
that contains
H^{1}(K_{R},Z)
as a direct
summand, thus we end up with an element in
\Gr_{Z}(R)
and apply one more time (
3.1.2
) to get that
E
is trivial.
∎

3.2.
Semi-infinite orbits

3.2.0.
The Iwasawa decomposition

In this section
G
is a simply connected minimal KM group over
\mathbb{Z}
.
The next property is fundamental for the Iwasawa decomposition. The key subtlety is that we have a global curve.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

Let
R
be a principal ring.
Let
G
be a minimal KM group over
\mathbb{Z}
, and
B\subset G
a Borel subgroup.
Any map
x:\Spec(R)\to G/B
lifts to a map
\Spec(R)\rightarrow G
.

Proof.

We write
G/B=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{w\in W}(S_{w})
with
S_{w}=\overline{BwB}/B
.
The map
x
factors through some
S_{w}
.
We prove by induction on
\ell(w)
that
x
lifts to a map
\Spec(R)\to G
.
If
w=1
, it is clear. Assume that
\ell(w)\geq 1
. We consider a reduced decomposition

w=s_{i_{1}}s_{i_{2}}\dots s_{i_{n}}
.
We consider the associated Demazure scheme

D(\underline{w})=E(\underline{w})/B

with

E(\underline{w})=P_{i_{1}}\times^{B}\dots\times^{B}P_{i_{n}}.

The map

m:D(\underline{w})\rightarrow S_{w}

is proper, surjective, and birational over the open subset

BwB/B
of
S_{w}
by
[
66
, Lem. 29]
.
Note that this is proved for the formal version, but, since
P/B\cong\hat{P}/\hat{B}
, the formal version and the minimal versions of

D(\underline{w})
are isomorphic.
By the valuative criterion of properness, the map
x
lifts uniquely to a map

\Spec(R)\rightarrow D(\underline{w}).

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{E(\underline{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D(\underline{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{\Fl_{\leq w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G/B}

Since
R
is principal, we have
\Pic(R)=0
.
Hence, since
T
is split, we can lift
x
to
a map

\tilde{x}:\Spec(R)\to E(\underline{w})/U.

Note that
E(\underline{w})/U
is the quotient of
P_{\underline{w}}:=P_{i_{1}}\times\dots\times P_{i_{n}}

by
B^{n-1}\times U
acting by

(p_{1},\dots,p_{n})\cdot(b_{1},\dots,b_{n})=(p_{1}b_{1},b_{1}^{-1}p_{2}b_{2},\dots,b^{-1}_{n-1}p_{n}b_{n}).

Let
H
be a closed subgroup of
\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}R_{u}(P_{i})
which is normal in every
P_{i}

and such that
U/H
is split unipotent.
Set

P_{\underline{w}}/H^{n}=P_{i_{1}}/H\times\dots\times P_{i_{n}}/H.

Since
H
is normal, there is a map

\theta:P_{\underline{w}}/H^{n}\rightarrow E(\underline{w})/U

that takes the
H^{n}
-orbit of the tuple
(p_{1},\dots,p_{n})
to its
B^{n-1}\times U
-orbit.
The map
\theta
is thus an
(B^{n-1}\times U)/H^{n}
-torsor.
The group
(B^{n-1}\times U)/H^{n}
is split solvable.
Thus the torsor is trivialisable because
\Pic(R)=0
.
Thus we can assume that the map
x
lifts to a map

\tilde{x}:\Spec(R)\to P_{\underline{w}}/H^{n}.

By Lemma
2.4.4
, we can choose
H=V_{m}:=U\cap\hat{V}_{m}
,
where
\hat{V}_{m}
is
the pro-unipotent subgroup of
\hat{U}
consisting of roots
\alpha\in R^{+}
with

\hgt(\alpha)\geq m
for a big enough integer
m
.
We decompose
\tilde{x}
as a tuple
\tilde{x}=(\tilde{x}_{1},\dots,\tilde{x}_{n})
with

\tilde{x}_{s}:\Spec(R)\to P_{i_{s}}/H
.
We have
x=\tilde{x}_{1}\dots\tilde{x}_{n}
in
(G/B)(R)
.
We abbreviate
P=P_{i_{1}}
.
Consider the first projection to
P/H
.
We have

P\rightarrow P/H\rightarrow P/R_{u}(P).

The composed map is split by a Levi factor.
The second map is a torsor over a split unipotent group,
hence it is trivial over any ring
R
.
Thus we can find
y_{1}\in P(R)
such that

y_{1}\tilde{x}_{1}\in(R_{u}(P)/H)(R)\subset(U/H)(R).

Set
v=s_{i_{2}}s_{i_{3}}\dots s_{i_{n}}
.
We get a tuple

(y_{1}\tilde{x}_{1},\dots,\tilde{x}_{n})\in(U\times P_{\underline{v}})/H^{n}

whose image
y_{1}x
in
G/B
lands in
S_{v}
.
Thus
y_{1}x
lifts to
G(R)
by induction.
Since
y_{1}\in P(R)
, the
R
-point
x
lifts also to
G(R)
.
∎

The following proposition is well-known in the affine case.
It seems to be new in our generality.
Note that a similar statement in
[
39
]
uses group theory.

Proposition 3.2.2
.

Let
G
and
\hat{G}
be the minimal and formal KM groups over a field
k
.
We have

G(K)=B(K)\cdot G(\mathcal{O}),\quad G(K)=B^{-}(K)\cdot G(\mathcal{O}),\quad\hat{G}(K)=\hat{B}(K)\cdot\hat{G}(\mathcal{O}).

Proof.

The rings
K
and
\mathcal{O}
are both principal. Since
G/B\cong\hat{G}/\hat{B}
and
G/B^{-}
are ind-projective,
the valuative criterion of properness for
\Spec(\mathcal{O})
yields

(\hat{G}/\hat{B})(K)=(\hat{G}/\hat{B})(\mathcal{O}),\quad(G/B)(K)=(G/B)(\mathcal{O}),\quad(G/B^{-})(K)=(G/B^{-})(\mathcal{O}).

By Proposition
3.2.1
, we have

G(K)/B(K)=(G/B)(K)=(G/B)(\mathcal{O})=G(\mathcal{O})/B(\mathcal{O}).

The formal case follows because as
\hat{U}
is pro-unipotent and
T
is split

H^{1}(K,\hat{U})=H^{1}(K,T)=H^{1}(\mathcal{O},\hat{U})=H^{1}(\mathcal{O},T)=\{1\}.

∎

3.2.2.
The semi-infinite orbits

Let
G
and
\hat{G}
be the minimal and formal KM groups over
\mathbb{Z}
.
By (
3.1.1
) we can consider the affine Grassmannians
\Gr_{G}
and
\Gr_{\hat{G}}
of

G
and
\hat{G}
and the obvious morphism
\Gr_{G}\to\Gr_{\hat{G}}
.
For any cocharacters
\mu,\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
, there are subfunctors
S_{\mu}
,
T_{\nu}
,
S_{\leq\mu},

T_{\geq\nu}
of
\Gr_{G}
given by

(3.2.1)

\displaystyle S_{\mu}=s^{\mu}\cdot\Gr_{U},\quad T_{\nu}=s^{\nu}\cdot\Gr_{U^{-}},\quad S_{\leq\mu}=\bigsqcup_{\mu^{\prime}\leq\mu}S_{\mu^{\prime}},\quad T_{\geq\nu}=\bigsqcup_{\nu^{\prime}\geq\nu}T_{\nu^{\prime}}.

Let

S^{\prime}_{\mu}
,
T^{\prime}_{\nu}
,
S^{\prime}_{\leq\mu}
and
T^{\prime}_{\geq\nu}
be the subfunctors of
G_{K}

given by the inverse images
by the obvious map
\pi:G_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
.
We define the closures
\overline{S_{\mu}}
and
\overline{T_{\nu}}
of
S_{\mu}
and
T_{\nu}
in
\Gr_{G}
to be the quotients

\overline{S_{\mu}}=[\overline{S^{\prime}_{\mu}}/G_{\mathcal{O}}],\quad\overline{T_{\nu}}=[\overline{T^{\prime}_{\nu}}/G_{\mathcal{O}}]

where
\overline{S^{\prime}_{\mu}}
and
\overline{T^{\prime}_{\nu}}
are the closures in the ind-scheme
G_{K}
with the reduced structures.
The formal variants are the subfunctors
\hat{S}_{\mu}
,
\hat{T}_{\nu}
of
\Gr_{\hat{G}}
given by

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{\mu}=s^{\mu}\cdot\Gr_{\hat{U}},\quad\hat{T}_{\nu}=s^{\nu}\cdot\Gr_{U^{-}}\cong T_{\nu}.

There are obvious projections

\Gr_{B},\Gr_{\hat{B}}\rightarrow\Gr_{T}=\Gr_{T}=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}}\Gr_{T}^{\lambda}.

We define

(3.2.3)

\displaystyle\Gr_{B}^{\lambda}=\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{T}^{\lambda},\quad\Gr_{\hat{B}}^{\lambda}=\Gr_{\hat{B}}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{T}^{\lambda}.

Lemma 3.2.3
.

Let
\mu,\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The obvious map is a bijective fp closed immersion
S_{\mu}\to\Gr_{B}^{\mu}
.
We have
(S_{\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(\Gr_{B}^{\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The obvious map is a bijective fp closed immersion
T_{\nu}\to\Gr_{B^{-}}^{\nu}
.
We have
(T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(\Gr_{B^{-}}^{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

Proof.

Since
B/U\cong T
is fp and affine, by Proposition
3.1.1
the map

\Gr_{U}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}
is fp closed.
Since
S_{\mu}=s^{\mu}\cdot\Gr_{U}
by (
3.2.2
), by left translation by
s^{\mu}

the map
S_{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}
is also fp closed.
Part (a) follows, because
(\Gr_{T})_{\mathrm{red}}=X_{*}(T)
. Part (b) is proved similarly.
∎

Remark 3.2.4
.

We do not know if
S_{\mu}
and
T_{\nu}
are reduced.

Proposition 3.2.5
.

Let
k
be a field. We have

\Gr_{G}(k)=\bigsqcup_{\mu}S_{\mu}(k)=\bigsqcup_{\nu}T_{\nu}(k),\quad\Gr_{\hat{G}}(k)=\bigsqcup_{\mu}\hat{S}_{\mu}(k).

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
3.2.2
.
∎

There is a Plücker description of the semi-infinite orbits.
For any
\omega\in\Lambda_{+}
, let

\eta_{\omega}:L(\omega){Z}\to\ell_{\omega}=\mathbb{Z}\cdot v_{\omega}

be the projection to the highest weight line.
We consider the closed subsets of
G_{K}
given by

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle Y(\mu)

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{i\in I}\{g\in G_{K}\,;\,g^{-1}(\ell_{\omega_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{O})\subset s^{-\langle\omega_{i},\mu\rangle}L(\omega_{i})\otimes\mathcal{O}\},

(3.2.5)

\displaystyle Z(\nu)

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{i\in I}\{g\in G_{K}\,;\,\eta_{\omega_{i}}(gL(\omega_{i})\otimes\mathcal{O})\subset s^{\langle\omega_{i},\nu\rangle}\ell_{\omega_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}\}.

The set
Y(\mu)
is
U_{K}\times G_{\mathcal{O}}
-invariant and contains
s^{\mu^{\prime}}
for each
\mu^{\prime}\leqslant\mu
.
The set
Z(\nu)
is
U_{K}^{-}\times G_{\mathcal{O}}
-invariant and contains
s^{\nu^{\prime}}
for each
\nu^{\prime}\geqslant\nu
.
Hence, we have

S^{\prime}_{\leq\mu}\subseteq Y(\mu),\quad T^{\prime}_{\geq\nu}\subseteq Z(\nu).

In addition,
for each cocharacter
\lambda
such that
\lambda\not\leqslant\mu
there is a fundamental weight
\omega_{i}
such that

\langle\omega_{i},\lambda\rangle\geqslant\langle\omega_{i},\mu\rangle+1,

hence
s^{\lambda}\notin Y(\mu).

The Iwasawa decomposition in Proposition
3.2.2
yields

(S^{\prime}_{\leq\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}=Y(\mu)_{\mathrm{red}}
.
Similarly, we have

(T^{\prime}_{\geq\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}=Z(\nu)_{\mathrm{red}}.

Hence

(3.2.6)

\displaystyle\overline{S_{\mu}}\subset S_{\leq\mu},\quad\overline{T_{\nu}}\subset T_{\geq\nu},

Finally, let

\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
be the kernel of the evaluation
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}^{-}}\rightarrow\hat{U}
at
\infty
.

Proposition 3.2.6
.

Let
\mu,\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

\mathrm{(a)}

We have fp locally closed embeddings

S_{\mu}\,,\,T_{\nu}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
and

\hat{S}_{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}.

\mathrm{(b)}

We have

\overline{S_{\mu}}\subset S_{\leq\mu}
and

\overline{T_{\nu}}\subset T_{\geq\nu}
, with equalities if
G
is symmetrizable.

\mathrm{(c)}

The functor
\hat{S}_{\mu}
is representable by an ind-affine ind-scheme. We have
\hat{S}_{0}\cong\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
.

We need a preliminary result.

Proposition 3.2.7
.

Let
G
be a minimal symmetrizable KM group over
\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
{\check{\alpha}},{\check{\beta}}\in R^{\vee}_{+}
be such that
\langle{\check{\alpha}},\beta\rangle<0
.
Then
{\check{\alpha}}+{\check{\beta}}\in R^{\vee}_{+}
.
Hence, for any pair of dominant cocharacters
\mu<\lambda
, there is a positive
coroot
{\check{\alpha}}\in R_{+}^{\vee}
such that
\mu\leq\lambda-{\check{\alpha}}<\lambda
.

Proof.

The roots are the same over
\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{Q}
. Thus it is enough to check the assertion
over
\mathbb{Q}
where it follows from
[
64
, Lem. 3.6-3.7]
.
Once we have the first assertion, the proof becomes identical to
[
75
, Lem. 2.3]
.
∎

We can now prove Proposition
3.2.6
.

Proof.

For (a), it is enough to prove the claim for
\nu=0
up to a left translation by
s^{\nu}
.
The morphisms
S_{0}\,,\,T_{0}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
and
\hat{S}_{0}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
are fp locally closed embeddings
by Propositions
3.1.1
and
2.6.1
.
Now, we prove (b). Both assertions can be checked on
k
-points for an arbitrary field
k
.
Since
G
is symmetrizable, by Proposition
3.2.7
it is enough to check that
S_{\mu-\alpha}\subset\overline{S}_{\mu}

and
T_{\nu+\alpha}\subset\overline{T}_{\nu}
for any positive coroot
\alpha
.
Let us concentrate on the inclusion
S_{\mu-\alpha}\subset\overline{S}_{\mu}
because the other one is very similar.
Set
m=\langle\mu,{\check{\alpha}}\rangle-1
.
The inclusion follows from the construction of the curve
C_{\mu,\alpha}

in the proof of Proposition
3.3.5
. Indeed, with the notation there, we have

\displaystyle U_{K}\cap i_{\alpha}(K_{m})

\displaystyle\cong\begin{pmatrix}1&s^{m}\mathcal{O}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\Ad_{s^{\mu}}(G_{\mathcal{O}})\cap U_{K}\cap i_{\alpha}(K_{m})

\displaystyle\cong\begin{pmatrix}1&s^{m+1}\mathcal{O}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

For (c), note that the multiplication map

\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}\times\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\hat{U}_{K}

is a monomorphism, because
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}=\{1\}
.
It yields a monomorphism

f:\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}\rightarrow\hat{S}_{0}.

We must prove surjectivity on
R
-points for any ring
R
.
Since
\hat{U}
is split pro-unipotent, by
[
78
, Prop. A.6]
we have
H^{1}(S,\hat{U})=\{0\}
for any affine scheme
S
.
Thus there is a bijection

H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},\hat{U})\cong\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}^{-}}(R)\backslash\hat{U}_{K}(R)/\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}(R).

We consider the presentation
\hat{U}=\lim U^{(n)}
.
We have
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},\mathbb{G}_{a})=0
. Hence the maps

H_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},U^{(n+1)})\to H_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},U^{(n)})
are surjective.
We deduce that
{}^{1}\lim H_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},U^{(n)})=0
.
Hence
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},\hat{U})=0
by
[
78
, Lem. A.3]
.
Since
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},\hat{U})=\hat{U}(R)
, we deduce that the map
f
above is an isomorphism.
Finally
\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
is an ind-affine ind-scheme.
∎

We need the following result, which strengthens Proposition
3.2.6
.

Theorem 3.2.8
.

The obvious map
\Gr_{B}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
restricts to an fp immersion
\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}

for each
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

Proof.

The map
\Gr_{B}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is already a monomorphism.
The isomorphism
T_{K}\times U_{K}\cong B_{K}
yields an isomorphism
T_{K}\times S_{0}\cong B_{K}/U_{\mathcal{O}}
.
On the left hand side we have an action of
T_{\mathcal{O}}
given by
t\cdot(h,u)=(ht^{-1},tut^{-1})
.
The twisted product
T_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S_{0}
yields a commutative diagram

\textstyle{T_{K}\times S_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{K}/U_{\mathcal{O}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gr_{B}}

The vertical maps are
T_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsors and the top horizontal one is an isomorphism.
So the bottom horizontal map is an isomorphism.
We must prove that the composed map
T_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S_{0}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is an fp immersion
when restricted to a connected component
T^{\mu}_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S_{0}
.
We can be check this étale locally, after pulling back to
G_{K}
and prove that the following map is an fp-immersion

(3.2.7)

T^{\mu}_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow G_{K},

with
S^{\prime}_{0}=S_{0}\times_{\Gr_{G}}G_{K}
. By left multiplication by
s^{-\mu}
we can further assume that
\mu=0
.
By Proposition
3.2.6
the map
S^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow G_{K}
is fp locally closed.
Since
\Gr^{0}_{T}
is an ind-thickening of
\Spec(k)
, the map (
3.2.7
) is ind-locally closed.
Indeed, if the maps
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}Z
are such that
g\circ f
is fp locally closed,
the map
f
is a nilpotent closed immersion, and
g
is a monomorphism of ft schemes,
then the map
g
is fp locally closed
(after restricting to an open

U\subset Z
, we can replace locally closed by closed.
Then we use
[
86
, Tag. 03GN]
and the fact that a proper monomorphism is
a closed immersion
[
86
, Tag. 04XV]
).
Therefore, we must prove that for any
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
the map

(T^{0}_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S^{\prime}_{0})\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}\to G_{\leq\lambda}

is locally closed.
Let
R
be a
k
-algebra and consider

tug\in T_{K}(R)U_{K}(R)\cap G_{\mathcal{O}}(R)\cap G_{\leq\lambda}(R).

We have
tu\in G_{\leq\lambda}(R)
, because
G_{\leq\lambda}
is
G_{\mathcal{O}}
-invariant.
Hence, for any dominant character
\omega\in\Lambda^{+}
, we have

tu\cdot v_{\omega}=\omega(t)\cdot v_{\omega}\in s^{\langle\omega,\lambda\rangle}\mathcal{O}_{R}\cdot v_{\omega}.

Thus
t
is an
R
-point of the fiber product
T^{0}_{\leq\lambda}=T^{0}_{K}\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}
.
Since
T^{0}_{\leq\lambda}
is closed in
T_{K}
,
the quotient
T^{0}_{\leq\lambda}/T_{\mathcal{O}}
is a finite nilpotent scheme.
So the first map in the chain of maps

S^{\prime}_{0}\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}\rightarrow(T^{0}_{\leq\lambda}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S^{\prime}_{0})\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}\rightarrow G_{\leq\lambda},

is a nilpotent closed immersion. The second one is schematic, and the composite is fp locally closed.
Thus the second map is also fp locally closed.
Finally, the ind-ft ind-scheme

(T^{0}_{K}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S^{\prime}_{0})\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}

is sandwiched between

S^{\prime}_{0}\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}
and
(T^{0}_{\leq\lambda}\times^{T_{\mathcal{O}}}S^{\prime}_{0})\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}
.
Thus, we conclude by the following lemma.

Lemma 3.2.9
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
and
g:Y\rightarrow Z
be morphisms of ind-ft ind-schemes such that
f
and
gf
are nilpotent closed immersions and

g
is ind-locally closed. Then
g
is closed.

Proof.

By pulling-back to any closed subscheme
S\subset Z
, we are reduced to the case where
X
and
Z
are schemes of ft and
gf
is
defined by a coherent sheaf of ideals
\mathcal{I}
. We write
Y\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}Y_{\alpha}
with
Y_{\alpha}
locally closed in
Z
.
For some
\alpha
we have the commutative diagram of schemes

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g|_{Y_{\alpha}}}
\textstyle{Z}

As
f
is nilpotent, hence surjective, by
[
86
, Tag. 03GN]

the subscheme
Y_{\alpha}
is closed in
Z
. As
\mathcal{I}
is coherent,
there exists
\alpha_{0}
such that for every
\beta\geq\alpha_{0}
we have
Y_{\alpha_{0}}\cong Y_{\beta}
.
Thus
g
is a closed immersion.
∎

∎

Remark 3.2.10
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The ind-scheme
\hat{S}_{0}
is wild: the transition maps are closed but may not be fp.

\mathrm{(b)}

The proof above implies that

(\overline{S^{\prime}_{\mu}})_{\mathrm{red}}=Y(\mu)_{\mathrm{red}}
and

(\overline{T^{\prime}_{\nu}})_{\mathrm{red}}=Z(\nu)_{\mathrm{red}}.

\mathrm{(c)}

The map
\hat{T}_{\nu}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
may not be locally closed and

\Gr_{\hat{G}}(k)\neq\bigsqcup_{\nu}\hat{T}_{\nu}(k)
, see
[
15
, §2.3]
.

Proposition 3.2.11
.

Let
\mu,\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

\mathrm{(a)}

S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset\iff\mu\geq\nu
.

\mathrm{(b)}

S_{\mu}\cap T_{\mu}=\Spec(\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

If
S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset
, then the combination of (
3.2.4
) and (
3.2.5
) yields

\langle\omega_{i},\mu\rangle\geq\langle\omega_{i},\nu\rangle
for each
i\in I
,
hence
\mu\geq\nu
.
To prove (b), note that (a) and Proposition
3.2.6
imply that the open immersions

(3.2.8)

S_{\mu}\cap T_{\mu}\hookrightarrow S_{\mu}\cap\overline{T_{\mu}}\hookrightarrow\overline{S_{\mu}}\cap\overline{T_{\mu}}

are bijective. Thus the intersection
S_{\mu}\cap T_{\mu}\cong\overline{S_{\mu}}\cap\overline{T_{\mu}}
is closed in
\Gr_{G}
.
Further
S_{\mu}
and
T_{\mu}
are contained in the attractor and repeller of the point
[s^{\mu}]

for the action by
2{\check{\rho}}
on
\Gr_{G}
.
So any element
x\in(S_{\mu}\cap T_{\mu})(R)
yields a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}

that factors through
S_{\mu}\cap T_{\mu}
, because it is closed.
Composing this map with the obvious map
S_{\mu}\to\hat{S}_{\mu}
,
we get a map
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}\rightarrow\hat{S}_{\mu}
that is constant equal to
[s^{\mu}]
,
because
\hat{S}_{\mu}
is ind-affine by Proposition
3.2.6
.
∎

We will prove in Proposition
4.2.5
that the intersection
(S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft-scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.
Finally, we define the Laurent-series counterpart of our objects, i.e., we consider the functor
\Gr_{G}^{\mathrm{f}}=[G_{\hat{K}}/G_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}]

and the subfunctors

(3.2.9)

\displaystyle S_{\mu}^{\mathrm{f}}=s^{\mu}\cdot\Gr_{U}^{\mathrm{f}},\quad T_{\nu}^{\mathrm{f}}=s^{\nu}\cdot\Gr_{U^{-}}^{\mathrm{f}},\quad\hat{S}_{\mu}^{{\mathrm{f}}}=s^{\mu}\cdot\Gr_{\hat{U}}^{\mathrm{f}}.

Lemma 3.2.12
.

The obvious maps
S^{{\mathrm{f}}}_{\nu},T^{{\mathrm{f}}}_{\nu}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}^{\mathrm{f}}
are fp locally closed.
We have
\hat{S}_{0}\cong\hat{S}_{0}^{{\mathrm{f}}}.

Proof.

The first claim follows from Proposition
3.1.1
and Proposition
2.6.1
.
For second one, by Proposition
3.2.6
(c) it is sufficient to prove that
\hat{S}_{0}^{{\mathrm{f}}}\cong\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
.
By
[
9
, Lem. 2.2.11(b)]
, we have a Beauville-Laszlo gluing for
\hat{U}
, which yields

H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},\hat{U})\cong\hat{U}(R[t^{-1}])\backslash\hat{U}(R(\!(t)\!))/\hat{U}(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)=\{0\},

as in the proof of Proposition
3.2.6
(c).
We then conclude as in loc. cit. that
\hat{S}_{0}^{{\mathrm{f}}}\cong\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
.
∎

3.2.12.
Affineness of semi-infinite orbits

Let
G
be a minimal KM group over
\mathbb{Z}
.

Theorem 3.2.13
.

The immersions
S_{\mu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G}
and
T_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G}
are fp affine.

First, we consider the formal setting.

Lemma 3.2.14
.

The morphism
\Gr_{\hat{U}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
is affine and fp locally closed.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.2.6
, we already know that this morphism is fp and locally closed.
By Lemma
2.1.4
, to check that the morphism
\Gr_{\hat{U}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
is affine it is enough to prove it after pulling to

\hat{G}_{K}
.
Let

\hat{S}^{\prime}_{0}=\Gr_{\hat{U}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\hat{G}_{K}.

Since the morphism
\hat{G}_{K}\times\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}\to\hat{G}_{K}
is ind-affine, by Lemma
2.1.4
, the morphism
\hat{G}_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
is
also ind-affine.
Thus by base change and Proposition
3.2.6

we deduce that
\hat{S}^{\prime}_{0}
is ind-affine, fp and locally closed in
\hat{G}_{K}
.
Since
\hat{G}_{K}
is ind-affine, the map
\hat{S}_{0}^{\prime}\rightarrow\hat{G}_{K}
is affine by
[
86
, Tag. 01SG]
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.15
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The canonical maps

\Gr_{U}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{U}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}

and

\Gr_{B}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{B}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}

are bijective fp closed immersions. In particular they are affine.

\mathrm{(b)}

The map

S_{\mu}\rightarrow\hat{S}_{\mu}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}

is a bijective fp closed immersion.

Proof.

Closed immersions are affine. Let us prove the remaining statements.
Part (b) follows from (a) and (
3.2.1
).
By Proposition
3.2.6
, the maps
\Gr_{\hat{U}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
and
\Gr_{U}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
are fp locally closed.
Thus, the map

\Gr_{U}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{U}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}

is fp locally closed. Hence, it is enough to prove that
k
-points are the same for
k
an algebraically closed field.
Let
E
be a
k
-point of
\Gr_{\hat{U}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}
.
By Proposition
2.6.1
, we have
G/U\cong\hat{G}/\hat{U}
.
Thus the
G
-torsor
E
over
\mathbb{A}_{k}^{1}
has a
U
-reduction, see §
3.1.0
and §
3.1.5
.
We must prove that it defines an
k
-point of
\Gr_{U}
.
The
G
-torsor
E
is trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
.
Thus it yields an element in
(G/U)(K)
.
It lifts to
G(K)
by Proposition
3.2.1
.
This implies that the
U
-reduction of
E
is also trivial over
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
, proving the claim.
The proof for
\Gr_{B}
is the same using Theorem
3.2.8
instead.
∎

We can now finish the proof of Theorem
3.2.13
.

Proof.

We can assume that
\nu=0
.
By base change and composition, Lemmas
3.2.14
and
3.2.15
imply that the map

S_{0}=\Gr_{U}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is affine and fp.
The corresponding statement for
T_{0}
is proved in a similar way using
\hat{G}^{{\mathrm{op}}}
.
∎

Remark 3.2.16
.

Lemma
3.2.15
is finer than saying that we have equivalences on reduced stacks, because the map

\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{red}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
may not be schematic.

3.3.
The Cartan decomposition

3.3.0.
The Cartan semigroup

If
G
is a minimal KM group over a field
k
, then the Cartan decomposition may not hold for
G_{K}
.
One must introduce the Cartan sub-semigroup of
G_{K}
.
Its definition is inspired from
[
21
, Appendix A]
.
Let
g\in G(K)
.
We say that
g
is bounded if for each representation
L(\omega)
of highest weight
\omega\in\Lambda_{+}
,
there exists an integer
N_{\omega}
such that

g(L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}})\subset s^{-N_{\omega}}L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}}.

We consider the sub-semigroups
G_{b}(k)
and
G_{c}(k)
of
G(K)
given by

(3.3.1)

G_{b}(k)=\{g\in G(K)\,;\,g\penalty\ \text{is bounded}\},\quad G_{c}(k)=G_{b}(k)^{-1}.

They are stable by left and right action by
G(\mathcal{O})
, because
G(\mathcal{O})
preserves
L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}}
.
For
v\in L(\omega)_{K}
we define

\ord(v)=\min\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\,;\,s^{n}v\in L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}}\}.

By definition the element
v_{\omega}
has the order 0.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}
. We have
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
if and only if
s^{\lambda}\in G_{c}(k)
.

Proof.

For any vector
v\in L(\omega)
of weight
\mu
, we have
s^{\lambda}v=s^{\langle\mu,\lambda\rangle}v.

Hence
s^{\lambda}
is bounded if and only
\langle\mu,\lambda\rangle\geq-N_{\omega}
for some
N_{\omega}\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\mu\leq\omega
and
all

\omega
.
Hence, if and only if the set
\{\langle\alpha,\lambda\rangle\,;\,\alpha\in Q^{+}\}
is bounded above, thus
\lambda\in-\Lambda_{+}
.
∎

By Lemma
3.3.1
, for any field
k
we have

(3.3.2)

G_{c}(k)\supset\bigcup_{\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}}G(\mathcal{O})s^{\lambda}G(\mathcal{O}).

Proposition 3.3.2
.

For any field
k
, we have an equality

G_{c}(k)=\bigcup_{\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}}G(\mathcal{O})s^{\lambda}G(\mathcal{O}).

In particular, the right hand side is a semigroup.

Proof.

The proof in
[
21
, Appendix]
, which is based on the Iwasawa decomposition, applies.
We recall this proof, because loc. cit. considers only the untwisted affine case with coefficients in
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
.
We will use the Iwasawa decomposition in the form
G(\mathcal{O})T(K)U(K)
.
Let
g\in G_{b}(k)
.
By Lemma
3.3.1
it is enough to prove that
g=k_{1}s^{-\lambda}k_{2}
for
k_{1},k_{2}\in G(\mathcal{O})
and
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
.
Fix a dominant weight
\omega\in\Lambda_{+}
.
Since
g\in G_{b}(k)
, we may
choose
k\in G(\mathcal{O})
such that the element
(gk)\cdot v_{\omega}
in
L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}}

has maximal order.
By Proposition
3.2.2
there a re
k_{1}
,
u
and
t
such that

gk=k_{1}tu,\quad k_{1}\in G(\mathcal{O}),\quad u\in U(K),\quad t\in T(K).

We must prove that
u\in U(\mathcal{O})
.
Assume that
u\notin G(\mathcal{O})
.
The Iwasawa decomposition yields
u^{-}
,
\mu
and
k_{2}
such that

u=u^{-}s^{\mu}k_{2},\quad u^{-}\in U^{-}(K),\quad\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}},\quad k_{2}\in G(\mathcal{O})

We have
u\in T^{\prime}_{\mu}\cap S^{\prime}_{0}
.
By Proposition
3.2.11
, we have

T^{\prime}_{0}\cap S^{\prime}_{0}=G(\mathcal{O})
.
Since
u\notin G(\mathcal{O})
, we deduce that
\mu\neq 0
.
By Proposition
3.2.11
, we also have

T^{\prime}_{\mu}\cap S^{\prime}_{0}\neq\emptyset\iff\mu\leq 0.

Thus
\mu<0
.
Now we have

(3.3.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\ord((gkk_{2}^{-1})\cdot v_{\omega})&=\ord(k_{1}tu^{-}s^{\mu}\cdot v_{\omega})\\
&=\ord(tu^{-}s^{\mu}\cdot v_{\omega})\\
&\geq\ord(tv_{\omega})-\langle\omega,\mu\rangle\\
&=\ord(gkv_{\omega})-\langle\omega,\mu\rangle.\end{split}

This contradicts the maximality of the order. So
u\in G(\mathcal{O})
.
∎

3.3.2.
The cell decomposition of the Cartan semigroup

Let
G
be the minimal KM group over
\mathbb{Z}
.
By Proposition
3.3.2
, for any field
k
and any element
g\in G(K)
, there is an element

\inv_{k}(g)\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}\cup\{\infty\},

such that
\inv(g)
is the image of
g
in the double quotient

G(\mathcal{O})\backslash G_{c}(k)/G(\mathcal{O})
if
g\in G_{c}(k)
, and
\inv(g)=\infty
otherwise.
Now, for any ring
R
any
g\in G_{K}(R)
and any
x\in\Spec(R)
of residue field
k(x)
,
we set

\inv_{x}(g)=\inv_{k(x)}(g|_{\Spec(k(x))})

Lemma 3.3.3
.

For any
g\in G_{K}(R)
and
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
, the following subset of
\Spec(R)
is Zariski closed

\Spec(R)_{\leq\lambda}=\{x\in\Spec(R)\,;\,\inv_{x}(g)\leq\lambda\}.

Proof.

For each dominant character
\omega
,
the
G
-action (
2.3.2
) yields a morphism of ind-schemes

\rho_{\omega}:G_{K}\to\End^{\mathrm{ind}}(L(\omega)_{K}).

We consider the functor
X(\lambda,\omega)
such that

(3.3.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}X(\lambda,\omega)(R)&=\{g\in G_{K}(R)\,;\,\rho_{\omega}(g)(L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}_{k(x)}})\subset s^{\langle\omega,\lambda\rangle}L(\omega)_{\mathcal{O}_{k(x)}}\,,\,\forall x\in\Spec(R)\}.\end{split}

Given an
R
-point
g
of
G_{K}
,
the following fiber product is closed in
\Spec(R)

X(\lambda,\omega)\times_{G_{K}}\Spec(R)

We claim it coincides with
\Spec(R)_{\leq\lambda}
.
Since
X(\lambda,\omega)
is
G_{\mathcal{O}}\times G_{\mathcal{O}}
-invariant and contains
s^{\mu}
for any
\mu\leq\lambda
, we have

\Spec(R)_{\leq\lambda}\subseteq X(\lambda,\omega)\times_{G_{K}}\Spec(R).

Set
X(\lambda)=\bigcap_{i\in I}X(\lambda,\omega_{i})
.
Then

\Spec(R)_{\leq\lambda}\subseteq X(\lambda)\times_{G_{K}}\Spec(R).

If
g\in X(\lambda)
then
g\in G_{c}(k(x))
for each
x\in\Spec(R)
.
Further, for each cocharacter
\mu
such that
\mu\not\leqslant\lambda
there is a fundamental weight
\omega_{i}
such that
\langle\omega_{i},\mu\rangle>\langle\omega_{i},\lambda\rangle
.
Hence
s^{\mu}\notin X(\lambda,\omega_{i})
. Thus

(3.3.5)

X(\lambda)\times_{G_{K}}\Spec(R)_{\leq\mu}=\emptyset,\quad\Spec(R)_{\leq\lambda}=X(\lambda)\times_{G_{K}}\Spec(R).

∎

By Lemma
3.3.3
, there is a reduced closed ind-subscheme
G_{\leq\lambda}\subset G_{K}
such that
for any field
k
we have

(3.3.6)

\displaystyle G_{\leq\lambda}(k)=\{g\in G_{K}(k)\,;\,\inv_{k}(g)\leq\lambda\}.

Let
G_{\lambda}\subset G_{\leq\lambda}
be the open subset given by

G_{\lambda}=G_{\leq\lambda}\setminus\bigcup_{\mu<\lambda}G_{\leq\mu}.

We formulate the following proposition over fields. To have it over
\mathbb{Z}
, we need finer information.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

Let
G
be a minimal KM group over an algebraically closed field
k
.
The subfunctor
G_{c}\subset G_{K}
is a closed semigroup ind-subscheme.

Proof.

For any closed subscheme
Z\subset G_{K}
, we have

Z\times_{G_{K}}G_{c}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in S}Z\times_{G_{K}}G_{\leq\lambda}

for some finite
S\subset\Lambda_{+}
.
Thus
Z\times_{G_{K}}G_{c}
is closed in
Z
by Lemma
3.3.3
.
It remains to prove that
G_{c}
is a semigroup ind-scheme.
For
\lambda,\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
the multiplication gives a map

G_{\leq\lambda}\times G_{\leq\mu}\rightarrow G_{K}.

We must prove that it factors through
G_{c}
.
As we considered the reduced structure and work over an algebraically closed field, a product of reduced is reduced.
Thus proving that the map factors is a statement on closed points.
This statement is clear, because
G_{c}(k)
is a semigroup.
∎

Proposition 3.3.5
.

Let
G
be a minimal symmetrizable KM group.
The open subset
G_{\lambda}
of
G_{\leq\lambda}
is dense.

Proof.

We must prove that the closure of
G_{\lambda}
contains each stratum
G_{\mu}
for
\mu\leq\lambda
.
The argument adapts
[
90
, Prop. 2.1.5]
in the KM case.
Let
\mu<\lambda
with
\lambda,\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
.
Since
G
is symmetrizable, by Lemma
3.2.7
, there exists a positive coroot
\alpha
such that
\mu\leq\lambda-\alpha<\lambda
.
It suffices to prove that
G_{\lambda-\alpha}\subset\overline{G_{\lambda}}
.
To do this, we construct a curve

C_{\lambda,\alpha}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
in
\Gr
with

\infty\in\Gr_{\lambda-\alpha}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}\subset\Gr_{\lambda}
.
First, for any integer
m
let

\theta_{m}=\begin{pmatrix}s^{m}&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\in PGL_{2}(K).

Set

K_{m}=\Ad_{\theta_{m}}(SL_{2}(\mathcal{O}))

and

H_{m}=\Ad_{\theta_{m}}(I^{-})
, with
I^{-}\subset SL_{2}(\mathcal{O}^{-})
the opposite Iwahori subgroup.
We have
K_{m}/H_{m}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Let
i_{\alpha}:SL_{2}(K)\rightarrow G_{K}
be the morphism associated with
\alpha
.
Set
m=\langle\lambda,{\check{\alpha}}\rangle-1
.
We consider the orbit

C_{\lambda,\alpha}=i_{\alpha}(K_{m})\cdot[s^{\lambda}].

Since
i_{\alpha}(H_{m})\subset\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(G_{\mathcal{O}})
, we have
C_{\lambda,\alpha}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}.

Since
m\geqslant 0
, we have

\displaystyle G_{\mathcal{O}}\cap i_{\alpha}(K_{m})

\displaystyle\cong\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{O}^{\times}&s^{m}\mathcal{O}\\
\mathcal{O}&\mathcal{O}^{\times}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(G_{\mathcal{O}})\cap G_{\mathcal{O}}\cap i_{\alpha}(K_{m})

\displaystyle\cong\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{O}^{\times}&s^{m+1}\mathcal{O}\\
\mathcal{O}&\mathcal{O}^{\times}\end{pmatrix}

Hence, there is an isomorphism

(G_{\mathcal{O}}\cap i_{\alpha}(K_{m}))\cdot[s^{\lambda}]\cong\mathbb{A}^{1}.

We set

\sigma_{m}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-s^{m}\\
s^{-m}&0\end{pmatrix}=\Ad_{\theta_{m}}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\in K_{m}.

We have

s^{-\lambda+\alpha}\cdot i_{\alpha}(\sigma_{m})\cdot[s^{\lambda}]=i_{\alpha}(\sigma_{0})\in G_{\mathcal{O}}.

We deduce that

i_{\alpha}(\sigma_{m})\cdot[s^{\lambda}]=\{\infty\}

under the isomorphism
C_{\lambda,\alpha}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
and that
i_{\alpha}(\sigma_{m})\cdot[s^{\lambda}]\in\Gr_{\lambda-\alpha}
.
∎

3.4.
The Schubert cells

Let
G
be a simply connected minimal KM group over
\mathbb{Z}
, with the associated formal group
\hat{G}
over
\mathbb{Z}
.
We define the Schubert cell
\Gr_{\leqslant\lambda}
and the open Schubert cell
\Gr_{\lambda}
to be the substacks
of
\Gr_{G}
given by

(3.4.1)

\displaystyle\Gr_{\leqslant\lambda}=[G_{\leq\lambda}/G_{\mathcal{O}}],\quad\Gr_{\lambda}=[G_{\lambda}/G_{\mathcal{O}}].

The subfunctor
\Gr_{\lambda}\subset\Gr_{G}
is locally closed because
G_{\lambda}\subset G_{K}
is locally closed.
Let
i_{\lambda}
denote the immersion

(3.4.2)

\displaystyle i_{\lambda}:\Gr_{\lambda}\to\Gr_{G}.

Note that the set of points
\Gr_{\lambda}(k)
is a
G(\mathcal{O})
-orbit for any field
k
.

3.4.0.
The formal group case

We first consider the formal group case, where we have better representability statements.
Recall that
\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
is the kernel of the evaluation
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}^{-}}\rightarrow\hat{U}
at
\infty
.
Let
\hat{G}_{\pi}
and
\hat{I}
be the kernel of the evaluation
\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\hat{G}
at
0

and the inverse image of
\hat{B}
.
For each cocharacter
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}
we set

(3.4.3)

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{\lambda}^{-}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}),\quad\hat{U}_{\lambda}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}),\quad\hat{J}_{\lambda}=\hat{I}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{I})

Lemma 3.4.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The multiplication yields an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-ind-schemes

\hat{U}_{\lambda}^{-}\times\hat{U}_{\lambda}\cong\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}.

\mathrm{(b)}

The map
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
given by
g\rightarrow g\cdot[s^{\lambda}]
yields an immersion

[\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}/\hat{U}_{\lambda}]\cong\hat{U}_{\lambda}^{-}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}.

Proof.

We have the scheme isomorphism

\hat{U}\cong\Spec(\Sym(\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}))
.
By
[
80
, §3.2]
it yields compatible group ind-scheme isomorphisms

(3.4.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.71487pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.82646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.82646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.747pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 147.00446pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 93.01233pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise
