35.71489pt\raise-33.81445pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.71487pt\raise-33.81445pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.71487pt\raise-33.81445pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{\alpha}(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{K}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 131.04443pt\raise-33.81445pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\prod_{\alpha}s^{\langle\lambda,\alpha\rangle-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})_{\mathcal{O}^{-}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 131.04443pt\raise-33.81445pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.04445pt\raise-33.81445pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

The claim (a) follows.
To prove claim (
3.4.1
), note that by Proposition
3.2.6
the obvious map

\Gr_{\hat{U}}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
is an fp immersion and
\Gr_{\hat{U}}\cong\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}
whicn induces that
S_{\lambda}\cong s^{\lambda}\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}s^{-\lambda}
.
Further, by (
3.4.4
) the obvious inclusion

\hat{U}_{\lambda}^{-}\rightarrow\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}

is a closed immersion.
∎

Lemma 3.4.2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The inclusion
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\subset\hat{I}
yields an isomorphism of stacks

[\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}/\hat{U}_{\lambda}]\cong[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}].

\mathrm{(b)}

The map
\hat{I}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
given by
g\mapsto g\cdot[s^{\lambda}]
yields an immersion

[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]\hookrightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
.

Proof.

We must prove that the monomorphism

[\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}/\hat{U}_{\lambda}]\to[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]

is surjective.
We will use
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
instead of
\mathcal{O}
, because
if
W\hookrightarrow X
is a qc open immersion of ind-schemes,
then we have an isomorphism

(3.4.5)

\displaystyle W_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}\cong X_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}\times_{X}W

(the proof reduces to the scheme case, which is well-known).
We define
\hat{\hat{I}}\subset\hat{G}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}
and

U_{\widehat{\pi}}^{-}\subset U_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}^{-}

as above.
Since
\hat{\Omega}=U^{-}\hat{B}
is open in
\hat{G}
, from (
3.4.5
) we deduce that
\hat{\hat{I}}=U_{\widehat{\pi}}^{-}\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}T_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}
. As
U_{\widehat{\pi}}^{-}
fixes
s^{\lambda}
, we have

[\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}/(\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}))]\cong[\hat{\hat{I}}/(\hat{\hat{I}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{\hat{I}}))]

In addition, we have a monomorphism

[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]\to[\hat{\hat{I}}/(\hat{\hat{I}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{\hat{I}}))]

and a similar computation as in Lemma
3.4.1
gives

\displaystyle[\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}/(\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}))]\cong\hat{U}_{\hat{\mathcal{O}}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}})=\hat{U}_{\lambda}^{-}\cong[\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}/\hat{U}_{\lambda}].

The lemma follows.
∎

By §
2.3.2
, the parabolic
\hat{P}_{\lambda}
and the opposite parabolic
P_{\lambda}^{-}

represent the functors

R\mapsto\{g\in\hat{G}(R)\,;\,s^{\lambda}gs^{-\lambda}\in\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}(R)\},\quad R\mapsto\{g\in\hat{G}(R)\,;\,s^{-\lambda}gs^{\lambda}\in\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}(R)\}

We have
P^{-}_{\lambda}=\hat{G}\cap\hat{K}_{\lambda}
with

\hat{K}_{\lambda}=\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}})
.
We consider the quotient stacks

\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}=[\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}/\hat{K}_{\lambda}],\quad\widehat{\underline{\Gr}}_{\lambda}=[\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}].

The evaluation
\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}\to\hat{G}
at 0 yields a morphism of stacks

(3.4.6)

\displaystyle\phi:\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\rightarrow\widehat{\underline{\Gr}}_{\lambda}.

Proposition 3.4.3
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The stack
\widehat{\underline{\Gr}}_{\lambda}
is representable by a scheme.

\mathrm{(b)}

We have a Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\hat{U}_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}}

\mathrm{(c)}

We have
[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]\cong\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{\lambda}
.
It is a qc open subset of
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}
.
We have
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}=\bigcup_{w\in W/W_{\lambda}}w\cdot[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}].

\mathrm{(d)}

The stack
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}
is representable by a weak ind-scheme. It is locally closed in
\Gr_{\hat{G}}
.

Proof.

Part (a) is obvious because
\hat{G}/P^{-}_{\lambda}
is a partial thick flag manifold.
To prove (b) we consider the open cell
\hat{U}_{\lambda}
in
\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}
.
By (a) we have the map
\phi:\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\rightarrow\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}
such that

\phi^{-1}(1)=\hat{G}_{\pi}\cdot[s^{\lambda}]
.
Part (b) follows because the evaluation at zero sends surjectively
[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]
to
\hat{U}_{\lambda}
(seen as the
\hat{B}
-orbit of
s^{\lambda}
.
To prove (c), note that Lemma
3.4.2
yields the inclusion

[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]\subset\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{\lambda}.

Further, the intersection
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{\lambda}
is contained in the attractor for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}

given by conjugation by
\lambda
, while, by §
2.3.2
, the group
\hat{U}_{\lambda}
is the strict attractor of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}

given by conjugation by
\lambda
.
Thus the map
\phi

takes
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{\lambda}
into
\hat{U}_{\lambda}
.
Thus the reverse inclusion follows from (b).
The last claims in (c) follows form the open cover

\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}=\bigcup_{w\in W/W_{\lambda}}w\cdot\hat{U}_{\lambda}.

The representability statement in part (d) follows from (c).
By Lemma
3.4.2
, there is a locally closed embedding
[\hat{I}/\hat{J}_{\lambda}]\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}
.
Thus the map

\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}

is also locally closed by
[
86
, Tag. 0FCZ]
, proving (d).
∎

The
W
-translates of the open subset
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{\lambda}

yield the open cover

(3.4.7)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}=\bigcup_{w}\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}.

Similarly, let
\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\subset\Gr_{\lambda}
be the

w
-translate of the intersection
\Gr_{\lambda}\cap S_{\lambda}

which yield the open cover

(3.4.8)

\displaystyle\Gr_{\lambda}=\bigcup_{w}\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}.

3.4.3.
The minimal group case

Proposition 3.4.4
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The obvious map yields a bijective closed immersion

\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}\rightarrow\Gr_{\lambda}.

\mathrm{(b)}

The stack
\Gr_{\lambda}
is locally closed in
\Gr_{G}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

The obvious map
\Gr_{\lambda}^{\circ}\rightarrow\Gr_{\lambda}
is a qc open immersion such that

\Gr_{\lambda}=\bigcup_{w\in W}\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

For (a) and (b), working étale locally and using (
3.4.1
) and the Plücker description of
G_{\leq\lambda}
given by (
3.3.4
), we get an inclusion

\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}\subset\Gr_{\lambda}

which is bijective on closed points.
This map is also locally closed by base change and Proposition
3.4.3
(d).

The map
\Gr_{\lambda}\to\Gr_{G}
is a locally closed immersion by base change, (a) and Proposition
3.4.3
(d).
Now we prove (c). By (a) and because the open cells of
\hat{G}/P^{-}_{\lambda}
and
G/P_{\lambda}^{-}
match, we have

(3.4.9)

\widehat{\Gr}^{\circ}_{\lambda}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}=\Gr_{\lambda}^{\circ}.

The map
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\rightarrow\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}
is qc and open by Proposition
3.4.3
(c).
Hence, the map
\Gr_{\lambda}^{\circ}\rightarrow\Gr_{\lambda}
is also qc and open by base change.
The last claim is proved similarly.
∎

We abbreviate

(3.4.10)

\displaystyle K_{\lambda}=G_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(G_{\mathcal{O}}),\quad U_{\lambda}=U_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(U_{\mathcal{O}}).

Proposition 3.4.5
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The map
G_{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
,
g\mapsto g\cdot[s^{\lambda}]
yields an isomorphism

[G_{\mathcal{O}}/K_{\lambda}]_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(\Gr_{\lambda})_{\mathrm{red}}.

\mathrm{(b)}

The isomorphism in
\mathrm{(a)}
factors through an isomorphism

[U_{\mathcal{O}}/U_{\lambda}]_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(\Gr^{\circ}_{\lambda})_{\mathrm{red}}.

Proof.

We have monomorphisms

[G_{\mathcal{O}}/K_{\lambda}]\subset\Gr_{\lambda}

and

[U_{\mathcal{O}}/U_{\lambda}]\subset\Gr^{\circ}_{\lambda}.

We have to prove that functors are the same on reduced algebras. The claim is étale local.
By Proposition
3.4.4
, for any ring
R
,
we can assume that the map
G_{\mathcal{O}}(R)\rightarrow\Gr_{G}(R)
,
g\mapsto g\cdot[s^{\lambda}]
lands in some open set

\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}(R)
Zariski locally on
R
.
So we are reduced to prove (b).
Both functors in (b) commute with filtered colimits, being lft prestacks as defined in §
5.2.2
, as quotients of ind-ft group ind-schemes.
Indeed, the source is a quotient in the category of ind-schemes of ind-ft,
and the target is fp locally closed in such a quotient by Proposition
3.4.4
.
So it is enough to compare the functors on reduced local strictly henselian rings.
Let
R
be such a ring and
g\in\Gr_{\lambda}^{\circ}(R)
.
Since
R
is strictly local henselian, we can assume that
g
admits a lift
\tilde{g}\in G_{K}(R)
and that

\tilde{g}=us^{\lambda}k,\quad u\in U_{K}(R),k\in G_{\mathcal{O}}(R).

As
g\in\Gr_{\lambda}(R)
, we can also assume that
k=1
.
We must prove that
u\in U_{\mathcal{O}}(R)
.
Since
U_{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow U_{K}
is closed and
R
is reduced, by
[
86
, Tag. 056B]
,
the map
u:\Spec(R)\rightarrow U_{K}
factors through
U_{\mathcal{O}}
if it does on each closed point
x\in\Spec(R)
.
So we can assume that
R=k
is a field, that
u\in U(K)
, and it is enough to check that
u\in\hat{U}(\mathcal{O})
.
By Proposition
3.4.3
and Lemma
3.4.2
, we have

us^{\lambda}=vs^{\lambda}h,\quad v\in\hat{U}(\mathcal{O}),h\in\hat{G}(\mathcal{O}).

We deduce that

h=s^{-\lambda}v^{-1}us^{\lambda}\in\hat{G}(\mathcal{O})\cap\hat{U}(K)=\hat{U}(\mathcal{O}).

Since
\lambda
is dominant, we have
\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}})\subset\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}
.
We deduce that

u=vs^{\lambda}hs^{-\lambda}
lies in
\hat{U}(\mathcal{O}).

∎

4.
The Mirkovic-Vilonen cycles

4.1.
The space of maps

4.1.0.
The space of maps

Consider a scheme
S
, a proper fppf morphism
X\rightarrow S
and an
S
-algebraic stack
\mathcal{Y}
.
The functor
\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y})

on the category of
S
-schemes is such that

\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y})(T)=\Hom_{T}(X_{T},\mathcal{Y}_{T})

Here
X_{T}
,
\mathcal{Y}_{T}
are the corresponding base change and morphisms

X_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{T}
are morphisms of algebraic stacks over
T
.

Lemma 4.1.1
.

Assume that
\mathcal{Y}=Y
is a separated
S
-scheme.

\mathrm{(a)}

The functor
\Map_{S}(X,Y)
is representable by an
S
-algebraic space.

\mathrm{(b)}

Assume that
X
is projective and
Y
is a union of qc open subsets that admit a presentation
as a filtered limit of fp quasi-projective schemes over
S
with affine transition maps.
Then
\Map_{S}(X,Y)
is representable by an
S
-scheme.

Proof.

The assertion is local on
S
, so we can assume that
S
is affine.
Assume first that
Y
is qc.
By
[
86
, Tag. 0GS1]
, we have
Y\cong\lim Y_{a}
with
Y_{a}\to S
fp and with affine transition maps,
and by
[
86
, Tag. 01ZQ]
we can assume
Y_{a}\to S
to be separated.
Then, by
[
86
, §0DPL]
the functor
\Map_{S}(X,Y_{a})
is representable by a lfp algebraic space over
S
.
For the general case, write
Y=\bigcup U
as the union of qc open subsets.
For
U\subset U^{\prime}

the space of maps
\Map_{S}(X,U)
is open in
\Map_{S}(X,U^{\prime})
.
Hence
\Map_{S}(X,Y)
is an increasing union of open subsets.
Thus it is representable by an
S
-algebraic space, proving (a).

Assume now that
X
is projective and
Y_{a}
is quasi-projective fp over
S
.
By noetherian approximation
we can assume that
S
is noetherian. Then, by
[
32
, Thm. 5.23]
, the functor
\Map_{S}(X,Y_{a})

is representable by a lfp
S
-scheme.
Thus, we have
\Map_{S}(X,Y)\cong\lim\Map_{S}(X,Y_{a})
, and by

[
86
, Tag. 05Y6]
the transition maps are affine.
For the general case, write
Y=\bigcup U
as the union of its qc open subsets
that are limit of quasi-projective fp schemes over
S
.
Then part (b) follows as above.
∎

Example 4.1.2
.

An example of scheme which is an union of qc pro-quasi-projective
open subsets is
\mathbb{P}(V)
for
V
a pro-finite dimensional vector space.
Indeed, write
V\cong\lim V_{a}
. Then
\mathbb{P}(V)
is the union of the open subsets

\mathbb{P}(V)\,\setminus\,\mathbb{P}(\Ker(V\rightarrow V_{a}))
,
and each of them is the limit of

\mathbb{P}(V_{b})\,\setminus\,\mathbb{P}(\Ker(V_{b}\rightarrow V_{a}))
for
b\geq a
.
Consequently, any fp closed subset in
\mathbb{P}(V)
also satisfies this property.

Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a lfp algebraic stack over
S
with quasi-affine diagonal.
By
[
86
, Tag. 0CMG]
, the diagonal of
\mathcal{Y}
is fp.
Thus
\mathcal{Y}
has affine stabilizers,
as a quasi-affine algebraic group over a field is affine by
[
24
, Exp. VI, Prop. 11.11]
.
By
[
47
, Thm. 1.2]
the functor

\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y})
is representable by a lfp algebraic stack over
S
with affine diagonal.
Fix an open substack
\bullet\subset\mathcal{Y}
.
Let

(4.1.1)

\displaystyle\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})\subset\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y})

be the sub-functor
which consists of the maps that generically land in
\bullet
.
We consider the evaluation map

\text{can}:X\times\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{Y},\,(x,f)\mapsto f(x)

and the projection

p:X\times\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\Map_{S}(X,\mathcal{Y}).

The map
p
is fppf. Thus it is open. We have

(4.1.2)

\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})=p(\text{can}^{-1}(\bullet))

Hence the map (
4.1.1
) is open.
The subfunctor

\Map_{S}(X,\bullet)

is also open.
Indeed, for any
S
-scheme
T
and any map
\sigma_{T}:X_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{T}
,
the locus where
\sigma_{T}
maps to
\bullet
is
T\,\setminus\,p(X_{T}\,\setminus\,\sigma_{T}^{-1}(\bullet))
,
which is open in
T
because the map
p:X_{T}\rightarrow T
is proper.
Note that the openess holds without assuming that
X\rightarrow S
is fppf.

4.1.2.
Maps to pointy stacks

Let
S
be a scheme.
Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a lfp Artin
S
-stack and
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathcal{Y}
a closed substack with complement

\bullet=\mathcal{Y}\,\setminus\,\mathcal{D}
. Assume that
\bullet

is isomorphic to
S
and is dense open in
\mathcal{Y}
.
In
[
25
]
, such a stack is called a pointy stack.
Consider a diagram

(4.1.3)

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.88887pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.24303pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.24303pt\raise-26.0837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.43256pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

where
i
is closed with complement
j
, and
\pi
is proper fppf.
Let
\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})
be the functor that assigns to any
S
-scheme
T
the groupoid of maps

f:X_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{T}
that generically land in
\bullet
and such that
f^{-1}(\mathcal{D}_{T})
is proper over
T
.
By (
4.1.2
), the locus
U_{T}
where
f
lands in
\bullet
surjects on
T
.

Lemma 4.1.3
.

Assume that the diagonal of
\mathcal{Y}
is separated and the map
\pi
has geometrically integral fibers.
For any
S
-scheme
T
the groupoid
\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})(T)
is a set.

Proof.

Write
\mathcal{Y}
as a quotient of a smooth
S
-groupoid in algebraic spaces
[U/R]
, see
[
86
, Tag. 04T5]
.
Assume that the diagonal of
\mathcal{Y}
is separated and the map
\pi
has geometrically integral fibers.
The map
R\rightarrow U\times_{S}U
is also separated, see
[
57
, Prop. 4.3.2]
.
Further, the stabilizer groupoid
G=U\times_{U\times_{S}U}R\rightarrow U
is separated by base change.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Y}}
be the inertia stack.
By
[
86
, Tag. 06PR]
, we have an cartesian diagram

\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}

Thus, by descent, the map
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Y}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is separated.
Fix
\sigma:X_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
.
Set
I_{X_{T}}=\sigma^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{Y}}
.
We must prove that

I_{X_{T}}\cong X_{T}.

Consider a section
\epsilon:X_{T}\to I_{X_{T}}
given by
\id_{x_{T}}
for each
x_{T}\in\mathcal{Y}(X_{T})
.
Let
T^{\prime}\rightarrow T
and
y\in(I_{X_{T}})(T^{\prime})
.
By assumption, over the open subset
U_{T^{\prime}}=\sigma^{-1}(\bullet)_{T^{\prime}}
of
X_{T^{\prime}}
, the morphisms
y
and
\epsilon
agree.
Since the morphism
X_{T}\rightarrow T
is fppf with geometrically integral fibers, and the open set
U_{T^{\prime}}
is fiberwise non-empty,
we deduce that
U_{T^{\prime}}
is schematically dense fiberwise.
Thus by
[
42
, Prop. 11.10.10]
, the open subset
U_{T^{\prime}}
is schematically dense in
X_{T^{\prime}}
.
Then claim follows, because the map
I_{X_{T}^{\prime}}\rightarrow X_{T^{\prime}}
is separated.
∎

Lemma 4.1.4
.

Let
S
be a scheme.
Let
W
be an
S
-scheme, and
W_{\circ}
an open subscheme of
W
.
Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a pointy
S
-stack.
There is a bijection between the set of
S
-maps
\sigma_{\circ}:W_{\circ}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
such that

(\sigma_{\circ})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subset W_{\circ}
is closed,
and the set of
S
-maps
\sigma:W\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
such that
\sigma^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subset W_{\circ}
.

Proof.

Recall that
S\cong\bullet=\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{D}
.
Given
\sigma_{\circ}
, the scheme
W
admits an open cover given by
W_{\circ}
and
W\setminus(\sigma_{\circ})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})
.
Let
\sigma|_{W_{\circ}}=\sigma_{\circ}
and let
\sigma|_{W\setminus(\sigma_{\circ})^{-1}(\mathcal{D})}
be
the structural map to
S=\bullet
. This defines the map
\sigma
. The converse is obvious.
∎

The following conjecture is made by Drinfeld
[
25
, Conj. 4.2.3]
.
Note that Drinfeld only considers the case
S=\Spec(k)
for
k
a field, and
C
a smooth curve.
So the assumption on the relative compactification is automatic.

Conjecture 4.1.5
.

With the assumptions above, assume in addition that
\mathcal{Y}
has a separated diagonal,
then
\Map_{S,\bullet}(C,\mathcal{Y})
is representable by a lfp
S
-algebraic space.

We prove the following weaker statement.

Proposition 4.1.6
.

Assume that the diagonal of
\mathcal{Y}
is quasi-affine and we are in the situation of (
4.1.3
).
The stack
\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})
is representable by a lfp
S
-algebraic space.

Proof.

The stack
\Map_{S,\bullet}(\overline{X},\mathcal{Y})
is representable by a lfp algebraic stack over
S
by
[
47
, Thm. 1.2]
.
By
[
86
, 04SZ]
and Lemma
4.1.3
,
we deduce that it is representable by an
S
-algebraic lfp space.
By Lemma
4.1.4
, the functor

\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})\subset\Map_{S,\bullet}(\overline{X},\mathcal{Y})

consists of
maps
\sigma_{1}:\overline{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
such that
\sigma_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subset X
.
Restricting
\sigma_{1}
to
H
yields a map of functors:

\Map_{S,\bullet}(\overline{X},\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\ \Map_{S}(H,\mathcal{Y}).

Thus, we have the following isomorphism

\Map_{S,\bullet}(X,\mathcal{Y})\cong\Map_{S,\bullet}(\overline{X},\mathcal{Y})\times_{\Map_{S}(H,\mathcal{Y})}\Map_{S}(H,\bullet)

The claim follows because
\Map_{S}(H,\bullet)
is open in
\Map_{S}(H,\mathcal{Y})
.
Note that here
H\rightarrow S
is only proper but as mentioned after (
4.1.2
), openess does not need the fppf part.
∎

4.2.
Affine Zastavas

In this section we work over the ring
k=\mathbb{Z}
, because the next section will involve a counting argument over finite fields.
We prove that the Zastava are representable by finite type schemes in Proposition
4.2.5
.
So far, it was only proved that they have a finite number of points over any finite field.

4.2.0.
Definition of the Zastavas

We define the Birkhoff stacks to be the double quotients

\Bk_{G}=[B^{-}\backslash G/B]=[B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}/\hat{B}],\quad\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}=[U^{-}\setminus\hat{G}/\hat{B}]

Proposition 4.2.1
.

The
\infty
-stack
\Bk_{G}
is representable by a smooth Artin stack over
\mathbb{Z}
with affine diagonal.

Proof.

The proof is taken from
[
31
, Lem. 6]
.
Recall the open cell
\hat{\Omega}/\hat{B}\subset\hat{G}/\hat{B}
which is isomorphic to
U^{-}
.
For any finite set
F\subset W
we form the following open subset

\Omega_{F}=\bigcup_{w\in F}w\hat{\Omega}/\hat{B}.

Set

U_{w}^{-}=U^{-}\cap w(U^{-}),\quad U^{-,w}=U^{-}\cap w(\hat{U}).

Choose an integer
n
such that for each
w\in F
we have

U_{w}^{-}\cong U_{(n)}^{-}\times W_{n}

with
W_{n}
being the split unipotent group generated by negative roots that occur in
U_{w}^{-}
but not in
U_{(n)}^{-}
.
Here
U_{(n)}^{-}\subset U^{-}
is defined as in Proposition
2.4.3
.
By Proposition
2.4.2
, the set
\Omega_{F}
has a finite cover by open subsets isomorphic to

U_{(n)}^{-}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
for some integers
m
,
n
.
We deduce that
[U_{(n)}^{-}\backslash\Omega_{F}]
is representable by a smooth ft scheme.
Now, since
U_{(n)}^{-}\subset B^{-}
has a finite dimensional quotient, we have

\Bk_{G}=\bigcup_{F\subset W}[B^{-}\backslash\Omega_{F}]

is an increasing union of qc opens that are representable by smooth ft Artin stacks.
Set

\mathcal{U}_{F}=[B^{-}\backslash\Omega_{F}].

For the last claim, since
\Delta_{\Bk_{G}}=\bigcup\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{F}}
, it is enough to prove
that
\mathcal{U}_{F}
has an affine diagonal.
By Lemma
2.1.4
, as
\Omega_{F}
is ind-separated, we obtain that
\mathcal{U}_{F}
has ind-affine diagonal.
On the other hand by considering

[U_{(n)}^{-}\backslash\Omega_{F}]\rightarrow\mathcal{U}_{F}

The source is a smooth qc scheme.
Thus it is quasi-separated.
Hence
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{F}}
is qc schematic by Lemma
2.1.5
.
The diagonal
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}_{F}}
being qc schematic and ind-affine, it is affine.
∎

By Proposition
4.2.1
, the stack
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}
is fp-smooth over
\mathbb{Z}
.
The substack

\bullet=[U^{-}\backslash\hat{\Omega}/\hat{B}]\subset\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}

is qc, dense and open.
By Proposition
4.1.6
, the functor
\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G})

is representable by a lft algebraic space.
The evaluation at
0
yields a morphism

\ev_{0}:\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}.

We define the Zastava space to be the fiber

(4.2.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}=\{f\in\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G})\,;\,f(0)=\bullet\}

The Zastava space is an open subfunctor of
\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G})
.
Hence it is representable by a
\mathbb{Z}
-lft algebraic space.
By Theorem
2.3.6
and Example
4.1.2
,
the Kashiwara flag scheme
B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}
satisfies the
assumptions of Lemma
4.1.1
.
Hence the functor

\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}),\quad\bullet=B^{-}\backslash\hat{\Omega}

is representable by a
\mathbb{Z}
-scheme as well as the subfunctor of based maps

(4.2.2)

\displaystyle\Map_{b}(\mathbb{P}^{1},B^{-}\backslash\hat{G})=\{f\in\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},B^{-}\backslash\hat{G})\,;\,f(0)=B^{-}\backslash B^{-}\}.

Corollary 4.2.2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The obvious map yields an isomorphism

\Map_{b}(\mathbb{P}^{1},B^{-}\backslash\hat{G})\cong\mathcal{Z}.

\mathrm{(b)}

The functor
\mathcal{Z}
is representable by a lft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

The following morphism is an
\hat{U}
-torsor

B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}\rightarrow[B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}/\hat{U}]=\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}

Since
\hat{U}
is split pro-unipotent, the proof of Proposition
3.2.6
yields

H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k},\hat{U})=\hat{U}(k)
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k},\hat{U})=0
for any ring
k
.
Thus the obvious morphism
\Map_{b}(\mathbb{P}^{1},B^{-}\backslash\hat{G})\to\mathcal{Z}
is surjective on
k
-points.
Since we considered based maps, it is an isomorphism.
Recall that
\Map_{b}(\mathbb{P}^{1},B^{-}\backslash\hat{G})
is representable by a scheme and
\mathcal{Z}

by a lft algebraic space by Proposition
4.1.6
.
Thus the functor
\mathcal{Z}
is representable by a lft scheme.
∎

Set

\overline{T}=\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[\Lambda_{+}])

and

(4.2.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{\Delta}=\prod\limits_{\alpha\in\Delta}\mathbb{A}^{1},\quad\mathbb{G}_{\Delta}=\prod\limits_{\alpha\in\Delta}\mathbb{G}_{m}

The scheme
\overline{T}
is isomorphic to

\mathbb{A}_{\Delta}
and contains
T
as an open dense subset isomorphic to
\mathbb{G}_{\Delta}
.
We set

\Div_{\overline{T}}=\Map_{\bullet}(\mathbb{P}^{1},[T\backslash\overline{T}]),\quad\bullet=[T\backslash T]

The set
\Div_{\overline{T}}
is the set of effective Cartier divisors on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
colored by
{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
.
We have

\Div_{\overline{T}}=\bigsqcup\limits_{\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}}\mathbb{P}^{\lambda},\quad\mathbb{P}^{\lambda}=\prod_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}^{\langle\omega_{i},\lambda\rangle}.

The map

\hat{G}/\hat{U}\rightarrow\overline{T}
such that

g\mapsto(\langle v_{i}^{\vee},g\cdot v_{i}\rangle)

factors through a map

\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}\cong[B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}/\hat{U}]\rightarrow[T\backslash\overline{T}]

It yields a morphism

\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\Div_{\overline{T}}.

For each
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
we define

\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda}=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\Div_{\overline{T}}}\mathbb{P}^{\lambda}.

Proposition 4.2.3
.

For any cocharacter
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
the
\mathbb{Z}
-scheme
\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda}
is lft, smooth and connected.

Proof.

Once smoothness is proved, it is enough to check the remaining claims over the generic fiber, hence over
\mathbb{C}
.
Thus the result follows from
[
16
, Prop. 2.25]
.
In loc. cit. it is assumed that
G
is symmetrizable. This is not needed as soon as the necessary results on thick flag schemes
for arbitrary KM groups are established, as explained at the beginning of §2 in loc. cit. This is done in §
2.3.3
.
Now we prove smoothness.
The argument is the same as in
[
16
, Prop. 2.24]
.
We briefly recall it because loc. cit. is over
\mathbb{C}
.
Using Corollary
4.2.2
, let
\bar{S}\rightarrow S
be a square zero extension of Artinian schemes,
and let

\sigma_{0}:\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}}\rightarrow B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}
be a based map that we must lift.
Since
B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}
is formally smooth,
the map
\sigma_{0}
lifts Zariski locally on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}}
.
The obstruction to lift
\sigma_{0}
globally lies in the cohomology group

H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}}\,,\,(\sigma_{0}^{*}T_{B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}})(-1)).

The tangent sheaf
T_{B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}}
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}
.
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
surjects to
T_{B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}}
at every point.
So we have a surjective morphism of sheaves

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}}}(-1)\otimes\mathfrak{g}\twoheadrightarrow(\sigma_{0}^{*}T_{B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}})(-1).

Hence, there is a surjection of the first cohomology groups.
Finally, we have

H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}}}(-1)\otimes\mathfrak{g})=H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\bar{S}}}(-1))\otimes\mathfrak{g}=0.

∎

4.2.3.
Zastavas and intersections of semi-infinite orbits

We consider the diagonal embedding

\{\infty\}\hookrightarrow\bigsqcup\limits_{\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}}\mathbb{P}^{\lambda}=\Div_{\overline{T}}.

We define the central fibers of
\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda}
to be the schemes

(4.2.4)

\displaystyle Z=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\Div_{\overline{T}}}\{\infty\},\quad Z^{\lambda}=Z\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda}.

Recall the functors
\Gr_{B}^{\lambda}
and
\Gr_{\hat{B}}^{\lambda}
introduced in (
3.2.3
).
For future use, we compare the intersection

S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu}
of the subfunctors of
\Gr_{G}
with the intersection
S^{\mathrm{f}}_{\mu}\cap T^{\mathrm{f}}_{\nu}

of the subfunctors of
\Gr_{G}^{\mathrm{f}}
introduced in (
3.2.9
).

Proposition 4.2.4
.

\mathrm{(a)}

Z_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{U^{-}})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

(S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}=(S^{\mathrm{f}}_{\mu}\cap T^{\mathrm{f}}_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.2.2
, the scheme
Z
is the moduli space of maps
\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}

such that
f|_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
factors through
\hat{U}
and
f(0)=B^{-}
.
Since the scheme
B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}
is separated,
the Beauville-Laszlo gluing applies to
B^{-}\backslash\hat{G}

by
[
69
, Cor. 4.4]
.
Hence, we can replace in the moduli problem above
\mathbb{P}^{1}
by
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}\llbracket s\rrbracket)
and use the variant of
Corollary
4.2.2
for
\mathbb{Z}\llbracket s\rrbracket
to go backwards.
Also, we have

G/B=\Spec(\mathbb{Z})\times_{\mathbb{B}G}\mathbb{B}B
.
Quotienting by the group
U^{-}
we deduce that

\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}=\mathbb{B}B\times_{\mathbb{B}G}\mathbb{B}U^{-}.

Recall the functors
\Gr
,
\Gr^{\prime}
introduced in §
3.1.0
-
3.1.5
.
This implies that

(4.2.5)

Z\cong(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{B}})^{\prime}\times_{(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{G}})^{\prime}}(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{U^{-}})^{\prime}

Next, we must remove the upperscript
(-)^{\prime}
, the
f
’s and pass from
\hat{G}
to
G
.
The scheme
Z
is locally of finite type. Thus it commutes to filtered colimits.
Also, the fiber product
\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{U^{-}}
is a prestack locally of finite type,
see Remark
5.2.4
, thus it commutes with filtered colimits.
Therefore, it is sufficient to check that they have the same points on reduced strictly henselian local rings.
We still have
\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{T}=(\Gr_{T}^{\mathrm{f}})^{\prime}
and
\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{B}}=(\Gr_{\hat{B}}^{\mathrm{f}})^{\prime}
.
Since
\Gr_{\hat{B}}^{\mathrm{f}}\rightarrow(\Gr_{\hat{G}}^{\mathrm{f}})^{\prime}
factors through
\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{G}}
, we first prove that:

((\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{U^{-}})^{\prime}\times_{(\Gr_{\hat{G}}^{\mathrm{f}})^{\prime}}\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{G}})(R)=(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{U^{-}})(R).

As
R
is strictly henselian, it amounts to prove that a
U^{-}
-torsor
E
over
R\llbracket s\rrbracket
whose induced
\hat{G}
-torsor is trivial over
R\llbracket s\rrbracket
, is trivial. It amounts to an
R\llbracket s\rrbracket
-point of
\hat{G}/U^{-}
, that lifts by
Proposition
2.3.4
and as
\Pic(R\llbracket s\rrbracket)=0
.
We thus obtain that

Z(R)=(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{B}}\times_{\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{G}}}\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{U^{-}})(R).

To pass to polynomial loops, we use Lemma
3.2.12
to get that
\Gr_{\hat{B}}=\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{B}}
and,
as
\Gr_{\hat{B}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}^{\mathrm{f}}
factors through
\Gr_{\hat{G}}
, we need to have

(4.2.6)

(\Gr_{\hat{G}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}^{\mathrm{f}}}\Gr_{U^{-}}^{\mathrm{f}})(R)=\Gr_{U^{-}}(R).

Since
\Gr_{U^{-}}^{\mathrm{f}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}^{\mathrm{f}}
is a monomorphism, by base change and Proposition
3.2.6
,
the map

\iota:\Gr_{U^{-}}\rightarrow\Gr_{\hat{G}}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}^{\mathrm{f}}}\Gr_{U^{-}}^{\mathrm{f}}

is fp locally closed. In particular, to get (
4.2.6
), it is thus sufficient to check that the
k
-points are the same for
k
algebraically closed, because
\iota
will induce an isomorphism on the reduced stacks.
Then, the assertion follows from Iwasawa decomposition.
Finally, using Lemma
3.2.15
, we obtain an element in
(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{U^{-}})(R)
as wished.
For the second assertion, by multiplying by
s^{\nu}
, we reduce to the case
\nu=0
.
Using (
4.2.5
), we then have a factorization

S_{\mu}\cap T_{0}\rightarrow S_{\mu}^{{\mathrm{f}}}\cap T_{0}^{{\mathrm{f}}}\rightarrow Z^{\mu}=(\Gr^{\mu,{\mathrm{f}}}_{\hat{B}})^{\prime}\times_{(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\hat{G}})^{\prime}}(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{U^{-}})^{\prime}

and the previous assertion with Lemma
3.2.3

gives that the composite is an isomorphism on the reduced stacks and as all the maps are monomorphisms, this concludes.
∎

We deduce the following which strengthens Proposition
3.2.11
and

[
20
, Thm. 1.9]
,
[
49
, Thm. 5.6]
.

Proposition 4.2.5
.

For each
\mu,\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
the intersection
(S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

If
S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset
then
\mu\geq\nu
by Proposition
3.2.11
.
The multiplication by
s^{\nu}
gives an isomorphism

S_{\mu-\nu}\cap T_{0}\cong S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu}.

We can thus assume that
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
\nu=0
.
By Lemma
3.2.3
the maps
S_{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}
and
T_{0}\rightarrow\Gr_{B^{-}}
are fp and closed.
Thus the following map is also fp closed

(4.2.7)

\displaystyle S_{\mu}\cap T_{0}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{U^{-}}.

By Lemma
4.2.4
we have

(\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{U^{-}})_{\mathrm{red}}=(Z^{\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}
.
From Proposition
4.2.3
and (
4.2.4
), we deduce that
Z^{\mu}
is a scheme.
Thus
(S_{\mu}\cap T_{0})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

4.3.
Finite dimensional Mirkovic-Vilonen cycles

The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 4.3.1
.

Assume that
G
is symmetrizable. Let
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The intersection
(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
of relative dimension
\langle\rho,\lambda-\nu\rangle
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The number of irreducible components of
\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu}
of maximal dimension is
\dim L(\lambda)_{\nu}
.

The proof of Theorem
4.3.1
involves a counting argument and combinatorics involved need symmetrizability. For the dimension part,
there is an other approach, using the dimension formula of Zastavas
[
16
, Conj. 2.27, Cor. 2.28]
, but the conjecture in loc. cit. is only
settled in the affine case.

4.3.1.
Preliminary lemmas

To prove the theorem we need more material.
Recall the cover of
\Gr_{\lambda}
by the open subsets
\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
introduced in (
3.4.8
).

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\nu\leq\lambda
.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\nu\leq w\lambda
.

\mathrm{(c)}

(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Composing the Cartan involution with the inverse, we get an anti-automorphism
\theta
of the group

G
which fixes the set
{\check{\Lambda}}

and switches the positive and negative unipotent subgroups.
Claim (a) follows by applying
\theta
to
[
20
, Thm. 1.9]
.

To prove (b) note that
if
\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset
then
(wS_{\lambda})\cap T_{\nu}\neq\emptyset.

Since
T_{\nu}
is preserved by left translations by elements of the torus
T
and since
wS_{\lambda}
is the attractor of
the element
[s^{w\lambda}]
in the affine Grassmannian for the action of the cocharacter
2w{\check{\rho}}
of
T
,
by the Iwasawa decomposition, we deduce that
for each closed point
x
in
(wS_{\lambda})\cap T_{\nu}
the limit of
2w{\check{\rho}}(z)\cdot x
as
z\to 0
is equal to
[s^{w\lambda}]

and belongs to the closure of
T_{\nu}
.
By Proposition
3.2.6
, we deduce that
w\lambda\geqslant\nu
.

Now, we concentrate on (c).
The functor
\Gr_{\lambda}
is covered by the open subsets
\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
with
w\in W
.
The set
\{w\lambda\,;\,w\in W\,,\,w\lambda\geqslant\nu\}
is finite.
Hence it is enough to prove that

(\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
whenever it is non empty.
To do this, we will prove that
((wS_{\lambda})\cap T_{\nu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a ft scheme.
If
w=1
this follows from Proposition
4.2.5
. The general case is proved in a similar way.
More precisely, we have an obvious isomorphism
(wS_{\lambda})\cap T_{\nu}\cong(wS_{\lambda-w^{-1}\nu})\cap T_{0}.

Hence, according to the discussion above, we can assume that
w\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
\nu=0
.
Further, we identify

(wS_{\lambda})\cap T_{0}\cong S_{\lambda}\cap(w^{-1}T_{0})

and we observe as in (
4.2.7
) that
the obvious inclusion
w^{-1}(U^{-})\subset G
yields a closed immersion

(4.3.1)

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}\cap(w^{-1}T_{0})\hookrightarrow\Gr^{\lambda}_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{w^{-1}(U^{-})}.

The automorphism of
G
given by the left translation by
w
yields a stack isomorphism

[w^{-1}(U^{-})\backslash G/B]\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}.

In particular, the substack

\bullet=[w^{-1}(U^{-})\backslash w^{-1}\Omega]\subset[w^{-1}(U^{-})\backslash G/B]\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}

is qc, dense and open.
The proof of Lemma
4.2.4
yields an isomorphism

\displaystyle(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{w^{-1}(U^{-})})_{\mathrm{red}}\cong Z_{\mathrm{red}}

The map

\hat{G}/\hat{U}\rightarrow\overline{T}
such that

g\mapsto(\langle v_{i}^{\vee},wg\cdot v_{i}\rangle)

factors through a map

\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{G}\cong[w^{-1}(B^{-})\backslash\hat{G}/\hat{U}]\rightarrow[T\backslash\overline{T}]

which yields a morphism

Z\to\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\Div_{\overline{T}}.

Recall that
w\lambda
is dominant.
Let
Z^{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\lambda}
denote the base changes
of
Z
,
\mathcal{Z}
from
\Div(\overline{T})
to
\mathbb{P}{Z}^{w\lambda}
as above.
We have an isomorphism

\displaystyle(\Gr_{B}^{\lambda}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{w^{-1}(U^{-})})_{\mathrm{red}}\cong Z_{\mathrm{red}}^{\lambda}

Further
Z^{\lambda}
is of ft over
\mathbb{Z}
by the proof of Propositon
4.2.3
.
Hence the intersection
(S_{\lambda}\cap(w^{-1}T_{0}))_{\mathrm{red}}
is also of ft.

∎

4.3.2.
Hall-Littlewood functions

We first recall some combinatorics following
[
89
]
.
We assume in this section that
G
is symmetrizable.
Set

\mathcal{E}_{t}=\Big\{f=\sum\limits_{\mu\in\Lambda}c_{\mu}(t)\,e^{\mu}\,;\,c_{\mu}(t)\in\mathbb{C}\llbracket t\rrbracket\Big\}

where
c_{\mu}(t)=0
outside the union of a finite number of sets of the form

D(\omega)=\{\nu\in\Lambda\,;\,\nu\leq\omega\}.

The
e^{\mu}
are formal exponentials with
e^{0}=1
and
e^{\mu+\nu}=e^{\mu}e^{\nu}
.
Consider the partially defined operator

J=\sum\limits_{w\in W}(-1)^{w}w\quad\text{on}\quad\mathcal{E}_{t}.

If the stabilizer
W_{\mu}
of
\mu
in
W
is finite then the infinite sum

J(e^{\mu})=\sum_{w\in W}(-1)^{\ell(w)}e^{w\mu}

is well-defined in
\mathcal{E}_{t}

but
J(f)
is not in general. For
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
, we set

f_{\lambda}=e^{\lambda+\rho}\prod\limits_{\alpha\in\Delta_{+}}(1-te^{-\alpha})^{m_{\alpha}}\in\mathcal{E}_{t}

with
m_{\alpha}
is the root multiplicity of
\alpha
.
By
[
89
, Prop. 1]
, the infinite sum
J(f_{\lambda})
is well-defined in
\mathcal{E}_{t}
.
Let
W_{\lambda}(t)
be the formal series in
\mathbb{C}\llbracket t\rrbracket
given by

W_{\lambda}(t)=\sum\limits_{\sigma\in W_{\lambda}}t^{\ell(\sigma)}.

The series
W_{\lambda}(t)
is invertible, because
W_{\lambda}(0)=1
.
We define the
Hall-Littlewood function
to be

(4.3.2)

P_{\lambda}(t)=W_{\lambda}(t)^{-1}J(e^{\rho})^{-1}J(f_{\lambda}).

Note that the Weyl-Kac character formula yields

J(e^{\rho})^{-1}=e^{-\rho}\prod\limits_{\alpha\in R^{+}}(1-e^{-\alpha})^{-m_{\alpha}},

where we interpret

(1-e^{-\alpha})^{-1}=1+e^{-\alpha}+e^{-2\alpha}+\dots.

Thus, we have

P_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}.

By
[
89
, (3.1)]
, there are formal series
c_{\lambda\mu}\in\mathbb{C}\llbracket t\rrbracket
such that

(4.3.3)

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(t)=\sum\limits_{\mu\in\Lambda^{+}}c_{\lambda\mu}(t)\chi_{\mu}.

We need an explicit description of the coefficients
c_{\lambda\mu}(t)
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be the set of all finite multisets of positive roots such that each
\alpha\in R^{+}
occurs at most
m_{\alpha}
times.
By
[
89
, (3.2)]
, we have

(4.3.4)

\displaystyle c_{\lambda\mu}(t)=W_{\lambda}(t)^{-1}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{w\in W}(-1)^{\ell(w)}(-t)^{\sharp A},

where the sum runs over all
w\in W
and all elements
A\in\mathcal{A}

such that

w\big(\lambda+\rho-\sum_{\alpha\in A}\alpha\big)=\mu+\rho.

We have the following properties.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

\mathrm{(a)}

c_{\lambda\mu}(t)\in\mathbb{Z}[t]
and when non-zero
\mu\leq\lambda
.

\mathrm{(b)}

c_{\lambda\lambda}(t)=1
.

\mathrm{(c)}

\mu<\lambda\Rightarrow c_{\lambda\mu}(0)=0
. In particular,
P_{\lambda}(0)=\chi_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

The claim (a), (b) are proved in
[
89
, §3.1, 7.1]
.
For Part (c), we have
W_{\lambda}(0)=1
. Thus, in (
4.3.4
) the only contributing
A
if
t=0

is
A=\emptyset
. This forces
w(\lambda+\rho)=\mu+\rho
. As
\mu
is dominant, we get
\lambda=\mu
.
∎

4.3.3.
Affine MacDonald formula

We still assume that
G
is symmetrizable.
Since the references we use work over
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(t)\!)
rather that
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,t^{-1}]
, we must check that
the countings over both rings give the same answer.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

For any
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
, we have
(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu})(\mathbb{F}_{q})=(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\lambda}\cap T^{\mathrm{f}}_{\nu})(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.2.11
, we have a finite stratification of
\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu}

by triple intersections
\Gr_{\lambda}\cap S_{\mu}\cap T_{\nu}
for
\lambda\geq\mu\geq\nu
.
By Lemma
4.2.4
, we know that

T_{\nu}\cap S_{\mu}=T^{\mathrm{f}}_{\nu}\cap S^{\mathrm{f}}_{\mu}.

By (
3.3.4
) the triple intersections match (on both sides intersecting with
Gr_{\lambda}
is a condition on poles using Plücker description).
So the strata are in bijection and we get an equality

(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu})(\mathbb{F}_{q})=(\Gr^{\mathrm{f}}_{\lambda}\cap T^{\mathrm{f}}_{\nu})(\mathbb{F}_{q}).

∎

Following
[
89
, (4.1)]
we consider the series
\Delta(t)\in\mathcal{E}_{t}
given by

\Delta(t)=\prod\limits_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\left(\frac{1-te^{-\alpha}}{1-e^{-\alpha}}\right)^{m_{\alpha}}.

By
[
21
, §7.3]
in the affine untwisted case,
and
[
2
, Thm. 7.3]
in the symmetrizable one, for each dominant cocharacter
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
the series

H_{\lambda}(t)=W_{\lambda}(t)^{-1}\sum_{w\in W}w(\Delta)\penalty\ e^{w\lambda}

and
H_{0}^{-1}
are well-defined in
\mathcal{E}_{t}
and the coefficients lie in
\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]
.
We set

(4.3.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Sat(1_{G_{\lambda}(\mathbb{F}_{q})})&=q^{\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle}\frac{H_{\lambda}(q^{-1})}{H_{0}(q^{-1})}.\end{split}

We now compare
H_{\lambda}
with the Hall-Littlewood function
P_{\lambda}
.
By
[
52
, p. 172, §10.2.2]
, for any
w\in W
we have

wJ(e^{\rho})=(-1)^{\ell(w)}J(e^{\rho}).

We deduce that

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(t)

\displaystyle=W_{\lambda}(t)^{-1}\sum_{w}(-1)^{\ell(w)}e^{w(\lambda+\rho)}J(e^{\rho})^{-1}\prod\limits_{\alpha\in R^{+}}(1-te^{-w\alpha})^{m_{\alpha}},

\displaystyle=W_{\lambda}(t)^{-1}\sum_{w}w\Big(e^{\lambda+\rho}J(e^{\rho})^{-1}\prod\limits_{\alpha\in R^{+}}(1-te^{-w\alpha})^{m_{\alpha}}\Big),

\displaystyle=W_{\lambda}(t)^{-1}\sum_{w}w\Big(e^{\lambda}\prod\limits_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\frac{(1-te^{-w\alpha})^{m_{\alpha}}}{(1-e^{-\alpha})^{m_{\alpha}}}\Big),

\displaystyle=H_{\lambda}(t).

In particular, we have

(4.3.6)

\displaystyle\Sat(1_{G_{\lambda}(\mathbb{F}_{q})})=q^{\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle}\frac{P_{\lambda}(q^{-1})}{P_{0}(q^{-1})}.

We need an alternative formula for
\Sat(1_{G_{\lambda}(\mathbb{F}_{q})})
that involves the affine MV cycles.
For an arbitrary KM group (not necessarily symmetrizable), the Satake transform involves Hecke paths.
By
[
2
, §2.8.(1)]
, see also
[
40
, prop. 5.2]
, we have

\Sat(1_{G_{\lambda}(\mathbb{F}_{q})})=\sum\limits_{\mu\leq\lambda}n_{\lambda}(\mu)q^{\langle\rho,\mu\rangle}e^{\mu},

where
n_{\lambda}(\mu)=\sum_{\pi}S_{\infty}(\pi,\mu)
is the sum over all Hecke paths
\pi
of type
\lambda
from 0 to
\mu

of some integers
S_{\infty}(\pi,\mu)
that involve line segments in the masure.
The only thing that matters for us is that Muthiah in
[
72
, §4.5 and §4.5.5]
re-interprets this sum in the following way

(4.3.7)

\displaystyle\Sat(1_{G_{\lambda}(\mathbb{F}_{q})})=\sum\limits_{\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}}\sharp(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\mu})_{{}_{GR}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})q^{\langle\rho,\mu\rangle}e^{\mu}

Note that loc. cit. is written is the untwisted affine case, but for §4, this restriction is not relevant.
The subscript
GR
is for Gaussent-Rousseau and means that the numbers are computed inside
G(\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(t)\!))

where double orbits are with respect to the group
G_{x}
in §
2.5.2
.
By Lemma
4.3.4
and Proposition
2.5.3
, we have

(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\mu})_{{}_{GR}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\mu})(\mathbb{F}_{q}).

Thus, in (
4.3.7
) we can freely replace the GR’s sets by ours.

4.3.4.
Proof of Theorem
4.3.1

We can now prove the theorem.
By Lemma
4.3.2
the intersection
(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is empty if
\lambda\not\geqslant\mu
, and, else,
it is ft scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
.
We must check that the dimension of
(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is
\langle\rho,\lambda-\mu\rangle
and that
the number of irreducible components of maximal dimension is
\dim L(\lambda)_{\mu}
.
By (
4.3.3
), Lemma
4.3.3
, (
4.3.6
) and (
4.3.7
) we have

\sharp(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu})(\mathbb{F}_{q})q^{\langle\rho,\nu-\lambda\rangle}=\frac{1}{P_{0}(q^{-1})}\big(\dim L(\lambda)_{\nu}+\sum_{\mu<\lambda}c_{\lambda\mu}(q^{-1})\dim L(\mu)_{\nu}\big)

and the coefficient
c_{\lambda\mu}(q^{-1})
is a polynomial in
q^{-1}
without constant term.
Since
P_{0}(q^{-1})\to 1
as
q\to\infty
, we deduce that

\lim_{q\to\infty}\sharp(\Gr_{\lambda}\cap T_{\nu})(\mathbb{F}_{q})q^{\langle\rho,\nu-\lambda\rangle}=\dim L(\lambda)_{\nu}

from which the theorem follows.

4.4.
Finite codimensional Mirkovic-Vilonen cycles

The finite codimensional Mirkovic-Vilonen cycles satisfy the following analogue of Lemma
4.3.2
.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

Let
w\in W
,
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
\Gr_{\lambda}\cap S_{\mu}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\mu\leq\lambda
.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap S_{\mu}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\mu\geq w\lambda
.

\mathrm{(c)}

The formal analogues of
\mathrm{(a)}
and
\mathrm{(b)}
hold.

Proof.

The proof is the same as for Lemma
4.3.2
.
Let us recall the argument for (b).
If

\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap S_{\mu}\neq\emptyset
then
(wS_{\lambda})\cap S_{\mu}\neq\emptyset.

Since
S_{\mu}
is preserved by left translations by elements of the torus
T
and since
wS_{\lambda}
is the attractor of
the element
[s^{w\lambda}]
in the affine Grassmannian
\Gr
, for the action of the cocharacter
2w{\check{\rho}}
of
T
, we deduce that
for each closed point
x
in
(wS_{\lambda})\cap S_{\mu}
the limit of
2w{\check{\rho}}(z)\cdot x
as
z\to 0
is equal to
[s^{w\lambda}]

and belongs to the closure of
S_{\mu}
, hence
\mu\geqslant w\lambda
by Proposition
3.2.6
.
∎

Following §
2.4.0
, we define

U_{w}=U\cap w(U),\quad\hat{U}_{w}=\hat{U}\cap w(\hat{U}),\quad U^{w}=U\cap w(U^{-}),\quad\hat{U}^{w}=\hat{U}\cap w(U^{-}).

Proposition
2.4.2
yields the following scheme and ind-scheme isomorphisms

(4.4.1)

\displaystyle\hat{U}\cong\hat{U}_{w}\times\hat{U}^{w},\quad U\cong U_{w}\times U^{w},\quad U^{w}\cong U/U_{w}\cong\hat{U}^{w}\cong\hat{U}/\hat{U}_{w}.

We abbreviate
\hat{U}_{w,\mathcal{O}}=(\hat{U}_{w})_{\mathcal{O}}
and
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}^{w}=(\hat{U}^{w})_{\mathcal{O}}
.
Define
U_{w,\mathcal{O}}
and
U_{\mathcal{O}}^{w}
similarly.
From (
4.4.1
) we deduce the following ind-scheme isomorphisms

(4.4.2)

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\cong\hat{U}_{w,\mathcal{O}}\times\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}^{w},\quad U_{\mathcal{O}}\cong U_{w,\mathcal{O}}\times U_{\mathcal{O}}^{w},\quad U_{\mathcal{O}}^{w}\cong\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}^{w}.

Recall the notation
\hat{U}^{-}_{\lambda}
and
\hat{U}_{\lambda}
in (
3.4.3
).
Let
H\subset w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})
be the closed subgroup given by

H=w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cap\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}=\hat{U}_{w,\mathcal{O}}\cap w\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}).

Note that
H=w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cap\hat{U}_{K}
because
w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\subset\hat{G}_{\mathcal{O}}
, and that

w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})/H\cong w(\hat{U}^{w^{-1}}_{\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}})).

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Let
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
and
w\in W
.

\mathrm{(a)}

There is a fp locally closed embedding
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}\subset\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The
w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})
-action on
\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
by left translation on
[s^{w\lambda}]

yields an isomorphism
w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cong\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

The
H
-action on
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}
by left translation yields an isomorphism

H\cong\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}
.

\mathrm{(d)}

w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})/H\cong\mathbb{A}^{\langle\rho,\lambda-w\lambda\rangle}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.4.3
, to prove (a) we must check that the image of
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}

by the map
\phi
in (
3.4.6
) is contained
w\hat{U}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}^{-}/P_{\lambda}^{-}
.
The intersection
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}
is the attractor locus of
[s^{w\lambda}]
in
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}

for the cocharacter
2{\check{\rho}}
of the torus
T
.
Since the map
\phi
commutes with the
T
-action, we deduce that

\phi(\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda})
is contained in the attractor locus
\hat{U}_{\lambda}wP_{\lambda}^{-}/P_{\lambda}^{-}

of the closed point
w
in
\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}

for the cocharacter
2{\check{\rho}}
, from which the inclusion

\phi(\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda})\subset w\hat{U}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}^{-}/P_{\lambda}^{-}
follows, because

\hat{U}_{\lambda}wP_{\lambda}^{-}/P_{\lambda}^{-}\subset w\hat{U}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}^{-}/P_{\lambda}^{-}
.
In particular, we get
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}=\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}
.
Thus, we have a fp locally closed embedding
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}\subset\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}

by base change and Proposition
3.2.6
.
Part (b) follows from Lemmas
3.4.1
,
3.4.2
and Proposition
3.4.3
.
Part (c) is obvious, because

\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}=\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}=(w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cdot[s^{w\lambda}])\cap(\hat{U}_{K}\cdot[s^{w\lambda}])=(w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cap\hat{U}_{K})\cdot[s^{w\lambda}]\cong H,

where the third equality follows from the fact that

(w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cdot[s^{w\lambda}])\cap(\hat{U}_{K}\cdot[s^{w\lambda}])
is the attractor locus of
[s^{w\lambda}]
for the cocharacter
2{\check{\rho}}
and

w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cdot[s^{w\lambda}]\cong w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})
by (b), hence the intersection is identified with
the attractor locus of
1
in
w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})
for the cocharacter
2{\check{\rho}}
, which is
w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cap\hat{U}_{K}
.
Parts (b) and (c) identify the inclusion
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}\subset\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}

with the inclusion
H\subset w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})
, which is closed, thus parts (a), (b) and (c) are proved.
For part (d), note that

w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})\cong\prod_{\alpha\in w(R^{+})}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha,\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{w\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha,\pi^{-}}),\quad H\cong\big(\prod_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha,\mathcal{O}}\big)\cap w(\hat{U}_{\lambda}).

Hence, we have

w(\hat{U}_{\lambda})/H\cong\prod_{\alpha\in w(R^{+})\cap R^{-}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha,\mathcal{O}}\cap\Ad_{s^{w\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha,\pi^{-}})\cong\prod_{(\alpha,n)\in\Gamma}U_{\alpha,n}

where
\Gamma
is the following set

\Gamma=\{(\alpha,n)\in R^{+}\cap w^{-1}(R^{-})\times\mathbb{N}\,;\,n<\langle\alpha,\lambda\rangle\}

and, for each
\alpha\in R_{{\mathrm{re}}}^{+}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

(4.4.3)

\displaystyle U_{\alpha,n}=x_{\alpha}(s^{n}\mathbb{G}_{a}).

Further, we have

\displaystyle\sharp\Gamma

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in w(R^{+})\cap R^{-}}\langle\alpha,w\lambda\rangle=\langle\!\!\!\!\sum_{\alpha\in w(R^{+})\cap R^{-}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\alpha\,,\,w\lambda\rangle=\langle w\rho-\rho\,,\,w\lambda\rangle=\langle\rho\,,\,\lambda-w\lambda\rangle

∎

Since
H\subset\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}
, the
H
-action on
\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
preserves
the intersection
\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap\hat{S}_{\mu}
for each
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}

with
w\lambda\leq\mu\leq\lambda
.
We consider the quotient stacks
\hat{X}_{\mu}
and
\hat{X}
given by

(4.4.4)

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{\mu}=[\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap\hat{S}_{\mu}/H]\subset\hat{X}=[\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}/H].

By the above, the stack
\hat{X}
is representable by the affine space

\mathbb{A}^{\langle\rho,\lambda-w\lambda\rangle}
, while
\hat{X}_{\mu}
is a locally closed susbscheme and

\hat{X}_{w\lambda}\cong\Spec(\mathbb{Z}).

For a future use, let us record the following well-known fact.

Lemma 4.4.3
.

Let
X_{1}
be a separated scheme of ft, and
X_{0}\subset X_{1}
a closed subscheme such that
X_{1}\setminus X_{0}
is affine.
Let
Z_{1}\subset X_{1}
be an irreducible component such that
Z_{1}\not\subset X_{0}
and
Z_{1}\cap X_{0}\neq\emptyset
.
Let
Z_{0}\subset Z_{1}\cap X_{0}
be an irreducible component.
Then
\dim Z_{1}=\dim Z_{0}+1
.
∎

We can now prove the following.

Lemma 4.4.4
.

For each
w\lambda\leq\mu\leq\lambda
,
the subscheme
\hat{X}_{\mu}\subseteq\hat{X}

is equidimensional of codimension
\langle\rho,\lambda-\mu\rangle
.

Proof.

Let

\hat{X}_{d}=\bigsqcup_{\langle\rho,\mu\rangle\leqslant d}\hat{X}_{\mu}
.
We have a sequence of closed embeddings of schemes

\Spec(\mathbb{Z})=\hat{X}_{\langle\rho,w\lambda\rangle}\subseteq\hat{X}_{1+\langle\rho,w\lambda\rangle}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\hat{X}_{\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle}=\mathbb{A}^{\langle\rho,\lambda-w\lambda\rangle}

where the last equality follows from Lemma
4.4.1
(a) and the Iwasawa decomposition.
For all
d
the open subset

\hat{X}_{1+d}\setminus\hat{X}_{d}=\bigsqcup_{\langle\rho,\mu\rangle=1+d}\hat{X}_{\mu}

is affine by Theorem
3.2.13
.
For any irreducible component
Z_{d}\subset\hat{X}_{d}
, either
Z_{d}
is still an irreducible component of
\hat{X}_{d+1}
, or there is
an irreducible component
Z_{d+1}
containing strictly
Z_{d}
, in which case Lemma
4.4.3
applies.
Hence, in both cases, by descending induction we deduce that the codimension of
Z_{d}
in
\mathbb{A}^{\langle\rho,\lambda-w\lambda\rangle}
is smaller than

\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle-d
.

Now, we prove that the codimension of
Z_{d}
is greater than
\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle-d
by ascending induction.
If
d=\langle\rho,w\lambda\rangle
the claim is obvious.
Assume that
d>\langle\rho,w\lambda\rangle
and fix any irreducible component
Z_{d}\subset\hat{X}_{d}
.
If
Z_{d}\subset\hat{X}_{d-1}
we are done by induction.
Else, we claim that
Z_{d}\cap\hat{X}_{d-1}\neq\emptyset
, and we conclude by applying Lemma
4.4.3
.

To prove the claim we consider the map
\phi
in (
3.4.6
).
By Proposition
3.4.3
we have
\phi(\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ})=w\hat{U}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}^{-}
in
\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}
.
Let
Z^{\prime}_{d}
be the inverse image of
Z_{d}
by the obvious projection

\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\to\hat{X}_{\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle}
.
Since
Z^{\prime}_{d}
is preserved by the
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}
-action, we deduce that
\phi(Z^{\prime}_{d})
is a union of
\hat{U}
-orbits
and is contained in the big cell
w\hat{U}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}^{-}
.
The big cell contains a unique
\hat{U}
-orbit.
Hence
\phi(Z^{\prime}_{d})=\hat{U}wP_{\lambda}^{-}
.
Further, by definition of the map
\phi
, the image
\phi(x)
of any point
x\in\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}

is the limit
t\cdot x
as
t\to 0
for the loop action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Such a limit is indeed well defined by Lemma
3.4.2
and the open cover of
\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}

by the
\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
’s.
Since
Z^{\prime}_{d}
is closed, this implies that the image of

\phi(Z^{\prime}_{d})
by the obvious immersion
\hat{G}/P_{\lambda}^{-}\to\widehat{\Gr}_{\lambda}
, which is a section of
\phi
,
is contained in
Z^{\prime}_{d}
.
Since
\hat{U}w[s^{\lambda}]\subset\hat{S}_{w\lambda}
, we deduce that
Z^{\prime}_{d}\cap\hat{S}_{w\lambda}\neq\emptyset
.
Hence
Z_{d}\cap\hat{X}_{\langle\rho,w\lambda\rangle}\neq\emptyset
, and, a fortiori,
we have
Z_{d}\cap\hat{X}_{d-1}\neq\emptyset
, proving the claim and the lemma.
∎

Now, we consider the non formal setting.
Lemma
3.2.15
and Proposition
3.4.4
yield the isomorphisms

(4.4.5)

\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}\cong\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ},\quad(\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap\hat{S}_{\mu})\times_{\Gr_{\hat{G}}}\Gr_{G}\cong\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cap S_{\mu}

Recall the notation
\hat{U}_{\lambda}
and
U_{\lambda}
in (
3.4.3
), (
3.4.10
). Lemma
3.4.2
and Proposition
3.4.5
yield

\widehat{\Gr}_{w\lambda}^{\circ}\cong[w\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}/w\hat{U}_{\lambda}],\quad(\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ})_{\mathrm{red}}\cong[wU_{\mathcal{O}}/wU_{\lambda}]_{\mathrm{red}}.

By (
4.4.2
), we have the following ind-scheme isomorphisms

\displaystyle wU_{\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\cong w(U_{w^{-1},\mathcal{O}}\times\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}^{w^{-1}})

\displaystyle\cong(U_{\mathcal{O}}\cap wU_{\mathcal{O}})\times w(\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}^{w^{-1}})

\displaystyle\cong(U_{\mathcal{O}}\cap wU_{\mathcal{O}})\times(U_{\mathcal{O}}^{-}\cap w\hat{U}_{\lambda})\times(U_{\mathcal{O}}^{-}\cap w\hat{U}_{\mathcal{O}}\cap w\Ad_{s^{\lambda}}(\hat{U}_{\pi^{-}}))

\displaystyle\cong(U_{\mathcal{O}}\cap wU_{\mathcal{O}})\times(U_{\mathcal{O}}^{-}\cap w\hat{U}_{\lambda})\times\hat{X}

Let
\hat{X}
and
\hat{X}_{\mu}
be as in (
4.4.4
).
We consider the group ind-schemes of ind-ft
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
given by

H_{1}=wU_{\lambda},\quad H_{2}=(U_{\mathcal{O}}\cap wU_{\mathcal{O}})\times(U_{\mathcal{O}}^{-}\cap w\hat{U}_{\lambda}).

Let
p_{1}:wU_{\mathcal{O}}\to\Gr_{w\lambda}^{\circ}
and
p_{2}:wU_{\mathcal{O}}\to\hat{X}
be the quotients by the groups
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
.
We have the following commutative diagram with Cartesian squares, that we will need in the proof of Theorem
7.6.1

(4.4.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Note that the group ind-schemes

H_{1}
and
H_{2}
have a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant contracting presentation,
as in Lemma
5.2.11
below.
The vertical maps
i_{0}
,
i_{1}
,
i_{2}
are the obvious locally closed immersions.

5.
Perverse sheaves on the affine Grassmannian of a KM group

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field.
In this section all prestacks are defined over the field
k
.

5.1.
Category of
\ell
-adic sheaves

This section is a reminder on the category of sheaves on
\infty
-stacks.
The main reference is
[
10
]
.
Let
\ell
be a prime different from the characteristic of
k
.

5.1.0.
Definition of the category of
\ell
-adic sheaves

For an algebraic space
Y
of ft, we have
an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}_{c}(Y)=\mathcal{D}^{b}_{c}(Y,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
whose homotopical category is the derived category

D^{b}_{c}(Y,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
of bounded complexes of sheaves with constructible cohomology,
see
[
58
]
,
[
59
]
or
[
35
]
.
We will use the formalism of six functors for the
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{D}_{c}
.
This yields a functor

(5.1.1)

\mathcal{D}_{c}:(\AlgSp^{\mathrm{ft}}_{k})^{{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\StCat

which to each morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
associates
f^{!}:\mathcal{D}_{c}(Y)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{c}(X)
.
We define the functor

(5.1.2)

\mathcal{D}:(\AlgSp^{\mathrm{ft}}_{k})^{{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\PrCat

to be
\mathcal{D}=\Ind\circ\mathcal{D}_{c}
.
The embedding

\iota:\Aff_{k}\hookrightarrow\AlgSp_{k}
yields an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\iota^{*}:\Sh(\Aff_{k})\rightarrow\Sh(\AlgSp_{k}).

Thus, to construct the
\infty
-category
\Stk_{k}
we could use
the category
\AlgSp_{k}
instead of the category
\Aff_{k}
as in (
1.8.1
).
The same applies to the category
\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
of qcqs algebraic spaces.
By doing left Kan extensions, the functors
\mathcal{D}_{c}
and
\mathcal{D}
yield the functors

(5.1.3)

\mathcal{D}_{c}:(\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\StCat,\quad\mathcal{D}:(\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\PrCat

By doing right Kan extension we get the functors

(5.1.4)

\mathcal{D}_{c}:\PreSh(\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})\rightarrow\StCat,\quad\mathcal{D}:\PreSh(\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})\rightarrow\PrCat

Both Kan extensions exist by
[
60
, Thm. 5.1.5.6]
,
because the category
\PrCat
is bicomplete, see, e.g.,
[
78
, §2.2]
.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}
be either
\mathcal{D}
or
\mathcal{D}_{c}
.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

The functor
\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}
is a sheaf for the étale topology, i.e., it factors through

\Sh(\AlgSp_{k})
via the sheafifying functor.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
be the semi-simplicial category, whose objects are the
[m]
’s with
m\in\mathbb{N}

and whose morphisms
[m]\to[n]
are the injective monotone maps
\{0,\dots,m\}\to\{0,\dots,n\}
.
To any fp étale covering of qcqs algebraic spaces

\pi:X\rightarrow Y
we associate a semi-simplicial algebraic space
(X^{[n]})

such that

(5.1.5)

\displaystyle\pi^{[n]}:X^{[n]}=X\times_{Y}\dots\times_{Y}X\to Y

is the obvious projection and
X^{[n]}
is the
(n+1)
-th fiber power of
X
over
Y
.
We must prove that the functor

(5.1.6)

\displaystyle\underline{\pi}^{!}:\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(Y)\to\lim_{t^{!}}\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(X^{[n]})

given by the system of functors
(\pi^{[n]})^{!}

is an equivalence.
Here
[n]
runs over the objects of the category
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}
opposite to
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
.
If the map
\pi
has a section the claim is standard.
The functor
\underline{\pi}^{!}
has a right adjoint
\underline{\pi}_{*}
.
Indeed, the map
\pi^{[n]}
is fp étale.
So
(\pi^{[n]})^{!}
has a right adjoint
(\pi^{[n]})_{*}
.
Therefore
\underline{\pi}^{!}
has a right adjoint
\underline{\pi}_{*}
, which sends the object

\underline{K}=(K_{n})
in
\lim_{[n]}\mathcal{D}(X^{[n]})
to the limit
\lim_{n}(\pi^{[n]})_{*}(K_{n})
.

We claim that the unit
K\to\underline{\pi}_{*}\underline{\pi}^{!}K
is an isomorphism, i.e., the map

K\to\lim_{[n]}(\pi^{[n]})_{*}(\pi^{[n]})^{!}(K)
is an isomorphism. Since
\pi^{!}
is faithful, it suffices to check the isomorphism after we
apply
\pi^{!}
. Since
\pi^{!}
commutes with limits, because it has a left adjoint
\pi_{!}
, and with
(\pi^{[n]})_{*}
, by
[
10
, Prop. 5.2.7.(a)]
,
we are reduced to the corresponding assertion for the projection
\pi^{+}:X\times_{Y}X\to X
.
Since it has a section, which is the diagonal
X\to X\times_{Y}X
,
we are done.

Finally, we claim that the counit
\underline{\pi}^{!}\underline{\pi}_{*}(\underline{K})\to\underline{K}
is an isomorphism. It suffices to show that
the map
\pi^{!}(\lim_{[n]}(\pi^{[n]})_{*}(K_{n}))\to K_{0}
is an isomorphism. As above, the assertion follows from the commutativity of

\pi^{!}
commutes with limits and
(\pi^{[n]})_{*}
.
The claim is proved for
\mathcal{D}
. To prove it for
\mathcal{D}_{c}
, note that the functor
\pi^{!}
preserves
\mathcal{D}_{c}
.
∎

Proposition
5.1.1
allows us to see
\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}
as a functor on
\Stk_{k}
such that

\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{X})=\lim\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(X_{a})

where the limit runs over all schemes
i_{a}:X_{a}\to\mathcal{X}
over
\mathcal{X}
.
Unwinding the definition, an object
\mathcal{E}
in
\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{X})
is the datum of a sheaf
\mathcal{E}_{i_{a}}
in
\mathcal{D}(X_{a})
for each
i_{a}
as above,
with an equivalence of sheaves
\mathcal{E}_{f(i_{a})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}f^{!}\mathcal{E}_{i_{a}}
for each morphism of schemes
f
,
satisfying a homotopy-coherent system of compatibilities.

For any stack
\mathcal{X}\in\Stk_{k}
the inclusion
\mathcal{D}_{c}(\mathcal{X})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
yields a functor

\Ind(\mathcal{D}_{c}(\mathcal{X}))\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
. It is an equivalence if
\mathcal{X}\in\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
but may not be an equivalence in general.

Example 5.1.2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
X\in\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
has a presentation as a cofiltered limit
X\cong\lim X_{a}
with
X_{a}\in\AlgSp^{\mathrm{ft}}_{k}

and affine transition morphisms,
then we have

\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(X)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{t^{!}}\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(X_{a}).

\mathrm{(b)}

Let
X=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}X_{a}
be an ind-algebraic space with
X_{a}\in\AlgSp_{k}
.
Then we have

(5.1.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(X)\cong\lim_{t^{!}}\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(X_{a}).

5.1.2.
The bar construction

Given a group ind-scheme
H
acting on an ind-scheme
X
the quotient
\infty
-stack
[X/H]
is equipped with
the stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(X/H)
. By definition the
\infty
-prestack
[X/H]
is the (homotopy) colimit of the bar construction

\text{Bar}(H,X)
, which is the semi-simplicial
\infty
-prestack built out of action and projection maps

\text{Bar}(H,X)=\Big(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
