126.65967pt\raise 6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phantom{hh}a}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 153.84702pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.8159pt\raise-7.34026pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phantom{hh}p_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 153.84702pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 153.84702pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\Big).

There is an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(X/H)=\lim_{t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(\text{Bar}(H,X)).

Thus, an
H
-equivariant complex on
X
is a collection of objects

\mathcal{E}_{n}\in\mathcal{D}(H^{n}\times X)
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
, together with equivalences

a^{!}\mathcal{E}_{0}\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}
,
p_{2}^{!}\mathcal{E}_{0}\cong\mathcal{E}_{1}
, etc., one for any map in
\text{Bar}(H,X)
,
subject to the compatibility conditions given by the relations between the maps.

5.1.2.
Lurie’s adjunction

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a small category and
\mathcal{I}\rightarrow\PrCat
a functor.
For each
i\in\mathcal{I}
, we are given an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{i}
and for each morphism
\alpha:i\to j

a continuous functor
\phi_{\alpha}:\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{C}_{j}
.
Suppose that for each morphism
\alpha
the functor
\phi_{\alpha}
has a continuous right adjoint
\psi_{\alpha}
.
Since adjoints are compatible with compositions, the datum
(\mathcal{C}_{i},\psi_{\alpha})
extends to a functor

\mathcal{I}^{{\mathrm{op}}}\rightarrow\PrCat
, see
[
60
, Cor. 5.5.3.4]
.
We consider the limit

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}=\lim_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}

with the evaluation functor

\ev_{i}:\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{C}_{i}

for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
.

Theorem 5.1.3
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The colimit
\mathcal{C}=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}
exists in
\PrCat
. It is equivalent to
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The equivalence
\mathcal{C}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
is characterized by the condition that the evaluation functor

\ev_{i}:\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{C}_{i}
is the right adjoint to the tautological functor

\ins_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{C}
for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
.

∎

Remark 5.1.4
.

Assume
\mathcal{I}
is filtered.
In this case
[
81
]
gives another description of the equivalence
\mathcal{C}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
, because
for each
i,j\in\mathcal{I}
the composition
\ev_{j}\circ\ins_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{C}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{C}_{j}
coincides with the colimit

\ev_{j}\circ\ins_{i}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\alpha,\beta}\phi_{\beta}\circ\psi_{\alpha}

over all
\alpha:i\to k
and
\beta:j\to k
.

Corollary 5.1.5
.

For each object
c\in\mathcal{C}
, the assignment
i\mapsto\ins_{i}\circ\ev_{i}(c)
yields a functor
\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{C}
.
The obvious map

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i}\ins_{i}\circ\ev_{i}(c)\to c

is an isomorphism.
∎

Example 5.1.6
.

If
\mathcal{X}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\mathcal{X}_{a}
is a colimit of prestacks then we have

\mathcal{D}(X)\cong\lim_{t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(X_{a}).

If the morphisms
i_{a}:\mathcal{X}_{a}\to\mathcal{X}
are such that the functor

(i_{a})^{!}
has a left adjoint
(i_{a})_{!}
, then we also have

(5.1.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(X)\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{t_{!}}\mathcal{D}(X_{a}).

Further, for each
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X)
we have

\mathcal{E}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(i_{a})_{!}(i_{a})^{!}(\mathcal{E}).

In particular, assume that
X=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}X_{a}
is a reasonable ind-algebraic space of ind-fp type, see Definition
2.1.1
.
For each fp closed embedding
i
in
\AlgSp_{k}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
the functor
i^{!}
has a left adjoint
i_{!}
.
Hence (
5.1.7
) and Theorem
5.1.3
imply that we have an equivalence
as in (
5.1.8
).

Finally, we consider some adjunctions in limits or colimits, following
[
10
, §5.1.7]
.
Let
\Cat
be either
\StCat
or
\PrCat
.
Let
\mathcal{I}
be a small
\infty
-category.
Next, let
\mathcal{D}_{.}
and
\mathcal{C}_{.}
be two functors
\mathcal{I}\to\Cat
and
let
\Phi_{.}:\mathcal{C}_{.}\to\mathcal{D}_{.}
be a morphism of functors. It is given by
a functor
\Phi_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{D}_{i}
for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
and
an equivalence
\Phi_{\alpha}:\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{i}\cong\Phi_{j}\circ\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
for each morphism

\alpha:i\to j
in
\mathcal{I}
.
Let
\widehat{\Phi}=\lim_{i}\Phi_{i}
be the limit functor
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{D}}
.
If
\mathcal{I}
is filtered, let also
\Phi=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i}\Phi_{i}
be the colimit functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
.
Assume that

\mathrm{(a)}

for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
the morphism
\Phi_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{D}_{i}
has a left adjoint
\Psi_{i}
,

\mathrm{(b)}

for each morphism
\alpha:i\to j
in
\mathcal{I}
the base change morphism

bc_{\alpha}:\Phi_{j}\circ\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\to\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\circ\Psi_{i}
, obtained from the counit map by adjointness

\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\to\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{i}\circ\Psi_{i}\cong\Phi_{j}\circ\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\circ\Psi_{i}

is an equivalence
(Beck–Chevalley condition).

Proposition 5.1.7
.

Suppose the assumptions above hold.

\mathrm{(a)}

The collection of
\Psi_{i}
’s and
bc_{\alpha}
’s define a morphism of functors
\Psi:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The limit functor
\widehat{\Phi}
has the left adjoint
\widehat{\Psi}
.
For each
i\in\mathcal{I}
the base change morphism is an equivalence

\Psi_{i}\circ\ev_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\ev_{i}^{\mathcal{C}}\circ\widehat{\Psi}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

Assume that
\mathcal{I}
is filtered.
The colimit functor
\Phi
has the left adjoint
\Psi
.
For each
i\in\mathcal{I}
the base change morphism is an equivalence

\Psi\circ\ins_{i}^{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow\ins_{i}^{\mathcal{C}}\circ\penalty\ \Psi_{i}

∎

5.2.
Cohomological operations

5.2.0.
General functoriality

For each morphism of
\infty
-stacks
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, we have a functor
f^{!}:\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y})\to\mathcal{D}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{X})
.
If
f
is a topological equivalence then the functor
f^{!}
is an equivalence of categories by
[
10
, Cor. 5.3.6]
.
If
f
is an ind-fp ind-proper morphism, then the functor
f^{!}
admits a left adjoint
f_{!}

that satisfies base change by
[
10
, Prop. 5.3.7]
.
If
f
is a topological fp locally closed immersion, then we have a functor
f_{*}
by
[
10
, §5.4.4]
.
We are interested in the existence of a left adjoint
f^{*}
.
To do so, we consider an intermediate class between ind-schemes and
\infty
-stacks that contains
[X_{K}/H]

for any group ind-scheme
H
of ind-ft over
k
which acts on
X_{K}
, for any
X\in\Aff^{\mathrm{ft}}_{k}
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

Following
[
10
, Def. 5.5.1]
,
we say that an
\infty
-stack
\mathcal{Y}
satisfies gluing if for each topological fp immersion

f:\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}
, the functor
f_{*}
has a left adjoint
f^{*}
.

Remark 5.2.2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
\mathcal{Y}
satisfies gluing, then so does each topologically fp-immersion

f:\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}
by
[
10
, Lem. 5.5.5.(a)]
.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\mathcal{X}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}
where each
\mathcal{X}_{\alpha}
satisfies gluing and transition maps are qc open immersions
or closed fp immersions, then
\mathcal{X}
satisfies gluing, see
[
10
, Lem. 5.5.5.(b)]
.
Note that any open immersion is of finite presentation if and only if it is qc by
[
86
, Tag 01TU]
.
In particular, it is the case of ind-ft-schemes.

\mathrm{(c)}

The quotient
[X/H]
of an ind-ft-algebraic space by an ind-ft group ind-scheme
H
satisfies gluing,
see
[
10
, Prop. 5.5.7]
. In particular, the Grassmannian
\Gr_{G}
statisfies gluing for any minimal KM group
G
.

5.2.2.
Functoriality for lft prestacks

We will restrict to a class of prestacks for which we have more operations.

Definition 5.2.3
.

A prestack
\mathcal{X}
is locally of finite type (lft) if

\mathcal{X}=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{S\rightarrow\mathcal{X}}S

with

S\in\Aff^{\mathrm{ft}}_{k}.

Remark 5.2.4
.

A quotient
[X/H]
is a lft prestack if
X
is an ind-ft ind-scheme and
H
is an ind-ft
group ind-scheme acting on
X
, due to the bar construction.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
and
g:\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be morphisms of lft prestacks.

\mathrm{(a)}

The functor
f^{!}
has a left adjoint
f_{!}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
f
is ft schematic, there is a continuous adjoint pair
(f^{*},f_{*})

with base change equivalences for
(f_{*},g^{!})
and
(f^{*},g_{!})
.

\mathrm{(c)}

If
f
is ind-ft ind-schematic, there is a continuous functor
f_{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})

with base change equivalences for
(f_{*},g^{!})
.
We have a left adjoint at the level of pro-categories

f^{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\Pro(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})).

Proof.

Part (a) is
[
34
, Cor. 1.4.2]
.
Part (b) is
[
51
, §2.6-2.8]
.
Note that all stacks are lft in Gaitsgory’s work by
[
34
, §0.8.1]
, as well as in
Ho’s work which uses the same conventions as
[
34
]
,
see
[
51
, §2.1]
.
To extend
f_{*}
from ft-schematic to ind-ft-schematic, if
\mathcal{Y}
is a ft-scheme, then using Lurie’s adjunction, we write

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{S\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X},i_{*}}\mathcal{D}(S)
where
S
runs over the ft-closed subschemes of
\mathcal{X}
and we get a functor

f_{*}
that satisfies base change
(f_{*},g^{!})
, thus it extends to a functor
f_{*}
for any ind-ft-schematic morphism of
lft prestacks that continues to satisfy base change.
The second one is proved in
[
28
, App. A3]
.
∎

Remark 5.2.6
.

Given lft prestacks
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
, the Lurie adjunction yields equivalences

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\mathcal{D}(X_{a}),\quad\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\mathcal{D}(Y_{a})

where the colimits run over all ft schemes
\lambda_{a}:X_{a}\to\mathcal{X}
and
\mu_{a}:Y_{a}\to\mathcal{Y}
.
Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a morphism such that
f=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}f_{a}
where
(f_{a})
is a system of morphisms of schemes
f_{a}:X_{a}\to Y_{a}
.
By
[
51
, Cor. 2.8.5]
we have

f^{*}=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\lambda_{a})_{!}(f_{a})^{*}(\mu_{a})^{!}.

Further, if
f:\mathcal{X}\to Y
is a morphism from a lft prestack to a ft scheme and
f_{a}=f\mu_{a}
, then

[
51
, Lem. 2.5.9]
and
[
34
, Proof of Prop. 1.5.2]
yield

f_{!}=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(f_{a})_{!}(\mu_{a})^{!}.

5.2.6.
Monoidal structure

Let
f_{i}:X_{i}\rightarrow Y_{i}
be morphisms of ft schemes with
i=1,2
.
Let
\star
denote either
!
or
*
.
The Künneth formula
[
43
, Exp. III, (1.6.1), Prop. 1.7.4]
yields an isomorphism

(5.2.1)

(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{\star}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})\cong f_{1}^{\star}K_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{\star}K_{2}

and
[
43
, Exp. III, §1.6.2, (1.7.1)]
yields an isomorphism

(5.2.2)

(f_{1}\times f_{2})_{\star}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})\cong(f_{1})_{\star}K_{1}\boxtimes(f_{2})_{\star}K_{2}.

Using the definition of
\mathcal{D}
as a colimit, this allows us to define
for arbitrary affine schemes
Y_{1}
,
Y_{2}
and for any objects
K_{i}\in\mathcal{D}(Y_{i})
the external tensor product
K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2}
.
For any prestacks
\mathcal{Y}_{i}
, not necessarily lft, we define the external tensor product such that (
5.2.1
) holds for pull-back
by morphisms
S_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{i}
for
S_{i}
being affine schemes, see also
[
34
, §1.3.4]

or
[
51
, §2.10]
.
Taking the first projection
p:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, we obtain

(5.2.3)

p^{!}K\cong K\boxtimes\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}.

Indeed it is enough to check (
5.2.3
) on schemes after pulling back to
S_{1}\times S_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
.
Let
\widehat{\boxtimes}
denote the external tensor product of pro-sheaves.

Lemma 5.2.7
.

Let
f_{i}:\mathcal{X}_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{i}
be ind-ft ind-schematic morphisms, between lft prestacks for
i=1,2
.

\mathrm{(a)}

We have an isomorphism

(5.2.4)

(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})\cong f_{1}^{*}K_{1}\widehat{\boxtimes}f_{2}^{*}K_{2}.

\mathrm{(b)}

If in addition
\mathcal{Y}_{i}
are ind-schemes of ind-ft, we have an isomorphism

(5.2.5)

(f_{1}\times f_{2})_{!}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})\cong(f_{1})_{!}K_{1}\boxtimes(f_{2})_{!}K_{2}.

Proof.

Let first assume that
\mathcal{Y}_{i}=Y_{i}
are ft schemes.
Thus
\mathcal{X}_{i}=X_{i}
are ind-schemes of ind-ft.
We write
X_{i}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}X_{i,a}
for a system of maps
\lambda_{i,a}:X_{i,a}\to X_{i}
where
X_{i,a}
are ft-schemes.
Hence, for any
K_{i}\in\mathcal{D}(Y_{i})
, the formula (
5.2.1
) yields

(5.2.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})&\cong\quotlim((f_{1,a}\times f_{2,a})^{*}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2}))\\
&\cong\quotlim((f_{1,a})^{*}K_{1}\boxtimes(f_{2,a})^{*}K_{2})\\
&\cong f_{1}^{*}K_{1}\widehat{\boxtimes}f_{2}^{*}K_{2}\end{split}

with
f_{i,a}=f_{i}\circ\lambda_{i,a}
a morphism of ft schemes.
For the second claim,
Lurie’s adjunction yields

\mathcal{D}(X_{i})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{(\lambda_{i,a})_{!}}\mathcal{D}(X_{i,a}),\quad\mathcal{D}(X_{1}\times X_{2})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{(\lambda_{a})_{!}}\mathcal{D}(X_{1,a}\times X_{2,a})

where
\lambda_{a}=\lambda_{1,a}\times\lambda_{2,a}
.
Any complex
K_{i}\in\mathcal{D}(X_{i})
admits the presentation

K_{i}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}(\lambda_{i,a})_{!}(\lambda_{i,a})^{!}K_{i}.

Since taking
!
-pushforwards is a left adjoint functor, it is continuous.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle(f_{1}\times f_{2})_{!}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})

\displaystyle\cong(f_{1}\times f_{2})_{!}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}(\lambda_{a})_{!}\lambda_{a}^{!}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}(f_{1,a}\times f_{2,a})_{!}\lambda_{a}^{!}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}(f_{1,a}\times f_{2,a})_{!}(\lambda_{1,a}^{!}K_{1}\boxtimes\lambda_{2,a}^{!}K_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}((f_{1})_{!}(\lambda_{1,a})_{!}\lambda_{1,a}^{!}K_{1}\boxtimes(f_{2})_{!}(\lambda_{2,a})_{!}\lambda_{2,a}^{!}K_{2})

\displaystyle\cong(f_{1})_{!}K_{1}\boxtimes(f_{2})_{!}K_{2}

where the third isomorphism follows from (
5.2.1
) and the fourth one from (
5.2.2
).

Consider now the general case.
Fix a presentation

\mathcal{Y}_{i}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}Y_{i,a},

for a system of maps

\mu_{i,a}:Y_{i,a}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{i}
with
Y_{i,a}
a scheme of ft.
This yields the presentation

\mathcal{X}_{i}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}X_{i,a}

for the system of maps
\lambda_{i,a}:X_{i,a}\to\mathcal{X}_{i}

where
X_{i,a}=\mathcal{X}_{i}\times_{\mathcal{Y}_{i}}Y_{i,a}

is an ind-ft ind-scheme.
Lurie’s adjunction yields

\displaystyle K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2}

\displaystyle\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}(\lambda_{a})_{!}(\lambda_{a})^{!}(K_{1}\boxtimes K_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{a}(\lambda_{a})_{!}((\lambda_{1,a})^{!}K_{1}\boxtimes(\lambda_{2,a})^{!}K_{2}).

The first isomorphism is as in Example
5.1.6
, the second one
is (
5.2.1
) extended to lft prestacks.
The claim (
5.2.4
) follows by proper base change and (
5.2.6
).
Indeed, since taking
*
-pullbacks is a left adjoint functor by Proposition
5.2.5
,
it commutes with colimits by
[
60
, Cor. 5.5.2.9]
.
Since we work with pro-sheaves, to check (
5.2.4
) we check it term by term, hence the claim follows from (
5.2.6
).
∎

5.2.7.
Projection formula

Let
\mathcal{X}
be any prestack. For any
K,L\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
, we set

K\overset{!}{\otimes}L=\Delta^{!}(K\boxtimes L).

By construction, for any morphism of prestacks
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
, we have

(5.2.7)

\phi^{!}(K\overset{!}{\otimes}L)=\phi^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}\phi^{!}L

Lemma 5.2.8
.

Let
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be any ind-ft ind-schematic morphism of lft prestacks.
For any
K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
and
L\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})
we have un isomorphism

\phi_{*}(\phi^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}L)\cong K\overset{!}{\otimes}\phi_{*}L.

Proof.

Using base change and (
5.2.7
),
we reduce to the case where
\mathcal{X}
is a ft scheme.
Then the assertion is standard and follows by Verdier duality from the usual projection formula, as in this case
K\overset{!}{\otimes}L=\bD(\bD(K)\otimes\bD(L))
.
∎

5.2.8.
Base change identities

Proposition 5.2.9
.

Let
H
be a group ind-scheme of ind-ft,

f:\uucX\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be an
H
-torsor between lft prestacks, and

\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be an ind-ft ind-schematic morphism of lft prestacks.
We form the Cartesian diagram

(5.2.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 10.15245pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.25pt\raise 5.83888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\uuphi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\uucX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.5pt\raise-15.34999pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{f}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.5pt\raise-23.42223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise-30.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.34584pt\raise-24.5889pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-30.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-30.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

\mathrm{(a)}

The obvious morphism is an isomorphism

(5.2.9)

\uuphi^{*}f^{!}\cong g^{!}\phi^{*}.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\uucX
is an ind-ft ind-scheme, then

(i)

the canonical map is an equivalence

(5.2.10)

\uuphi^{!}f^{*}\cong g^{*}\phi^{!},

(ii)

the base change gives equivalences

(5.2.11)

f^{!}\phi_{!}\cong\uuphi_{!}g^{!},\quad f^{*}\phi_{*}\cong\uuphi_{*}g^{*}.

Proof.

The bar-resolution gives a presentation of the stack
\mathcal{X}=[\uucX/H]
.
Hence

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})\cong\lim_{[n],t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(H^{n}\times\uucX).

Similarly, we have a stack isomorphism
\uucX=[H\times\uucX/H]
which yields an
equivalence

\mathcal{D}(\uucX)=\lim_{[n],t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(H^{n+1}\times\uucX).

The transitions morphisms
H^{n}\times\uucX\to H^{m}\times\uucX
decompose as isomorphisms and projections of the form

H\times\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}
as in
[
10
, Prop. 5.5.7]
.
Thus (
5.2.9
) follows from the base change property for the Cartesian
diagram of lft prestacks

\textstyle{H\times\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\id\times\uuphi}
\textstyle{H\times\uucX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\uuphi}
\textstyle{\uucX}

More precisely, by (
5.2.3
) and (
5.2.4
), for each
K\in\mathcal{D}(\uucX)
we have

(\id\times\uuphi)^{*}p^{!}K=\omega_{H}\boxtimes\uuphi^{*}K=q^{!}\uuphi^{*}K.

This proves (a). To prove (b), we assume that
\uucX
is an ind-ft ind-scheme. Then so is
\uucY
by base change.
Write
X=\uucX
and
Y=\uucY
.
Note that
\mathcal{X}=[X/H]
and
\mathcal{Y}=[Y/H]
.
For any
K\in\mathcal{D}(Y)
, using (
5.2.5
) we obtain

p^{!}\uuphi_{!}K=\omega_{H}\boxtimes\uuphi_{!}K=(\id\times\uuphi)_{!}(\omega_{H}\boxtimes K)=(\id\times\uuphi)_{!}q^{!}K.

Next, we consider the Cartesian diagram of ind-ft schemes

(5.2.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.68744pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.68744pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.09367pt\raise 7.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.79167pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\id\times\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.68744pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.68744pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.8957pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.8957pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.54369pt\raise-25.22221pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.361pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.361pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

We must check that

(5.2.13)

\displaystyle(\id\times\phi)^{!}p^{*}\cong q^{*}\phi^{!},\quad p^{*}\phi_{*}\cong(\id\times\phi)_{*}q^{*},\quad p^{!}\phi_{!}\cong(\id\times\phi)_{!}q^{!}.

Writing
H\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}H_{a}
as a colimit of ft schemes, one has an equality of pro-systems

(\id\times\phi)^{!}p^{*}K=\quotlim(\id\times\phi)^{!}p_{a}^{*}K\in\Pro(\mathcal{D}(H\times Y)),

with
p_{a}:H_{a}\times Y\rightarrow Y
. Similarly, we have

q^{*}\phi^{!}K=\quotlim p_{a}^{*}\phi^{!}K\in\Pro(\mathcal{D}(H\times Y)).

So we reduce the first isomorphism in (
5.2.13
) to the case where
H
is of ft.
We deduce that

(\id\times\phi)^{!}p^{*}K\cong(\id\times\phi)^{!}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\boxtimes K)\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\boxtimes\phi^{!}K\cong q^{*}(\phi^{!}K).

For the second one, we are reduced to prove the base change property for the Cartesian diagram
(
5.2.12
).
Again, we first reduce to the case where
H
is of ft.
By continuity of the functors, we can assume that everything is of ft.
Using (
5.2.2
), we get

p^{*}\phi_{*}K\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\boxtimes\phi_{*}K\cong(\id\times\phi)_{*}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\boxtimes K)\cong(\id\times\phi)_{*}q^{*}K.

∎

5.2.9.
Contractive morphisms

Definition 5.2.10
.

\mathrm{(a)}

A
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action contracts a prestack
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathcal{X}^{0}
if the attractor,
repeller and fixed point locus are such that

\mathcal{X}^{+}=\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}^{-}=\mathcal{X}^{0}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

A
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation
H=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}H_{a}
of a group ind-scheme is contracting
if the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action contracts
H
to
\{1\}
.

Lemma 5.2.11
.

Let
H
be a group ind-scheme of ind-ft with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant contracting presentation.
Let
X
be an ind-ft ind-scheme with an
H
-action.
Set
\mathcal{X}=[X/H]
and
f:X\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
.
Then, the functors
f^{*}
and
f^{!}
are fully faithful.
Equivalently, we have
f_{!}f^{!}\cong\id\cong f_{*}f^{*}
.

Proof.

Since
H
admits a contracting
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation,
we have
H\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}H_{a}
with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on each
H_{a}
contracting to
\{1\}
.
Let
\pi^{[n]}:H^{n}\times X\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be the obvious projection.
Let
K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
.
Since
\mathcal{X}=[X/H]
, we have an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})=\lim_{[n],t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(H^{n}\times X).

Under this equivalence, the complex

K
identifies with the projective system
(K^{n})
given by
K^{n}=(\pi_{n})^{!}K
.
Since
X=[H\times X/H]
, we also have an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(X)=\lim_{[n],t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(H^{n}\times H\times X).

Let
p_{n}
be the obvious map
H^{n+1}\times X\to H^{n}\times X
.
The functor
(p_{n})_{*}
satisfies base change by Proposition
5.2.5
.
Hence Proposition
5.2.9
yields

f_{*}f^{*}K=((p_{n})_{*}(p_{n})^{*}K^{n}),\quad f_{!}f^{!}K=((p_{n})_{!}(p_{n})^{!}K^{n})

In particular, we are reduced to prove that for each
n
, we have

(p_{n})_{*}(p_{n})^{*}\cong(p_{n})_{!}(p_{n})^{!}\cong\id.

Fix a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation
H\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}H_{a}
.
For each complex
K\in\mathcal{D}(H^{n}\times X)
we have an equality of pro-systems

(p_{n})_{*}(p_{n})^{*}K=\quotlim(p_{n,a})_{*}(p_{n,a})^{*}K,

We are thus reduced to the case
H=H_{a}
.
In this case, the functors
(p_{n,a})_{*}
and
(p_{n,a})^{*}
are both continuous
by Proposition
5.2.5
.
Taking a presentation
X=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}X_{a}
and using Lurie’s adjunction again, we obtain

\mathcal{D}(X)=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\mathcal{D}(X_{a})
.
Thus we can assume
H^{n}\times X
to be a ft
k
-scheme.
Since the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is contracting, the contraction principle for algebraic spaces in
[
28
, Prop. 3.2.2.(a)]

implies that for each
n,

a
we have

(p_{n,a})_{*}(p_{n,a})^{*}K=\epsilon_{a}^{*}(p_{n,a})^{*}K\cong K,

where
\epsilon_{a}:1\rightarrow H_{a}
is the unit.
The proof of the second assertion is similar, using instead the contraction principle for
\epsilon_{a}^{!}
in
[
28
, Prop. 3.2.2.(b)]
.
∎

We now turn Lemma
5.2.11
and Proposition
5.2.9
into a definition.

Definition 5.2.12
.

Let
f:\uucX\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a morphism of lft prestacks.
We say that

\mathrm{(a)}

f
satisfies universal descent if the functor
\underline{f}^{!}
in (
5.1.6
) yields an equivalence

\underline{f}^{!}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})\cong\lim_{[n],t^{!}}\mathcal{D}(\uucX^{n}),

as well as after any base change
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
of lft prestacks,

\mathrm{(b)}

f
is weakly smooth if it is ind-ft ind-schematic,
satisfies universal descent and for each ind-ft ind-schematic morphism
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
with Cartesian diagram (
5.2.8
),
we have

(i)

\uuphi^{!}f^{*}\cong g^{*}\phi^{!}
and
\uuphi^{*}f^{!}\cong g^{!}\phi^{*},

(5.2.14)

(ii)

f^{!}\phi_{!}\cong\uuphi_{!}g^{!}
and
f^{*}\phi_{*}\cong\uuphi_{*}g^{*}
,
(5.2.15)

as well as after any ind-ft-schematic base change
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
of lft prestacks,

\mathrm{(c)}

f
is contractive, if it is weakly smooth and
f^{*}
,
f^{!}
are fully faithful and stay so after any ind-ft-schematic base change

\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
of lft prestacks.

Lemma 5.2.13
.

Let
H
be a group ind-scheme of ind-ft, and

f:\uucX\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be an
H
-torsor between lft prestacks.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
H
is formally smooth then
f
is also formally smooth.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\uucX
is an ind-ft-scheme then
f
weakly smooth.

Proof.

Formal smoothness of morphisms of stacks is étale-local on the target.
Since
f
is an étale
H
-torsor, there are sections étale locally, see §
2.1.0
.
Part (a) follows. Part (b) follows from Proposition
5.2.9
.
∎

Remark 5.2.14
.

\mathrm{(a)}

Ind-ft-schematic morphisms are stable by ind-ft-schematic base change and by composition.
Indeed, for such
X\rightarrow Y\rightarrow Z
, we can assume
Z
is a scheme.
Then, write
Y\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}Y_{a}
with
Y_{a}
of finite type over
Z
.
We have
X\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}X\times_{Y}Y_{a}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}X_{aa^{\prime}}
with
X_{aa^{\prime}}
of finite type over
Y_{a}
, hence the claim.

\mathrm{(b)}

Universal descent, weakly smoothness and contractiveness are also stable by ind-ft ind-schematic base change
and by composition.

We need stability by base change of these notions for a larger class of morphisms.

Proposition 5.2.15
.

Consider a Cartesian diagram of lft prestacks

67.55556pt\raise-30.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.55556pt\raise-30.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

Assume that
\psi
is weakly smooth and
\phi\psi
is ind-ft-schematic.
If
f
is contractive, then
g
is contractive.

Proof.

By base change, we already know that
g
is ind-ft-schematic and satisfies universal descent.
We claim that
g^{!}
and
g^{*}
are fully faithful. We start with
g^{!}
.
We must prove that the counit
g_{!}g^{!}\rightarrow\Id
is an equivalence.
Since
\psi
satisfies universal descent, the functor
\psi^{!}
is conservative, thus we can check the assertion after applying
\psi^{!}
.
Since
f
is contractive and
\phi\psi
is ind-ft ind-schematic, the morphism
h
is contractive by base change.
Thus
h^{!}
is fully faithful. Since
\psi
is weakly smooth, we apply (

(
b
)

(ii)
) to
\psi
to get

\psi^{!}g_{!}g^{!}\cong h_{!}\uupsi^{!}g^{!}\cong h_{!}h^{!}\psi^{!}\cong\psi^{!},

as wished.
The claim for
g^{*}
is similar: one check it after applying
\psi^{!}
, using base change, full faithfulness of
h^{*}

and (

(
b
)

(i)
) for
\psi
.
Let us prove now that
g
is weakly smooth.
Let
\lambda:\mathcal{W}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be an ind-ft morphism.
We consider the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\underline{\mathcal{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{g}}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}

Let us prove that
\underline{\lambda}^{!}g^{*}\cong\underline{g}^{*}\lambda^{!}
, the
g^{!}
-assertion is similar.
We base change by
\lambda
the diagram (
5.2.16
) to get

\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{\uucZ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\lambda}_{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\uupsi}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{h}}
\textstyle{\uucZ\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\uupsi}
\textstyle{\underline{\mathcal{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{g}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\psi}}
\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}

By base change by ind-ft-schematic morphisms, the maps
\uupsi
and
\tilde{\uupsi}
are still weakly smooth.
Thus the functors
\uupsi^{!}
and
\tilde{\uupsi}^{!}
are conservative, and it is enough to prove the claim after applying
\tilde{\uupsi}^{!}
.
We obtain

\tilde{\uupsi}^{!}\underline{\lambda}^{!}g^{*}=\lambda_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}\uupsi^{!}g^{*}=\lambda_{\mathcal{Z}}^{!}h^{*}\psi^{!}=\tilde{h}^{*}\underline{\lambda}^{!}\psi^{!}.

Here the second and third equalities follow from weakly smoothness of
\psi
and
h
.
Finally, by base change again
\tilde{\psi}
is weakly smooth, thus we get

\tilde{h}^{*}\underline{\lambda}^{!}\psi^{!}=\tilde{h}^{*}\tilde{\psi}^{!}\lambda^{!}=\tilde{\uupsi}^{!}\underline{g}^{*}\lambda^{!},

as wished.
The second claim about proper base change is proved along the same lines.
∎

5.2.15.
Homotopically ind-schematic morphisms

We must define
*
-pushforwards for a lft prestack. It may not exist.
We define some pushforwards using Lemma
5.2.11
.

Definition 5.2.16
.

Let
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
be a morphism of lft prestacks.

\mathrm{(a)}

\phi
is homotopically ind-schematic if there is a morphism of lft
prestacks
g:\uucY\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
such that

(i)

the composition

\phi\,g

is ind-ft ind-schematic,

(ii)

g
is contractive.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\phi
is homotopically ind-schematic
we define the functor
\phi_{\bullet}=(\phi\,g)_{*}g^{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\Pro(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X}))
.

Lemma 5.2.17
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The functor
\phi_{\bullet}
is well-defined.
If
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is ind-ft ind-schematic, then
\phi_{*}
exists and
\phi_{\bullet}=\phi_{*}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

Homotopically ind-schematic morphisms are stable by base change by morphisms

\phi
of lft prestacks such that there exists a weakly smooth
\psi
with
\phi\,\psi
ind-ft ind-schematic.

Proof.

To prove (a) note that, if there are two contractive morphisms
g_{1}:\uucY_{1}\to\mathcal{Y}
and
g_{2}:\uucY_{2}\to\mathcal{Y}
as in the definition,
then we can form the morphism
g_{3}:\uucY_{3}\to\mathcal{Y}
where
\uucY_{3}=\uucY_{1}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY_{2}
.
Since
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are contractive, the
*
-pullbacks by the maps

f_{1}:\uucY_{3}\rightarrow\uucY_{1}
and
f_{2}:\uucY_{3}\rightarrow\uucY_{2}
given by ind-ft base change are fully faithful.
Hence

(\phi\,g_{1})_{*}g_{1}^{*}=(\phi\,g_{1})_{*}(f_{1})_{*}f_{1}^{*}g_{1}^{*}=(\phi\,g_{3})_{*}g_{3}^{*}=(\phi\,g_{2})_{*}(f_{2})_{*}f_{2}^{*}g_{2}^{*}=(\phi\,g_{2})_{*}g_{2}^{*}

In particular, the functor
\phi_{\bullet}
does not depend of the choice of the contractive morphism
g:\uucY\to\mathcal{Y}
.
Part (b) follows from Proposition
5.2.15
, and the fact that ind-ft ind-schematic morphisms are preserved by base change.
∎

Lemma 5.2.18
.

Let
H
be a group ind-scheme of ind-ft with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant contracting presentation.

\mathrm{(a)}

Any
H
-torsor
g:\uucY\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
with
\uucY
an ind-ft ind-scheme is contractive.

\mathrm{(b)}

Any morphism
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
such that

\phi g
is ind-ft-schematic is homotopically ind-schematic.

Proof.

The torsor
g
is weakly smooth by Lemma
5.2.14
.
The functors
g^{*}
,
g^{!}
are fully faithful by Lemma
5.2.11
,
and they stay so after any ind-ft ind-schematic base change

\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
of lft prestacks, because
H
-torsors are stable by base change.
Thus
g
is contractive.
∎

Lemma 5.2.19
.

The composition of two homotopically ind-schematic morphisms

\psi:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
and
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
is also homotopically ind-schematic.

Proof.

Consider the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\uucZ\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\uucZ\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}

As ind-ft-schematic morphisms are preserved by composition and base change, remark
5.2.14
gives that
g^{\prime}\,h^{\prime}
is contractive.
Moreover, by base change, the morphism
\uucZ\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\rightarrow\uucY
is ind-ft ind-schematic and,
by composition, the morphism
\uucZ\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is also ind-ft ind-schematic.
∎

We claim the functor
(-)_{\bullet}
is compatible with base changes, compositions, and the projection formula.

Proposition 5.2.20
.

Let
\psi:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
and
\phi:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
be homotopically ind-schematic morphisms.

\mathrm{(a)}

For any ind-ft-schematic morphism
\pi:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
of lft prestacks, let

\pi^{\prime}:\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Y}
and
\phi^{\prime}:\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
be the base changes of
\pi
and
\phi
.
There is an isomorphism of functors
\pi^{!}\phi_{\bullet}\rightarrow(\phi^{\prime})_{\bullet}(\pi^{\prime})^{!}.

\mathrm{(b)}

There is an isomorphism of functors

(\phi\,\psi)_{\bullet}\cong\phi_{\bullet}\psi_{\bullet}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

For each
K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
and
L\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})
we have an isomorphism

\phi_{\bullet}(\phi^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}L)\cong K\overset{!}{\otimes}\phi_{\bullet}L
.

Proof.

For part (a) we consider the following Cartesian diagram

(5.2.17)

\textstyle{\uucY^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\uuphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\uuphi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}

The base changes yields a chain of isomorphisms

\pi^{!}\phi_{\bullet}=\pi^{!}\uuphi_{*}g^{*}\cong(\uuphi^{\prime})_{*}(\pi^{\prime\prime})^{!}g^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{shriek-star}}}{{\cong}}(\uuphi^{\prime})_{*}(g^{\prime})^{*}(\pi^{\prime})^{!}=(\phi^{\prime})_{\bullet}(\pi^{\prime})^{!}.

To prove (b), note that, by Lemma
5.2.19
, the composed map
\phi\,\psi
is homotopically ind-schematic.
We have the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\uucZ\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\uupsi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\underline{g}}
\textstyle{\uucZ\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\uupsi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\uucY\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\uuphi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}

By Lemma
5.2.19
and base change, to compute the functor
(\phi\,\psi)_{\bullet}
we can use the map

\uuphi\uupsi^{\prime}:\uucZ\times_{\mathcal{Y}}\uucY\rightarrow\mathcal{X}.

We have

\phi_{\bullet}\psi_{\bullet}=\uuphi_{*}g^{*}\uupsi_{*}h^{*}=\uuphi_{*}\uupsi^{\prime}_{*}\underline{g}^{*}h^{*}=\uuphi_{*}\uupsi^{\prime}_{*}(g^{\prime}h^{\prime})^{*}=(\phi\,\psi)_{\bullet},

where the second equality follows from the fact that the map
g
satisfies (

(
b
)

(ii)
).

To prove (c), let
g:\uucY\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a contractive morphism such that the map
\phi\,g
is ind-ft ind-schematic.
The projection formula holds for the maps
\phi\,g
and
g
.
Further, Lemma
5.2.17
yields
(\phi\,g)_{*}=(\phi\,g)_{\bullet}
and
g_{*}=g_{\bullet}
.
Finally, since
g
is contractive we have
g_{*}g^{*}=\id
.
Thus Lemma
5.2.8
and Proposition
5.2.20
yield

\displaystyle K\overset{!}{\otimes}\phi_{\bullet}L

\displaystyle=K\overset{!}{\otimes}(\phi\,g)_{*}g^{*}L

\displaystyle=(\phi\,g)_{*}((\phi\,g)^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}g^{*}L)

\displaystyle=(\phi\,g)_{\bullet}((\phi\,g)^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}g^{*}L)

\displaystyle=\phi_{\bullet}g_{\bullet}((\phi\,g)^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}g^{*}L)

\displaystyle=\phi_{\bullet}g_{*}(g^{!}\phi^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}g^{*}L)

\displaystyle=\phi_{\bullet}(\phi^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}g_{*}g^{*}L)

\displaystyle=\phi_{\bullet}(\phi^{!}K\overset{!}{\otimes}L).

∎

5.2.20.
Formalism of kernels

We follow
[
29
, Cor. 4.2.3]
.
Let
\mathcal{Y}_{1},

\mathcal{Y}_{2}
be lft prestacks.
Let
p_{1},p_{2}:\mathcal{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{Y}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}_{1}
be the obvious projections.
Assume that
p_{2}
is homotopically ind-schematic.
For any sheaf
K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{Y}_{2})
we have the functor

\Phi_{K}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{1})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{2}),\quad\mathcal{M}\mapsto(p_{2})_{\bullet}(p_{1}^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{!}{\otimes}K)

We say that
K
is the kernel of the functor
\Phi_{K}
.
More generally, let

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{Y}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{2}}

be a commutative diagram of lft prestacks.
Assume that
p_{2}
and
r
are homotopically ind-schematic.
Let

K=r_{\bullet}(\omega_{\mathcal{X}})\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{Y}_{2}).

The projection formula in Proposition
5.2.20
implies that the functor
\Phi_{K}
identifies with
q_{\bullet}p^{!}
.
The functor
K\rightarrow\Phi_{K}
may not be fully faithful.
Nevertheless, the morphisms
\Phi_{K}\rightarrow\Phi_{L}
that we will encounter come from morphisms of sheaves
K\rightarrow L
.
In particular, we will encounter the following setting.
Consider a commutative diagram

(5.2.18)
