Assume that
j
is a qc open immersion.
Hence, it is ind-ft ind-schematic.
We consider the kernel

L=(rj)_{\bullet}(\omega_{\mathcal{U}})\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{Y}_{2}).

Proposition
5.2.20
, the equality
j_{\bullet}=j_{*}
in
Lemma
5.2.17
and the equality
j^{!}=j^{*}

yield a morphism of kernels

(5.2.19)

K\rightarrow r_{\bullet}j_{*}j^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow(rj)_{\bullet}\omega_{\mathcal{U}}=L.

By functoriality, this yields an obvious map

(5.2.20)

\Phi_{K}\rightarrow\Phi_{L}.

5.3.
The t-structures

Consider an
\infty
-stack
\mathcal{Y}
and a collection of non-empty topological fp
locally closed reduced substacks
\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}
, such that
\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{\beta}=\emptyset
whenever
\alpha\neq\beta
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The collection
\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\}
is a finite constructible stratification if
\mathcal{I}
is finite and there is a total order

\alpha_{1}<\dots<\alpha_{n}
of
\mathcal{I}
and an increasing sequence
\emptyset=\mathcal{Y}_{0}\subset\dots\subset\mathcal{Y}_{n}=\mathcal{Y}
such that

\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha_{i}}\subset\mathcal{Y}_{i}\backslash\mathcal{Y}_{i-1}
and the embedding is a topological equivalence for each
i=1,\dots,n
,
see
[
10
, §2.4.5]
.

\mathrm{(b)}

A substack
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{Y}
is adapted to the collection
\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\}
if for each
\alpha
,
either
\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}=\emptyset
or
\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\subset\mathcal{X}
. Then, we set
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{X}}=\{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}\,;\,\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\subset\mathcal{X}\}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

The collection
\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\}
is a bounded constructible stratification if there is a presentation
\mathcal{Y}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\beta}\mathcal{U}_{\beta}

as a filtered colimit where
\mathcal{U}_{\beta}
is an fp open adapted substack of
\mathcal{Y}
and the induced stratification
on each
\mathcal{U}_{\beta}
is finite constructible.

\mathrm{(d)}

The collection
\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\}
is a constructible stratification if there is a presentation
\mathcal{Y}\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\beta}\mathcal{Y}_{\beta}
as a filtered colimit
where
\mathcal{Y}_{\beta}
is a topologically fp closed adapted substack of
\mathcal{Y}
and the induced stratification on each
\mathcal{Y}_{\beta}
is bounded
constructible.

A perversity on a stratified stack
(\mathcal{Y}\,,\,\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\})
is a function

p_{\nu}:\mathcal{I}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
,
\alpha\mapsto\nu_{\alpha}
.
If
\mathcal{Y}
satisfies gluing, then we have pullback functors

(i_{\alpha})^{*},(i_{\alpha})^{!}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}).

If each
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha})
has a perverse
t
-structure
(\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha})\,,\,\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}))

then
[
10
, Prop. 6.4.2]
yields a unique
t
-structure
(\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}(\mathcal{Y})\,,\,\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{Y}))

on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})
such that

(5.3.1)

\begin{split}\mathstrut{}^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}(\mathcal{Y})=\{K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})\,;\,(i_{\alpha})^{!}K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq-\nu_{\alpha}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}),\forall\penalty\ \alpha\in\mathcal{I}\}.\end{split}

If the stratification
\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\}
is bounded constructible, then we also have

(5.3.2)

\begin{split}\mathstrut{}^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}(\mathcal{Y})=\{K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})\,;\,(i_{\alpha})^{*}K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq-\nu_{\alpha}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}),\forall\penalty\ \alpha\in\mathcal{I}\}.\end{split}

Let
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}
be the heart of the
t
-structure. We identify the heart with its homotopy category as in
[
61
, Rmk. 1.2.1.12]
.
In particular, we view it as an Abelian category.
If
j:\mathcal{U}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}
is an fp open immersion adapted to the stratification
(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha})
,
and
i:\mathcal{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}
is its closed complement,
we equip
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U})
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z})
with the induced
t
-structures.
For any
K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U})^{\heartsuit}

one can define the intermediate extension
j_{!*}K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}
as in
[
10
, §6.4.8.(b)]
.
If further the stratification
\{\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}\}
is bounded, then by
[
10
, Lem. 6.4.11.(d)]
the sheaf
j_{!*}K

is the unique sheaf
\tilde{K}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})
such that

(5.3.3)

(i_{\alpha})^{!}\tilde{K}\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{>-\nu_{\alpha}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}),\quad(i_{\alpha})^{*}\tilde{K}\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{<-\nu_{\alpha}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha}),\quad\forall\penalty\ \alpha\in\mathcal{I}\smallsetminus\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{U}}

Lemma 5.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a bounded stratified stack with a perversity
p_{\nu}
such that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{a})
has a
t
-structure.
The simple objects in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}
are the intermediate extensions of the simple objects in

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}
as
\alpha
runs into
\mathcal{I}
.

Proof.

Choose a partial order on the set
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{Y}_{\leq\alpha}=\bigcup_{\beta\leq\alpha}\mathcal{Y}_{\beta}
is
an fp closed substack of
\mathcal{Y}
and
\{\beta\in\mathcal{I}\,;\,\beta>\alpha\}
is finite for each
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}
.
Set
\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{Y}_{\leq\alpha}
and
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}
.
Let
i_{\alpha}:\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}\to\mathcal{Y}
and
j_{\alpha}:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\to\mathcal{Y}
be the obvious maps.
By general properties of glueing, we have a short exact sequences of Abelian categories

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i_{\alpha})_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(j_{\alpha})^{!}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where
(i_{\alpha})_{*}
is exact and fully faithful and embeds

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}
as a Serre subcategory, and
(j_{\alpha})^{!}
is exact and yields an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}/\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}\cong\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}
such that simple in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha})^{\heartsuit}
lift uniquely to

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})^{\heartsuit}
via
(j_{\alpha})_{!*}
. The claim follows by descending induction.
∎

5.4.
The t-structure on the affine Grassmannian

Recall that
\Gr_{G}=[G/G_{\mathcal{O}}]
,
\Gr_{G_{c}}=[G_{c}/G_{\mathcal{O}}]
and that
both stacks
\Gr_{G}
,
\Gr_{G_{c}}
are quotients of an ind-ft ind-scheme by a group ind-scheme of ind-ft.
Thus they both satisfy gluing.
Further, the Cartan decomposition yields a stratification on
\Gr_{G_{c}}
.

Lemma 5.4.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The decomposition
\Gr_{G_{c}}=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}}\Gr_{\lambda}

is a constructible stratification.

\mathrm{(b)}

The decomposition
\Gr_{\leqslant\lambda}=\bigsqcup_{\mu\leqslant\lambda}\Gr_{\mu}

is a bounded constructible stratification.

Proof.

The Cartan decomposition implies that it is a stratification. We must check that the stratification of
\Gr_{\leqslant\lambda}
is bounded.
The open sets
U_{\mu}=\Gr_{\leq\lambda}\setminus\Gr_{\leq\mu}
with
\mu\leq\lambda
and
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
yield
the open covering

\Gr_{\leq\lambda}=\bigcup_{\mu\leq\lambda}U_{\mu}.

We must check that
U_{\mu}
contains only a finite number of strata
\Gr_{\nu}
,
which by the closure relations ends to check that the set of cocharacters

\{\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}\,;\,\mu\leq\nu\leq\lambda\}
is finite.
∎

The
t
-structure we now construct holds only on the category of
G(\mathcal{O})
-equivariant sheaves on
\Gr_{G_{c}}
.
We’ll need the following contraction lemma.

Lemma 5.4.2
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a morphism of ind-schemes of ind-ft over
k
.
Assume that
X
has a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation and that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action contracts
X

to
X^{0}\cong S
. Then
f_{!}\omega_{X}\cong\omega_{S}
.

Proof.

Since the assertion commutes with filtered colimits,
considering a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation of
X

we reduce to the case
where
X
and
S
are ft
k
-schemes and
X
is contracted to
S
.
Then, the isomorphism
f_{!}\omega_{X}\cong\omega_{S}

is a particular case of hyperbolic localization,
with attractor
X^{+}=X
and repeller and fixed point locus
X^{-}=X^{0}=S
, see
[
76
, Thm. B]
.
∎

Recall that
K_{\lambda}

is the stabilizer in
G_{\mathcal{O}}
of the point
[s^{\lambda}]\in\Gr_{G}
and
P_{\lambda}^{-}=G\cap K_{\lambda}
.

Proposition 5.4.3
.

Let
\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}
. Assume that
G
is of affine type (untwisted or not).

\mathrm{(a)}

There is an equivalence of stacks

[G_{\mathcal{O}}\backslash\Gr_{\lambda}]\cong\mathbb{B}K_{\lambda}.

\mathrm{(b)}

There is an equivalence
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}K_{\lambda})\cong\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda})

for some reductive group
L_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Part (a) is clear, because by Proposition
3.4.5
we have
[G_{\mathcal{O}}/K_{\lambda}]_{\mathrm{red}}\cong(\Gr_{\lambda})_{\mathrm{red}}

and
\mathcal{D}
takes topological equivalences to equivalences of
\infty
-categories.
Let us prove (b).
The obvious map
p_{\lambda}:\Spec(k)\rightarrow\mathbb{B}K_{\lambda}
is a morphism of lft prestacks, see Remark
5.2.4
.
The functor
(p_{\lambda})^{!}
is continuous by construction.
It admits a left adjoint
(p_{\lambda})_{!}
by Proposition
5.2.5
, which is also continuous.
The functor
(p_{\lambda})^{!}
is conservative, having sections locally for the étale topology.
By Barr-Beck-Lurie
[
61
, Thm. 4.7.3.5]
, we have an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}K_{\lambda})\cong(p_{\lambda})^{!}(p_{\lambda})_{!}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}\text{-}\text{mod}\cong H_{*}(K_{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\text{-}\text{mod}.

The last equality follows from the base change formula (
5.2.11
), applied to the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{K_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\Spec(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{\lambda}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{B}K_{\lambda}}

The loop action contracts
K_{\lambda}
to
P_{\lambda}^{-}
.
So Lemma
5.4.2
yields an isomorphism

H_{*}(K_{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\cong H_{*}(P_{\lambda}^{-},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}).

By Proposition
3.4.3
the group
P_{\lambda}^{-}
is a parahoric of
G
.
Let
L_{\lambda}
be the Levi factor.
Since
G
is of affine type, the group
L_{\lambda}
is reductive.
By §
2.3.2
, we have

P_{\lambda}^{-}\cong L_{\lambda}\ltimes U_{\lambda}^{-}

where
U_{\lambda}^{-}
is the unipotent radical.
In particular, the cocharacter
\lambda
contracts
P_{\lambda}^{-}
to
L_{\lambda}
and Lemma
5.4.2
yields an isomorphism

H_{*}(K_{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\cong H_{*}(L_{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}).

Applying Barr-Beck-Lurie, we obtain an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}K_{\lambda})\cong\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda}).

∎

Remark 5.4.4
.

Using some ongoing work of Y. Varshavsky, it should be possible to construct a
t
-structure
for any simply connected KM group, using an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}G)\cong\lim_{P}\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}P),

where
P
runs over all fp-parabolics.

Since
\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda}
is a smooth ft Artin stack, this proposition yields a
!
-shifted
t
-structure
on
\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\lambda})

such that the dualizing sheaf
\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}
is perverse.
A sheaf
\mathcal{E}
is perverse for this
!
-shifted
t
-structure if
\mathcal{E}[-\dim(\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda})]
is perverse for the usual
t
-structure.
To get the right
t
-structure
\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda})
,
we need an extra shift
[-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle]
over each strata,
whose justification will appear when we prove the
t
-exactness of the constant term functor.
On
\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda})
we now define a
t
-structure by gluing,
using Lemma
5.4.1
and (
5.3.1
), (
5.3.2
) with the perversity function

p_{\nu}:\{\eta\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}\,;\,\eta\leqslant\lambda\}\to\mathbb{Z},\quad\eta\mapsto 2\langle\rho,\lambda-\eta\rangle.

We get

\displaystyle\begin{split}K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda})&\Longleftrightarrow(i_{\eta})^{*}K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq-2\langle\rho,\lambda-\eta\rangle}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\eta})\,,\,\forall\eta\leq\lambda,\\
K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda})&\Longleftrightarrow(i_{\eta})^{!}K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq-2\langle\rho,\lambda-\eta\rangle}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\eta})\,,\,\forall\eta\leq\lambda.\end{split}

Thus, we can consider the object
\IC_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda})^{\heartsuit}
given by

\IC_{\lambda}=(i_{\lambda})_{!*}\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}[-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle].

Note that Lurie’s adjunction yields

\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})\cong\lim_{\lambda}\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{\lambda}\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\leq\lambda}).

Closed immersions are
t
-exact.
The proof is as in
[
10
, Lem. 6.4.11]
.
Hence, we get a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})
.

Proposition 5.4.5
.

Assume that
G
is of affine type.

\mathrm{(a)}

The category
\mathstrut{}^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})
is generated by the sheaves

(i_{\lambda})_{!}\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}[m-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle]
with
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The category
\mathstrut{}^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})
is generated by the sheaves

(i_{\lambda})_{*}\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}[-m-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle]
with
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

First, note that

(i_{\lambda})_{!}\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}[-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle]\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})

because closed immersions are t-exact.
Let
K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})
be a complex such that

\Hom((i_{\lambda})_{!}\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}[m-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle]\,,\,K)=0,\quad m\in\mathbb{N}.

We must prove that
K\cong 0
.
By Lemma
5.4.1
, the stratification

(\Gr_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{+}}
is constructible.
Since
\Gr_{G_{c}}
satisfies gluing, an induction using the fiber sequence in
[
10
, Lem. 5.4.1]

implies that it is enough to check that for each
\lambda
we have
(i_{\lambda})^{!}K=0
.
Thus we are reduced to the case of a single strata and
K\in{}^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}
.
By Proposition
5.4.3
, we have an equivalence

\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\lambda})\cong\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda})
given by

!
-pullback, where
L_{\lambda}
is a connected reductive group.
Up to a shift by
[\left\langle 2\rho,\lambda\right\rangle]
, we can suppose that the
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{\lambda})

is the pullback of the
!
-
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda})
.
The map
\pi:\Spec(k)\rightarrow\mathbb{B}L_{\lambda}
is smooth and
\pi^{!}
is
t
-exact.
Thus by conservativity, it is sufficient to get the claim for
\pi^{!}K
, where it is clear.
The assertion follows.

If
K\in\mathstrut^{p}\mathcal{D}^{\geq 0}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})
is orthogonal to all

(i_{\lambda})_{*}\omega_{\Gr_{\lambda}}[-m-2\langle\rho,\lambda\rangle]
, it is enough to check that for every

\lambda
we have
(i_{\lambda})^{*}K=0
using the fiber sequence in
[
10
, Lem. 5.5.3]
.
We conclude in a similar way.
∎

Since the group
L_{\lambda}
is connected for each dominant cocharacter
\lambda
,
Lemma
5.3.1
and Proposition
5.4.3
yield the following.

Corollary 5.4.6
.

Assume that
G
is of affine type.
The set of isomorphism classes of simple objects in
\mathcal{D}_{G_{\mathcal{O}}}(\Gr_{G_{c}})^{\heartsuit}
is
\{\IC_{\lambda}\,;\,\lambda\in{\check{\Lambda}}_{+}\}
.
∎

6.
The hyperbolic localization

In this section we gather some material on hyperbolic localization on stacks.
We will apply it in §
7
to the affine Grassmannian to prove
Theorem
7.1.1

6.1.
Interpolating family

6.1.0.
Attractors and repellers

Given two
\infty
-prestacks
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Z}
over
k
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-actions, let

\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z})

be the functor of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant morphisms.
We define the functors

(6.1.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{+}=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathcal{Z}),\quad\mathcal{Z}^{-}=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{A}_{-}^{1},\mathcal{Z}),\quad\mathcal{Z}^{0}=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\Spec(k),\mathcal{Z}),

where
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is equipped with the standard
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action and
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}
the opposite
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.
The functor
\mathcal{Z}^{+}
is called the attractor,
\mathcal{Z}^{-}
the repeller and
\mathcal{Z}^{0}
the fixed point locus.
We say that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action contracts
\mathcal{Z}
to
\mathcal{Z}^{0}
if
\mathcal{Z}^{+}=\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{-}=\mathcal{Z}^{0}
.
We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{+}}
\scriptstyle{p^{+}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{-}}
\scriptstyle{i^{+}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{-}}
\scriptstyle{p^{-}}

The map
q^{\pm}
is the evaluation at 0,
the map
p^{\pm}
is the functoriality with respect to the inclusion
\mathbb{G}_{m}\subset\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
and
i^{\pm}
is the functoriality with respect to the structural map

\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\Spec(k)
or
\mathbb{A}_{-}^{1}\rightarrow\Spec(k)
.
Recall that a prestack is separated if the diagonal is closed.

Lemma 6.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}
be a separated prestack.
The maps
p^{\pm}:\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
are monomorphisms.

Proof.

Given a test scheme
Y
and two maps
a,b:Y\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
,
the locus
Z\subset Y
where
a|_{Z}=b|_{Z}
is
\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}}Y.

Since
\mathcal{Z}
is separated, the functor
Z
is closed in
Y
.
The map
p^{+}
is identified with the restriction map

\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathcal{Z})\rightarrow\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{Z}),

and similarly for
p^{-}
.
Since
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
is schematically dense in
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
for any ring
R
, a point
\phi\in\mathcal{Z}^{+}(R)
is completely determined by its restriction.
∎

A morphism
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
of prestacks admits a relative deformation theory if Definition
B.3.1
holds.

Proposition 6.1.2
.

Assume that
\mathcal{Z}
is formally smooth with a relative deformation theory.
Then
\mathcal{Z}^{0}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{+}
are formally smooth.

Proof.

Let us prove that
\mathcal{Z}^{+}
is formally smooth, formal smoothness of
\mathcal{Z}^{0}
is analog.
Let
I\subset R
be a square zero ideal
\bar{R}=R/I
.
Let
\bar{f}:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\bar{R}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant morphism.
By formal smoothness of
\mathcal{Z}
, we lift it non-equivariantly as
f:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
.
Let
L_{\mathcal{Z}/k}
be the pro-cotangent complex.
By Proposition
B.4.1
, the obstruction to the existence of a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map sits in

H^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m},M)
with

M=\Map(\bar{f}^{*}(L_{\mathcal{Z}/k}),\mathcal{J}),

where
\mathcal{J}
the sheaf of ideals defining
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\bar{R}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
.
Since
M
is a quasi-coherent sheaf, this
H^{1}
is zero because
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is diagonalizable.
∎

6.1.2.
The tilde functor

Let
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{A}^{2}
with the hyperbolic
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by

\lambda\cdot(x,y)=(\lambda x,\lambda^{-1}y).

We view
\mathbb{X}
as a scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, via the multiplication map

m:\mathbb{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1},\quad(x,y)\mapsto xy.

For any
\infty
-prestack
\mathcal{Z}
over
k
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action we
define
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}
to be the
\infty
-prestack over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
such that

(6.1.2)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S,\tilde{\mathcal{Z}})=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{X}_{S},\mathcal{Z}),\quad\mathbb{X}_{S}:=\mathbb{X}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}S

for any affine scheme
S
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
The
\infty
-groupoid

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}(S)
consists of all
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant maps

\mathbb{X}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}.

Let
str
be the structure map
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
For each section
\sigma:\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{X}
there is a morphism
\sigma^{*}:\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
.
Let

\pi^{-}=(\sigma_{1})^{*},\quad\pi^{+}=(\sigma_{2})^{*}

where
\sigma_{1}(t)=(t,1)
and
\sigma_{2}(t)=(1,t)
.
We define the map

(6.1.3)

\tilde{p}=(\pi^{-},\pi^{+},str):\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

The action of
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
on
\mathbb{X}
given by

(\lambda,\mu)\cdot(x,y)=(\lambda x,\mu y),\quad(x,y)\in\mathbb{X},\quad\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{G}_{m},

yields an action of
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
on
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
.
The map
\tilde{p}
is equivariant for the action of

(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
on
\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
given by

(6.1.4)

(\lambda,\mu)\cdot(x,y,t)=(\lambda x,\mu y,(\lambda\mu)^{-1}t).

In the non-affine case, the map
\tilde{p}
may not be closed, but we have the following.

Lemma 6.1.3
.

\mathrm{(a)}

If
\mathcal{Z}
is an ind-ft ind-affine ind-scheme, then
\tilde{p}
is a closed immersion.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\mathcal{Z}
is a separated prestack, then
\tilde{p}
is a monomorphism.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{Z}
is an affine scheme of ft, then, by
[
28
, Prop. 2.3.7]
,
the stack
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
is representable by an affine scheme of ft and
\tilde{p}
is a closed immersion.
The functor
\mathcal{Z}\mapsto\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
commutes with filtered colimits.
So if
\mathcal{Z}
is an ind-ft ind-affine ind-scheme, then the map
\tilde{p}
is again a closed immersion, proving (a).
To prove (b) set
\mathbb{X}^{\circ}=\mathbb{X}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
.
For a map
S\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
, the corresponding

\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
\mathbb{X}_{S}^{\circ}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is completely determined by the composition

S\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{p}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}.

Now, the scheme
\mathbb{X}^{\circ}_{S}
is schematically dense in
\mathbb{X}_{S}
.
Since
\mathcal{Z}
is separated, we conclude as in Lemma
6.1.1
.
∎

6.1.3.
Some fiber products

For any prestack
\mathcal{Z}
we form the fiber product
\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
using
\pi^{\pm}
.
We want to construct two maps

(6.1.5)

r^{\pm}:\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}.

To do this, following Drinfeld
[
26
, Def. 3.1.8]
, we define two schemes
\mathbb{X}^{\pm}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}

with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.
Let

(6.1.6)

\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{X},\quad(t,\lambda)\mapsto(t\lambda,\lambda^{-1}),

(6.1.7)

\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{X},\quad(t,\lambda)\mapsto(\lambda,t\lambda^{-1}).

Both maps are open embeddings. Let
\mathbb{X}^{+}
be the pushout of the diagram of open embeddings

\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\hookleftarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{X},

where the last arrow is (
6.1.6
), and
let
\mathbb{X}^{-}
be the pushout of the diagram

\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}^{1}\hookleftarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{X},

where the last arrow is (
6.1.7
).
Both diagrams hold in the category of schemes over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.
For
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}
the structural map is the first projection.
So
\mathbb{X}^{\pm}
are also schemes over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.
By
[
26
, (3.5)-(3.6)]
we have

(6.1.8)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S,\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}})\cong\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{X}_{S}^{\pm},\mathcal{Z}),\quad\mathbb{X}_{S}^{\pm}:=\mathbb{X}^{\pm}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}S.

In addition, there are
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant morphisms over
\mathbb{A}^{1}

\sigma^{+}:\mathbb{X}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\quad\sigma^{-}:\mathbb{X}^{-}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{-}.

The first one is the blow-up at
(0,0)
and the second one the blow-up at
(0,\infty)
.
See
[
26
, Lem. 3.1.10]
for details.
Thus for any test scheme
S\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
we have morphisms

\sigma_{S}^{\pm}:\mathbb{X}_{S}^{\pm}\rightarrow S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\pm}

where
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{+}=\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Since

\Hom_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S,\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\pm},\mathcal{Z}),

pulling back the isomorphisms (
6.1.8
) by
\sigma_{S}^{\pm}
yields the maps

(\sigma_{S}^{\pm})^{*}:\Hom_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S,\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\rightarrow\Hom_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S,\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}})

from which we obtain the maps
r^{\pm}
in (
6.1.5
).
By
[
26
, Prop. 3.1.3]
, these maps are open embeddings if
\mathcal{Z}
is a ft algebraic space over
k
.

6.1.3.
Interpolating family over
\mathbb{A}^{1}

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{t}
be the fiber over
t\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
By
[
30
, Prop. 2.2.6]
, the map
\tilde{p}
in (
6.1.3
) is an isomorphism from

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathbb{G}_{m}

to the graph
\Gamma
of the action map
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
.
The fiber over 0 is

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathcal{Z})

where

\mathbb{X}_{0}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{A}^{2}\,;\,xy=0\}=\mathbb{X}_{0}^{+}\cup\mathbb{X}_{0}^{-}

with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant isomorphisms

\mathbb{A}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{X}^{+}_{0},\,t\mapsto(t,0),\quad\mathbb{A}_{-}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{X}^{-}_{0},\,t\mapsto(0,t^{-1}).

In particular, we have a morphism

(6.1.9)

\displaystyle\iota:\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{-}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{+}.

It is an isomorphism if
\mathcal{Z}
is a scheme by
[
28
, Prop. 2.2.9]
.
Since the tilde-functor preserves closed immersions by
[
29
, Prop. 2.3.2]
,
the map
\iota
is also an isomorphism if
\mathcal{Z}
is an ind-scheme with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation.
In general, we have the following.

Proposition 6.1.4
.

For any separated prestack
\mathcal{Z}
the map
\iota
is a monomorphism.

Proof.

By construction, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{p}_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{-}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{-}\times\mathcal{Z}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

By Lemma
6.1.3
, the map
\tilde{p}_{0}
is a monomorphism.
Thus
\iota
is also a monomorphism.
∎

Lemma 6.1.5
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Z}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{2}
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant ind-schematic formally smooth morphism of prestacks.

\mathrm{(a)}

The following induced morphisms are formally smooth

\tilde{f}:\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},\quad f^{+}:(\mathcal{Z}_{1})^{\pm}\rightarrow(\mathcal{Z}_{2})^{\pm},\quad f^{0}:(\mathcal{Z}_{1})^{0}\rightarrow(\mathcal{Z}_{2})^{0}.

\mathrm{(b)}

If
\mathcal{Z}_{2}=\Spec(k)
, then the map
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
is formally smooth.

Proof.

Let
I\subset R
an ideal such that
I^{2}=0
.
Set
\bar{R}=R/I
.
We abbreviate
\mathbb{X}_{R}=\mathbb{X}_{\Spec(R)}
and
\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}=\mathbb{X}_{\Spec(\bar{R})}
.
Consider a diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\phi}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{f}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{X}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}

By formal smoothness of
f
, we can lift
\phi
to a map
\tilde{\phi}:\mathbb{X}_{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{1}
such that
\tilde{\phi}=\bar{\phi}
modulo
I
.
By Proposition
B.4.1
, the obstruction to lift
\phi
in a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant way lies in
H^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m},M)
,
where

M=\Map_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{X}_{\bar{R}}}}(\bar{\phi}^{*}L_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}/\mathcal{Z}_{2}},\mathcal{I})

and
L_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}/\mathcal{Z}_{2}}
is the relative cotangent complex.
Since
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is diagonalizable,
the group
H^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{m},M)
vanishes
[
23
, 3.7]
.
Thus, the map
\tilde{f}:\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
is formally smooth by (
6.1.2
).
Note that the morphism
f
has relative deformation theory because it is ind-schematic, see Remark
B.3.2
.
∎

6.2.
The specialization map

Given a lft prestack
\mathcal{Y}
, let

\pi:\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y},\quad i_{0},i_{1}:\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

be the projection and the fp closed embeddings associated with the inclusions
\{0\},\{1\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Let

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})

be the full subcategory generated by the essential image of the
!
-pullback
by the obvious map

\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathcal{Y}\times[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}].

An object of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}

is called a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic complex.
For any
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic complex
K
on
\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
we abbreviate

K_{0}=(i_{0})^{!}K,\quad K_{1}=(i_{1})^{!}K.

We will need the following lemma in §
7.5.1
.

Lemma 6.2.1
.

For any lft prestack
\mathcal{Y}
and any
K\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathcal{Y})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
there is a functorial specialization map

{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K}:K_{1}\rightarrow K_{0}.

Proof.

First, assume that
\mathcal{Y}=Y
is a
k
-scheme of ft.
The natural transformation

(6.2.1)

(i_{0})^{!}\rightarrow(i_{0})^{!}\pi^{!}\pi_{!}\cong\pi_{!}

is an equivalence by
[
28
, §4.1]
.
We define
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K}
to be the obvious map

K_{1}\cong\pi_{!}(i_{1})_{!}(i_{1})^{!}K\to\pi_{!}K\cong K_{0}.

This map is functorial in the following way.
Given a morphism of
k
-schemes of ft
f:Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
, we set
K^{\prime}=(f\times\id_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})^{!}K
.
Then, we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{K_{1}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{0}}
\textstyle{f^{!}K_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{f^{!}{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K}}
\textstyle{f^{!}K_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

Now, for any lft prestack
\mathcal{Y}
we define
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K}

to be the projective system
({\mathrm{Sp}}_{f^{!}K})
of its
!
-restriction to affine ft-schemes
f:Y\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
.
∎

If
K=L\boxtimes\omega_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
with
L\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y})
,
then the specialization
{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K}
is the identity morphism

(6.2.2)

K_{1}\cong L\cong K_{0}.

For any homotopically ind-schematic morphism
f:\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Y}
of lft prestacks and any

K^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
we write
K=(f\times\id_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})_{\bullet}K^{\prime}
.
Then, we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{K_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K}}
\textstyle{K_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{f_{\bullet}K^{\prime}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\bullet}{\mathrm{Sp}}_{K^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{f_{\bullet}K^{\prime}_{0}}

6.3.
The hyperbolic diagram for the affine Grassmannian

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field.
Recall that
G
is a minimal simply connected KM group over
k
.
We assume that the characteristic of
k
is zero, or that it is arbitrary if
G
is an affine group in order to use Theorem
2.4.6
.
Applying the previous construction with
\mathcal{Z}=G

and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by the adjoint action of
2{\check{\rho}}
, from (
6.1.1
) we get

\mathcal{Z}^{+}=B,\quad\mathcal{Z}^{0}=T,\quad\mathcal{Z}^{-}=B^{-}.

Next, we apply (
6.1.1
) with
\mathcal{Z}=\Gr_{G}

and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by the adjoint action of
2{\check{\rho}}
.

Proposition 6.3.1
.

The following obvious inclusions are isomorphisms

\Gr_{B}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{+},\quad\Gr_{T}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{0},\quad\Gr_{B^{-}}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{-}

Proof.

The ind-scheme
G_{\mathcal{O}}
is formally smooth by Lemma
2.1.7
and Theorem
2.4.6
.
Thus, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
G_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is formally smooth by Lemma
5.2.13
.
By Lemma
6.1.5
it yields formally smooth maps

(G_{K})^{+}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{+},\quad(G_{K})^{0}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{0}.

Since
G_{K}
is ind-affine, we have

(6.3.1)

\displaystyle(G_{K})^{+}=B_{K},\quad(G_{K})^{0}=T_{K}.

Hence, the formally smooth maps

B_{K}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{+},\quad T_{K}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{0},

factor through
\Gr_{B}
and
\Gr_{T}
.
Since
B_{K}\to\Gr_{B}
and
T_{K}\to\Gr_{T}
are étale torsors with groups
T_{\mathcal{O}}
and
B_{\mathcal{O}}
,
there are sections étale locally, see §
2.1.0
.
Thus, by Proposition
2.1.15
, the morphisms

\Gr_{B}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{+},\quad\Gr_{T}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{0}

are formally smooth.
We also have a bijection on
k
-points.

By Theorem
3.2.8
for each
\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
the map
\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is fp locally closed.
Thus, since
(\Gr_{G})^{+}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is a monomorphism,
the morphism
\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{+}
is fp locally closed and formally smooth, hence it is a qc open immersion.
Thus the morphism
\Gr_{B}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{+}
is a qc open immersion which is a bijection on
k
-points.
Hence it is an isomorphism.

Since
\Gr_{T}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}
is fp closed by Proposition
3.1.1
,
for each
\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}
the composed map

\Gr_{T}^{\nu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{B}^{\nu}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{G}

is fp locally closed by Theorem
3.2.8
again.
So the morphism
\Gr_{T}^{\nu}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{0}
is a qc open immersion,
and
\Gr_{T}\rightarrow(\Gr_{G})^{0}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 6.3.2
.

Let
j:\Gr_{T}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}
be the diagonal embedding.

\mathrm{(a)}

The map
j
is an fp closed and open immersion. We have

\Gr_{T}\cong\bigsqcup_{\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}}\Gr_{B}^{\nu}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}^{\nu}=\Gr_{T}\times_{(\Gr_{T})^{2}}(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}).

\mathrm{(b)}

We have the following Cartesian diagram, where
\Delta
is the diagonal,

\textstyle{\Gr_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gr_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}}

Proof.

By Proposition
3.1.1
the maps
\Gr_{T}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}
and
\Gr_{T}\rightarrow\Gr_{B^{-}}
are fp closed.
Thus, since the map

\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}\times\Gr_{B^{-}}

is a monomorphism, the map
j
is fp closed.
It is enough to check that
j
is formally smooth.
As
j
is equivariant for left multiplication on the left hand side and diagonal action on the right hand side,
it is enough to prove formal smoothness at
1
.
Since
\Gr_{G}
is a sheaf, by Proposition
6.3.1
, we have

\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\Gr_{G})=\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}.

Since
G_{K}
is ind-affine, by (
6.3.1
) we have

\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},G_{K})=B_{K}\times_{G_{K}}B_{K}^{-}=T_{K}

The obvious map
G_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, hence yields a map

\phi:\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},G_{K})\rightarrow\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\Gr_{G})

which factors as

T_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\rightarrow}}\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}.

Since
T_{K}\to\Gr_{T}
is an étale
T_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsor, there are sections étale locally, see §
2.1.0
.
Thus, by Proposition
2.1.15
, it is enough to prove that
\phi
is formally smooth to prove (a).
We first prove the formal smoothness of the map

\phi^{\prime}:\Hom(\mathbb{P}^{1},G_{K})\rightarrow\Hom(\mathbb{P}^{1},\Gr_{G}).

Let
R
be a ring and
I\subset R
a square zero ideal.
Let
y:\mathbb{P}_{R}^{1}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
, such that the map
\bar{y}=y\mod I
lifts as
\tilde{y}:\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}\rightarrow G_{K}
.
We form the pullback

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y}
\textstyle{\Gr_{G}}

It yields a
G_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsor
E
over
\mathbb{P}_{R}^{1}

for the étale topology, representable by an ind-affine ind-scheme with a section
\bar{\sigma}
modulo
I
.
The ind-scheme
G_{\mathcal{O}}
is formally smooth by Lemma
2.1.7
and Theorem
2.4.6
.
Hence
\bar{\sigma}
lifts Zariski locally on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}
and by writing
E\cong\operatornamewithlimits{colim}E_{a}
and
applying deformation theory for enoughly large
E_{a}
the obstruction to lift
\bar{\sigma}
globally sits in the cohomology group

H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I},\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\Map(\bar{x}^{*}(\Omega^{1}_{E_{a}/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}}),I))\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I},\bar{x}^{*}(T_{E/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}})\otimes I),

where the last equality follows from formal smoothness and
[
5
, Prop. 7.11.8]
.
Since
E|_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}}
is trivial, we have

\bar{x}^{*}(T_{E/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}})=\mathfrak{g}[s]\otimes_{R/I}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}}.

Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R/I}})=0
, the obstruction vanishes.
Using Proposition
B.4.1
similarly to Proposition
6.1.2
,
we can choose this lift to be
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, because
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is diagonalizable.
In particular, the map
\phi^{\prime}
is formally smooth.
We have a decomposition into open and closed subfunctors

\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}=\bigsqcup_{(\nu,\mu)\in({\check{\Lambda}})^{2}}\Gr^{\nu}_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}^{\mu},

obtained by pullback through the map

\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}\rightarrow\Gr_{T}\times\Gr_{T}.

By Proposition
3.2.11
, the map
j
identifies
\Gr_{T}
with the component labelled by the diagonal pairs

(\nu,\nu)
.

To prove the claim (b),
taking only the connected components indexed by
(\nu,\nu)
with
\nu\in X_{*}(T)

yields an open closed immersion

\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{B^{-}}\hookrightarrow\Gr_{B}\times\Gr_{B^{-}}.

We also have

\Gr_{G}\times_{(\Gr_{G})^{2}}(\Gr_{B}\times\Gr_{B^{-}})=\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}.

Further, if we restrict the map

\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}\rightarrow(\Gr_{T})^{2}

to the diagonal
\Gr_{T}\hookrightarrow(\Gr_{T})^{2}
,
we get an isomorphism

(6.3.2)

\displaystyle\Gr_{G}\times_{(\Gr_{G})^{2}}(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{B^{-}})\cong\Gr_{T}\times_{(\Gr_{T})^{2}}(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}})

and (b) follows from (a).
∎

6.4.
Interpolating family of groups

We keep the same notation as in §
6.3
.
We first set
\mathcal{Z}=G
.
The ind-ft ind-scheme
\tilde{G}
is closed in
G\times G\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
by Lemma
6.1.3
.
Let
\Gamma
be
the graph of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
G
.
Since
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
is functorial in
\mathcal{Z}
, the ind-scheme
\tilde{G}
is a group ind-scheme such that

(6.4.1)

\displaystyle\tilde{G}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\cong\Gamma\cong G\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\quad\tilde{G}_{0}\cong B\times_{T}B^{-}.

Let
\overline{\Gamma}
be the closure of
\Gamma
in
\tilde{G}
.

Lemma 6.4.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The closure
\overline{\Gamma}
is isomorphic to
\tilde{G}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The ind-scheme
\tilde{G}
is ind-flat over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

Proof.

By
[
28
, Prop. 2.5.5]
for a
k
-smooth affine scheme
Z
, we have the same description for
\tilde{Z}
.
The ind-scheme
G
is formally smooth, but it may not be ind-smooth.
So we can not aply loc. cit. to
G
.
Consider the open Bruhat cell
S=U^{-}\times T\times U
.
By
[
28
, Prop. 2.3.2]
, since the functor
\mathcal{Z}\mapsto\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
commutes with filtered colimits,
we have that
\tilde{S}
is open in
\tilde{G}
.
Since the functor
\mathcal{Z}\mapsto\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
commutes also with finite products and filtered colimits,
by
[
28
, Prop. 2.4.4, 2.4.6]
we have

\tilde{S}=\tilde{U}^{-}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\tilde{T}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\tilde{U}=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S.

In particular
\tilde{S}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
is dense in
\tilde{S}
.
Thus it is enough to prove that
\tilde{S}
is schematically dense in
\tilde{G}
.
Fix a presentation
G=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}X_{a}
as an ind-ft scheme.
We have
S=\operatornamewithlimits{colim}S_{a}
,
and there is an open immersion
\tilde{S}_{a}=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S_{a}\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}_{a}

that is schematically dense over each fiber over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. By
[
42
, Thm. 11.10.9]
,
we deduce that
\tilde{S}_{a}
is schematically dense in
\tilde{X}_{a}
.
Thus
\tilde{S}
is schematically dense in
\tilde{G}
.
Finally, over
\mathbb{G}_{m}
the ind-scheme
\Gamma
is ind-flat.
Flatness over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is equivalent to torsion freeness
and the schematic closure of a flat scheme over
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is flat over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
∎

One of the main input is the following proposition.

Proposition 6.4.2
.

The quotient stack
[(G\times G\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\tilde{G}]
is
G\times G
-equivariantly open
in an ind-affine ind-scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
∎

The proof will be given in §
A.2
.

Corollary 6.4.3
.

The map
\lambda:\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
is an fp immersion.

Proof.

By Propositions
3.1.1
and
6.4.2
, the map
\lambda
is locally closed.
By Proposition
6.1.3
, after a base change by the map

G_{K}\times G_{K}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1},

the fiber product is an immersion in an ind-ft scheme.
Thus
\lambda
is an fp immersion by Lemma
2.1.4
.
∎

Now, we apply (
6.1.1
) with
\mathcal{Z}=\Gr_{G}
.
We want to compare
\widetilde{\Gr}_{G}
with
\Gr_{\tilde{G}}
.
We first define a map

\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Gr}_{G}.

Since the assignment
\mathcal{Z}\mapsto\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
is functorial by §
6.1.2
, we have a map

\widetilde{G_{K}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Gr}_{G}.

Further, we have
\widetilde{G_{K}}\cong(\tilde{G})_{K}
because

\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{X},G_{K})=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{X}[t,t^{-1}],G)=\Hom(\Spec(k[t,t^{-1}]),\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{X},G)).

This isomorphism is
(\tilde{G})_{\mathcal{O}}=\widetilde{G_{\mathcal{O}}}
-equivariant.
Thus it factors to the quotient and we get a morphism

(6.4.2)

\eta:\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Gr}_{G}.

Proposition 6.4.4
.

The map (
6.4.2
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since the stack
\Gr_{G}
is separated, Lemma
6.1.3
implies that the map

\widetilde{\Gr}_{G}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

is a monomorphism.
So Corollary
6.4.3
implies that
\eta
is fp locally closed.
Moreover, it is bijective on
k
-points.
Above a point
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
this is obvious.
Above the point
t=0
we have an isomorphism

(6.4.3)

\Gr_{\tilde{G}_{0}}=\Gr_{B\times_{T}B^{-}}\cong\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{B^{-}}

which follows from the isomorphism

\mathbb{B}(B\times_{T}B^{-})\cong B\backslash T/B^{-}\cong\mathbb{B}B\times_{\mathbb{B}T}\mathbb{B}B^{-}

(note that the isomorphism

\mathbb{B}(G_{1}\times_{G_{2}}G_{3})\cong\mathbb{B}G_{1}\times_{\mathbb{B}G_{2}}\mathbb{B}G_{3}

does not hold in general, as can be seen from the following example:

G_{1}=B,

G_{3}=B^{-}
and
G_{2}=G
).
Thus (
6.4.3
) identifies
\eta_{0}
with a map

\Gr_{\tilde{G}_{0}}=\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{B^{-}}\rightarrow(\widetilde{\Gr}_{G})_{0}.

By Proposition
6.1.4
, we also have a monomorphism

(6.4.4)

(\widetilde{\Gr}_{G})_{0}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{B^{-}}.

Thus the fiber
\eta_{0}
of
\eta
is an isomorphism.
Now, to prove that
\eta
is an isomorphism, it suffices to prove that it is formally smooth.
As
\tilde{G}_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{\tilde{G}}
has sections étale locally and is ind-fp, by Proposition
2.1.15
,
it suffices to prove that the map

\tilde{G}_{K}\rightarrow\widetilde{\Gr_{G}}

is formally smooth.
Since
\tilde{G}_{K}=\widetilde{G_{K}}
, this follows from Lemma
6.1.5
,
by applying the tilde functor to
G_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
that is already formally smooth.
∎

By (
6.1.5
), for any prestack
\mathcal{Z}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action we have morphisms

(6.4.5)

r^{\pm}:\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm}

where
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm}
is the fiber product relative to the map
\pi^{\pm}
in (
6.1.3
) given by

(6.4.6)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm}=\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\times_{\mathcal{Z}}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}

Set
\mathcal{Z}=\Gr_{G}
with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by
2{\check{\rho}}
.
Propositions
6.3.1
and
6.4.4
yield

(6.4.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{+}=\Gr_{B},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{-}=\Gr_{B^{-}},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}=\Gr_{\tilde{G}}.

So the morphisms
r^{\pm}
become

(6.4.8)

r^{+}:\Gr_{B}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{\tilde{G}},\quad r^{-}:\Gr_{B^{-}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\Gr_{B^{-}}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{\tilde{G}}

where the fiber product
\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{\tilde{G}}
is relative to the map
\pi^{+}
and
the fiber product
\Gr_{B^{-}}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{\tilde{G}}
is relative to the map
\pi^{-}
.

Proposition 6.4.5
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=\Gr_{G}
with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action coming from
2{\check{\rho}}
.
The maps
r^{\pm}
are qc open embeddings.

Proof.

Let us prove the assertion for
r^{+}
.
The case of
r^{-}
is similar.
First we claim that
r^{+}
is a monomorphism.
Consider the chain of maps

(6.4.9)

\Gr_{B}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r^{+}}}{{\rightarrow}}\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\tilde{p},\id)}}{{\rightarrow}}(\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B}=\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{B}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

where the fiber product
(\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B}
is relative to the second projection

\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\to\Gr_{G}
.
The composed map is the base change to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
of the composition of the diagonal of
\Gr_{B}

with the map
\Gr_{B}\times\Gr_{B}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{B}
which is the identity on the second factor.
So it is a monomorphism. So
r^{+}
is also a monomorphism.
Next, we decompose

\Gr_{B}=\bigsqcup_{\mu\in{\check{\Lambda}}}\Gr_{B}^{\mu}.

We claim that the map

r^{+,\mu}:\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B}^{\mu}.

is an fp immersion.
Indeed, if we use the same composition as in (
6.4.9
), we obtain a map

\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}.

By Theorem
3.2.8
, the map
\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
is an fp immersion, as well as the diagonal of
\Gr_{B}^{\mu}
.
By Corollary
6.4.3
, the morphism

\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
is an fp immersion.
We deduce that
r^{+,\mu}
is an fp immersion.
To conclude, it is enough to prove that
r^{+}
is formally smooth. Let first check formal smoothness on the fibers.
Over a point
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
this is clear.
Over the point
t=0
, using (
6.4.4
), we must prove that the map

r_{0}^{+}:\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{T}=\Gr_{B}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}(\Gr_{B^{-}}\times_{\Gr_{T}}\Gr_{B})=\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{T}}(\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}}),

is a qc open immersion.
This map is just the base change to
\Gr_{B}
of the map

\Gr_{T}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}\times_{\Gr_{G}}\Gr_{B^{-}},

which is fp closed and open by Lemma
6.3.2
.
Now, to deduce formal smoothness for
r^{+}
it is enough to check it after pulling back by the étale torsor

B_{K}^{\mu}\times G_{K}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}^{\mu}\times\Gr_{G}
, where
B_{K}^{\mu}
is the connected component of
B_{K}

containing
s^{\mu}
.
Then, we get that the pullback is an open immersion and we just apply Lemma
2.1.4
to go back to the map
r^{+}
.
After this pullback, we get an fp immersion between ind-ft ind-schemes over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
and a formally smooth source.
Thus by
Proposition
B.3.4
, it is enough to check formal smoothness on fibers over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. This follows from the above.
∎

7.
The constant term

In this section we introduce and study the constant term functor for the affine Grassmannian.
The main results are Theorems
7.1.1
and
7.6.1
, which are proved in §§
7.1
-
7.5.1

and §
7.6
respectively.

7.1.
The definition of the constant term functors

We consider the following diagram of
\infty
-stacks

(7.1.1)

135.23495pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 73.8161pt\raise-78.2797pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{q^{-}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.8161pt\raise-86.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 135.23495pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gr_{G}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 194.16705pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-93.99994pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 61.34035pt\raise-93.99994pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gr_{T}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

By Theorem
3.2.8
, the maps
p^{+}
,
p^{-}

are fp immersions when restricted to each connected component
\Gr_{B}^{\nu}
,
\Gr_{B^{-}}^{\nu}
of
\Gr_{B}
,
\Gr_{B^{-}}
.
The affine Grassmannian
\Gr_{G}
satisfies gluing by Remark
5.2.2
.
Hence, since
p^{\pm}
is a fp immersion, the functors
(p^{\pm})_{*}
,
(p^{\pm})^{*}
and
(p^{\pm})^{!}
are well-defined by §
5.2.0
.
By base change, the map
\tilde{p}^{\pm}
is also a fp immersion when restricted to the connected components of
\Gr_{B}
(resp.
\Gr_{B^{-}}
).
Hence the functors
(\tilde{p}^{\pm})_{*}
,
(\tilde{p}^{\pm})^{*}
and
(\tilde{p}^{\pm})^{!}
are also well-defined.
By Proposition
3.1.1
, since
B/T=U
and
B^{-}/T=U^{-}
are
ind-ft ind-affine ind-schemes,
the maps
i^{\pm}
are fp closed immersions.
Since
\Gr_{B}
satisfies gluing, the functors
(i^{\pm})_{*}
,
(i^{\pm})^{*}
and
(i^{\pm})^{!}
are well-defined by §
5.2.0
.
Using the bar-complexes for the affine Grassmannians, we prove that all prestacks are lft.
Hence the functors

(\tilde{p}^{\pm})_{!}
,
(p^{\pm})_{!}
,
(i^{\pm})_{!}
,
(q^{\pm})_{!}
are well-defined by Proposition
5.2.5
.
We define the constant term functors

(7.1.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\CT_{*}&=(i^{+})^{*}(p^{+})^{!}:\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{T}),\\
\CT_{!}^{-}&=(i^{-})^{!}(p^{-})^{*}:\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{T}).\end{split}

There is a morphism of functors

(7.1.3)

\CT_{*}\rightarrow\CT_{!}^{-},

which is defined as follows

\displaystyle(i^{+})^{*}(p^{+})^{!}

\displaystyle\rightarrow(i^{+})^{*}(p^{+})^{!}(p^{-})_{*}(p^{-})^{*}

\displaystyle\cong(i^{+})^{*}(\tilde{p}^{+})_{*}(\tilde{p}^{-})^{!}(p^{-})^{*}

\displaystyle\rightarrow(i^{+})^{*}(\tilde{p}^{+})_{*}j_{*}j^{*}(\tilde{p}^{-})^{!}(p^{-})^{*}

\displaystyle\cong(i^{+})^{*}(i^{+})_{*}(i^{-})^{!}(p^{-})^{*}

\displaystyle\cong(i^{-})^{!}(p^{-})^{*}

Here, we used the base change for the Cartesian square in (
7.1.1
),
the fact that
j
is closed and open by Lemma
6.3.2
, hence
j^{!}\cong j^{*}
,
and the full faithfulness of
(i^{+})_{*}
which follows from
[
10
, Lem. 5.4.1]
.

Let
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic objects in
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{G})
, i.e.,
the full subcategory strongly generated by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant complexes, i.e.,
the complexes given by a finite iteration of taking a cone of a map in
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{G})
.
We define
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{B})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
and
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{B^{-}})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
similarly.

Theorem 7.1.1
.

The morphism of functors
\CT_{*}\rightarrow\CT_{!}^{-}
in (
7.1.3
) is an equivalence on
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{G})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
.

To prove the theorem, we follow the strategy of
[
28
]
,
[
30
]
.

7.2.
The contraction principle

To prove Theorem
7.1.1
, we first need a contraction principle.
Since
\Gr_{B}=\bigsqcup_{\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}}\Gr_{B}^{\nu}
, the map
q^{+}
decomposes as

q^{+}=\bigsqcup q_{\nu}
with

q_{\nu}:\Gr_{B}^{\nu}\rightarrow\Spec(k).

Let
T_{K}^{0}
be the neutral component of
T_{K}
.
By Lemma
5.2.18
, the
U_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsor

U_{K}\cdot s^{\nu}\cdot T_{K}^{0}\rightarrow\Gr_{B}^{\nu}.

is contractive and the map
q^{+}
is homotopically ind-schematic.
Thus the functor
(q^{+})_{\bullet}
is well-defined. The discussion is the same for
q^{-}
.
Let us change our notation. Following (
6.4.7
) we now write

Z=\Gr_{G},\quad Z^{+}=\Gr_{B},\quad Z^{0}=\Gr_{T},\quad Z^{-}=\Gr_{B^{-}},\quad\tilde{Z}=\Gr_{\tilde{G}}.

Proposition 7.2.1
.

The following morphisms of functors are equivalences

\mathrm{(a)}

(i^{-})^{!}\rightarrow(i^{-})^{!}(q^{-})^{!}(q^{-})_{!}=(q^{-}i^{-})^{!}(q^{-})^{!}\rightarrow(q^{-})_{!}
on
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{B^{-}})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
,

\mathrm{(b)}

(q^{+})_{\bullet}\rightarrow(q^{+})_{\bullet}(i^{+})_{*}(i^{+})^{*}\cong(q^{+})_{\bullet}(i^{+})_{\bullet}(i^{+})^{*}\cong(i^{+})^{*}

on
\mathcal{D}(\Gr_{B})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}{\mathrm{mon}}}
.

Proof.

The first isomorphism in (b) follows from Lemma
5.2.17
, because
i^{+}
is ind-ft ind-schematic,
and the second one from Proposition
5.2.20
.
To prove (a) we abbreviate
i=i^{-}
and
q=q^{-}
.
Consider the commutative triangle

\textstyle{\overline{Z}^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\overline{q}}
\textstyle{Z^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\imath}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{Z^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}

with

\overline{Z}^{-}=\bigsqcup_{\nu\in{\check{\Lambda}}}U_{K}\cdot s^{\nu}\cdot T_{K}^{0}
.
Note that
\overline{Z}^{-}
is an ind-ft ind-scheme with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant presentation that contracts to
s^{\nu}
.
Consequently, using
[
28
, Prop. 3.2.2 (b)]
and a colimit argument,
we get that
\overline{i}^{!}\cong\overline{q}_{!}
when restricted to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic objects.
Thus, the equality
\overline{q}=q\psi
yields

i^{!}\cong\overline{\imath}^{!}\circ\psi^{!}\cong\overline{q}_{!}\circ\psi^{!}\cong q_{!}\circ\psi_{!}\circ\psi^{!}\cong q_{!},

where the last equality is Lemma
5.2.11
.
The proof of (b) is similar, applying the contraction principle upstairs to the pair of functors

(\overline{\imath}^{+})^{*}
and
(\overline{q}^{+})_{*}
and
[
28
, 3.2.2 (a)]
instead,
because
(q^{+})_{\bullet}=(\overline{q}^{+})_{*}\psi^{*}
.
∎

In particular, the constant term functors in (
7.1.2
), when restricted to
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic complexes,
are given by the following formulas

(7.2.1)

\displaystyle\CT_{*}=(q^{+})_{\bullet}(p^{+})^{!},\quad\CT_{!}=(q^{-})_{!}(p^{-})^{*}

The contraction principle in Proposition
7.2.1
and (
7.1.3
) give a morphism of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic complexes

(q^{+})_{\bullet}(p^{+})^{!}\cong(i^{+})^{*}(p^{+})^{!}\to(i^{-})^{!}(p^{-})^{*}\cong(q^{-})_{!}(p^{-})^{*}

The adjoint pair of functors
((q^{-})_{!}(p^{-})^{*},(p^{-})_{*}(q^{-})^{!})
yields a map

(7.2.2)

(q^{+})_{\bullet}(p^{+})^{!}(p^{-})_{*}(q^{-})^{!}\rightarrow\id_{\mathcal{D}(Z^{0})},

On the other hand, the left adjoint of
(p^{-})_{*}(q^{-})^{!}
is
(q^{-})_{!}(p^{-})^{*}
.
Thus, Theorem
7.1.1
follows from the following statement which yields an isomorphism of functors

(q^{-})_{!}(p^{-})^{*}\cong(q^{+})_{\bullet}(p^{+})^{!}
.

Theorem 7.2.2
.

The morphism (
7.2.2
) is the restriction to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-monodromic categories of
the co-unit of an adjunction for the pair of functors
((q^{+})_{\bullet}(p^{+})^{!},(p^{-})_{*}(q^{-})^{!})
.

To prove Theorem
7.2.2
, we need the unit of this adjunction.
The morphism (
7.2.2
) can be obtained from the morphism (
5.2.20
) obtained from to the diagram

\textstyle{Z^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Z^{0}}
\textstyle{Z^{+}\times_{Z}Z^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{-}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{+}}
\textstyle{Z^{0}}
\textstyle{Z^{0}\times Z^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here
\alpha_{\pm}
are the composed maps

Z^{+}\times_{Z}Z^{-}\rightarrow Z^{\pm}\rightarrow Z^{0}.

Using this, we construct this unit via the interpolation.

7.3.
Equivariant version

Recall that
Z=\Gr_{G}
.
As in
[
28
, §3.4]
, we consider the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant version.
Set

\mathcal{Z}^{0}=Z^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}=Z^{\pm}/\mathbb{G}_{m},\quad\mathcal{Z}=Z/\mathbb{G}_{m},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}=\tilde{Z}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

where the last quotient is relative to the antidiagonal embedding
\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
and (
6.1.4
).
We have the obvious morphisms

\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}:\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z},\quad\mathfrak{q}^{\pm}:\mathcal{Z}^{\pm}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{0}.

The morphism (
7.2.2
) lifts to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant categories.
This yields a morphism

(7.3.1)

(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}\rightarrow\id_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}^{0})}

Theorem
7.2.2
follows from the following as in
[
28
, Thm. 3.4.3]
.

Theorem 7.3.1
.

\mathrm{(a)}

The morphism (
7.3.1
) is the co-unit of an adjunction for the pair of functors

((\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}\,,\,(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!})
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The equivariant version implies Theorem
7.2.2
.

We now focus on the construction of the unit of the adjunction in this setting.

7.4.
Construction of the unit

By Proposition
6.4.4
and Corollary
6.4.3
we have an fp immersion

\lambda:\widetilde{\Gr_{G}}=\Gr_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}.

By (
6.1.4
), the map
\lambda
yields a map

\lambda:\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

We consider the sheaf
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
given by

\mathcal{Q}=\lambda_{*}\omega_{\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}}.

The sheaf
\mathcal{Q}
descends to an object from
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}))
.
This follows from the Cartesian diagram

(7.4.1)

where the
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2}
-action in
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
is as in §
6.1.2
.
Note that (
6.4.1
) and Proposition
6.4.4
yield isomorphisms

(\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},\tilde{p}_{1})\cong(\mathcal{Z},\Delta),\quad(\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{0},\tilde{p}_{0})\cong(\mathcal{Z}^{+}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{-},\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-}).

In particular, using the specialization in Lemma
6.2.1
, we get a specialization map

(7.4.2)

{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}:\mathcal{Q}_{1}=(i_{1})^{!}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=\Delta_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{Z}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{0}=(i_{0})^{!}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=(\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{Z}^{-}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{+}}.

For nice spaces, this map suffices to construct the unit of the adjunction using the formalism of kernels.
We cannot apply this formalism because it involves considering the functor
p_{*}
for the map

p:\Gr_{G}\times\Gr_{G}\rightarrow\Gr_{G}
which may not be defined.
Thus, we first restrict to a smaller substack where such functor is defined and then apply pushforward.
We must construct a map

(7.4.3)

\id_{\mathcal{D}(Z)}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}.

(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}
,

The maps
\mathfrak{q}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{q}^{-}
are homotopically ind-schematic by the discussion in §
7.2
.
The maps
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{-}

are fp immersions when restricted to each connected component
\Gr_{B}^{\nu}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\Gr_{B^{-}}^{\nu}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
see §
7.1
.
By base change and composition,
the map
\mathfrak{q}
is also homotopically ind-schematic.
In particular, we have

(7.4.5)

(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}=\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{p}^{!}.

From (
7.4.4
), we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{+}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}\mathfrak{q}^{-}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}}

with
p_{1}:\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
the first projection.
Consider
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z})
. Tensoring (
7.4.2
) by
\mathcal{M}
yields a map

(p_{1})^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{!}{\otimes}\Delta_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{Z}}\rightarrow(p_{1})^{!}\mathcal{M}\overset{!}{\otimes}\kappa_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{Z}^{-}\times_{\mathcal{Z}^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{+}}.

Since we quotiented by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the map
\Delta
is no more fp closed, but it is still affine fp and

\kappa=\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-}
is an fp immersion when restricted to its connected components.
Since
p_{1}\Delta=\id_{\mathcal{Z}}
, the projection formula for
\Delta
and
\kappa
yields a map

(7.4.6)

\Delta_{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\kappa_{*}\kappa^{!}p_{1}^{!}\mathcal{M},

So we get from (
7.4.6
) a map

(7.4.7)

\Delta_{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\kappa_{*}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\mathcal{M}.

Using Lemma
6.3.2
, the isomorphism (
6.3.2
)
and taking
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-quotients, we get a Cartesian square and a lower commutative triangle

(7.4.8)

Since
p
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor we get an isomorphism

p^{*}p_{*}\cong-\otimes\RGm(\mathbb{G}_{m},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})[2].

Thus the counit
p^{*}p_{*}\rightarrow\id
splits by the unit section
1\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Now (
7.4.7
) and (
7.4.8
) yield

p_{*}(\Delta_{1})_{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow p_{*}(\kappa_{1})_{*}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\mathcal{M}.

Using the splitting this gives a map

(\Delta_{1})_{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow(\kappa_{1})_{*}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\mathcal{M}.

Since
\Delta_{1}
is fp closed, using the adjunction and the base change along (
7.4.8
), this gives a map

j_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{p}^{!}\mathcal{M}.

Applying the functor
\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}
we get a map

(7.4.9)

\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}j_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\mathcal{M}.

Since
j
is fp schematic, Lemma
5.2.17
and Proposition
5.2.20
yield

(7.4.10)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}j_{*}=\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}j_{\bullet}=i_{\bullet}=i_{*}.

So from (
7.4.9
) and (
7.4.10
) we obtain a map

(7.4.11)

\mathcal{M}\rightarrow i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathfrak{q}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{p}^{!}\mathcal{M},

which is functorial in
\mathcal{M}
.
So we get the putative unit of the adjunction (
7.4.3
) as reformulated in (
7.4.5
).

7.5.
Proof of Theorem
7.3.1

We have defined the following morphisms of functors in (
7.3.1
) and (
7.4.3
)

(7.5.1)

\id_{\mathcal{D}(Z)}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!},

(7.5.2)

(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}\rightarrow\id_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Z}^{0})}.

To prove Theorem
7.3.1
, hence Theorems
7.2.2
and
7.1.1
,
it is enough to prove that the composed morphisms

(7.5.3)

(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!},

(7.5.4)

(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}

are isomorphic to the identity.
We prove the assertion for (
7.5.3
).
The proof for (
7.5.4
) is similar.
The proof follows the argument in
[
28
, §5]
.
It relies on Proposition
6.4.5
.

7.5.0.
The kernel for the composition

We abbreviate

\Phi=(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}(\mathfrak{q}^{+})_{\bullet}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{!}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}.

We consider the morphism of functors (
7.5.3
)

\Phi\rightarrow(\mathfrak{p}^{-})_{*}(\mathfrak{q}^{-})^{!}.

The functor
\Phi
is given by pull-push along the diagram

(7.5.5)
